so every comparison is per flip or within one mode.
**Result** (board energy on aifoundry3, 600 MHz, 12 of 12 passes, unless noted; `docs/reports/data/2026-10-21-dtf/reduce.txt`):
the reduction's 15 registered rows read 1 CONFIRMED, 1 CONFIRMED-ours, 4 REFUTED, 5 OPEN, 3 under-resolved and 1 not scored.
E60 reproduces through its own images in the new build (a read-port bit into `fxor.pi` 20.70 ± 0.13 fJ against 20.83). A
result bit costs 26.7 ± 0.3 fJ inside one kernel family and 29.67 ± 0.15 fJ from the registered joint fit, not the page's
44.4 fJ; the page's sum of an input and an output bit (50.9 fJ) holds at 50.7 fJ through `fnot.pi`, its 6.81 : 1 split does
not. A carry position costs 0.98 ± 0.80 fJ (3.48 ± 0.74 with the changed-word term removed), not 14.2 fJ (CONFIRMED-ours).
`fmadd.ps` has no zero gate on the vector unit (0.23 ± 0.16 pJ/op, not 8.94). The Miller patterns run monotone the wrong way
(−0.22 ± 0.06 W over four k_adj levels: REFUTED as a delivered design). A register-file cell flip costs 0.76 ± 0.32 fJ
(under-resolved). Changing the read register's index costs 369–452 fJ per index bit, 12–14 times the page's 32 fJ, with no
linear law across the three matched-rate rungs (under-resolved, not measurable as registered). Two-hart interleaving destroys
operand sharing (ρ = 1.117 ± 0.024 on aifoundry1 card 0). The vector unit retires one op per cycle per hart; the harness's
0.58 / 0.61 is its loop overhead, and which of the two a mode gets is set by the loop head's address mod 32 (mode 179 ran 0.5791
in E60's build and 0.6088 in this one). On aifoundry2's thermal camera, swapping which 16 shires run loud moves heat across the
board by 373 ± 14 mK (p = 0.0007, exploratory). Two pipelines written independently agree on 48 quantities within 1.6 SE.
1,437 of 1,616 runs kept; passes set aside by §8.9: aifoundry2 p4, aifoundry1 card 0 p6, p7, p10, p12.
**Report:** [Verifying the deterministic transistor-flip experiments](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-verifying-transistor-flips).


## E62 — hm: spatial heat movies, where on the die the compute was scheduled (Q121; card runs 2026-10-21 from 17:06 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q121):** "design a set of spatial heating experiments. I want to generate some videos of heat spreading in
various patterns depending on how the compute is scheduled. Come up with some interesting ideas, maybe like corners or
the center, and create a set of videos. Turn this into a set of videos and eventually produce a [spacesheep] page,
which has these videos. Use the live stream to make this possible."
**Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/hm/prereg/`](../../tools/claims-v3/hm/prereg/PROTOCOL.md) (version 2, after a
red-team pass): the die map re-derived from `placements.py`, ten blocks with their masks, launch counts and executed
lap orders, every prediction as a formula anchored on E61's measured +372.9 ± 13.6 mK, a σ-per-lap prior of 27 mK, the
analysis plan, the falsifiers that would say "regulators, not die", and the runner's safety plumbing; `protocol.json`
sha256 `c227da78…`, frozen 16:58 PDT, before the session's first launch at 17:09 (`validate.out` exit 0). E62 takes
up E61's deferral of the constant-total-power spatial dipole, by chained launches rather than an in-kernel switch. The
instrument is the Thermal Master P3 that has watched aifoundry2's card since 16 Oct, recorded on aifoundry2 from the
phone's lossless 25 fps stream with its settings left as they were (the public live page kept its look). Every spatial
block is a **constant-total-power swap between equal-hart patterns**, cycled and shown as a phase-averaged difference
image, because the board blurs heat 29–49 mm, wider than the 22 × 26 mm die: the back of the board shows where one blob
is, not the shape of a hot region. Blocks: E62-CAL (the power ladder and the camera gate), E62-ORBIT (the four corners
then the centre, 128 harts each), E62-BLOOM (all 32 shires on and off), E62-WE42 and E62-NS42 (the west↔east swap and
its 90° keystone), E62-NULLAA (the same mask in both arms), E62-NULLGEO (12 shires against their 180° rotation),
E62-W8BE8B (the feature-matched mirror pair) and E62-SWEEP (a 4 × 2 block sliding one column at a time). New code:
`tools/claims-v3/hm/` (`run_hm.py`, `record_cam.py`, `p3rec.py`, `reduce_hm.py`, `analyze_hm.py`, `render_hm.py`).
**Amendment A1** ([`prereg/AMENDMENTS.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/hm/prereg/AMENDMENTS.md), 17:14 PDT, during
E62-ORBIT, nothing reduced): E62-CAL measured about 2.5× the designed power per hart, so the registered power fallback
fired. Every 12-shire set ran at `--per-shire 16` and every 8-shire set at `--per-shire 24` (192 harts either way),
UNI32 at 24 (768 harts), E62-BLOOM's off-phase 85.0 → 127.5 s, E62-WE17 dropped. Session 1 (CAL, ORBIT) ended by an
operator STOP in the gap after ORBIT and session 2 ran `protocol-A1.json` (sha256 `5d7910c4…`). 390 launches over
71.1 min of card time, 0 failed; claimed and released in #community-lab.
**Result** (9 blocks: 1 CONFIRMED, 8 PARTLY by their registered rules; the camera result is exploratory in the lab's
grading, as E61's was; data in [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-21-e62-heat-movies/`](../reports/data/2026-10-21-e62-heat-movies/)):
**the back of the board shows where on the die the compute runs.** The camera sees the die turned −88.0 ± 2.8° and not
mirrored (west→east runs up the picture, north→south runs right), so the hero's four corners land lower-left (NW),
upper-left (NE), upper-right (SE) and lower-right (SW), and the warm patch walks them clockwise in every lap before
fading into the centre: registered lobe contrast 1,271 mK, **+846 mK = 5.6 σ over its own permutation null, p = 0.0005**
(floor 0.001). Moving 192 harts from the die's west twelve shires to its east twelve at the same total power moves the
warm half of the picture by **+525 ± 22 mK** (4 laps: 585, 527, 500, 489; post hoc, cross-session template; the
registered lobe contrast is 1,071 mK with p at the 4-lap floor of 0.125). The same 24 shires split north/south move it
by **+511 ± 7 mK** and turn the pattern **94°** (first moment; 98° by the lobe centroids; registered band 90 ± 10°).
E62-SWEEP's block steps monotonically west→east (+253, +179, +49, −187, −294 mK along ORBIT's west–east template, post
hoc; registered excess +698 mK = 4.6 σ, p = 0.0005) while staying within ±21 mK on the north–south axis. The controls
hold: E62-NULLAA, the same mask in both arms, reads **+4 ± 11 mK** and E62-NULLGEO **−8 ± 9 mK** (post hoc; registered
excesses 0.65 σ and 1.2 σ). The replicate E62-W8BE8B points the same way as E62-WE42 (87° against 89°) at **+415 ± 135
mK** over its two laps (post hoc), too coarse to price the adjacency correction it was registered to arbitrate. The
bloom (768 harts, +39 W switched as measured: the governor throttled it, and the clean power law would have drawn 49 W) warms the whole frame by **+7.1 K** at the end of a 42.3 s on-phase and reaches
half its rise 14.8 s after the load step: what a change in total power looks like, and what every swap is built to
cancel. E62-CAL **refutes the design's power model**: the card is linear in harts but as **5.80 ± 0.30 W + 55.8 ± 1.0
mW per hart**, not 40.6 mW per hart from zero, and three 128-hart legs at 4 × 32, 8 × 16 and 16 × 8 agree within
0.51 W (power follows harts, not geometry). Against the re-priced predictions the amplitudes run high (ORBIT about
1.7×, SWEEP 2–2.4×, BLOOM about 1.9× its bound) while E62-WE42 sits at 0.9× and E62-W8BE8B at 0.6×: the shape and the
signs are right, a scale is not understood. No millimetres: the registered 10% gate for a scale bar was not met (two
routes disagree by 64%), so every finding is a direction. The measured per-lap noise is 66–80 mK, 2.4–3.0× the
registered prior, and it replaces the prior in every error bar.
**Deviations:** A1 above, whose branch 2 fired on an extrapolation (the UNI32 rung read 43.76 W against a 45 W trigger,
low only because the governor throttled it). Two post-hoc choices, labelled as such wherever they appear: per-pixel
shutter-step removal and cross-session template contrasts ([`tools/claims-v3/hm/posthoc/`](../../tools/claims-v3/hm/posthoc/README.md)),
made after the camera's shutter was seen to fire every 8–10 s under load with per-pixel steps up to 10 K; every
registered conclusion stands without them except E62-W8BE8B's amplitude (−23 ± 109 mK uncleaned). The registered FFC
drop window (§7.2) was not applied: it would remove 45–82% of every block. The bloom's 64 °C per-launch gate never armed
(`run_hm.py` keyed it to 1,024 harts and A1 ran 768): the die reached 69 °C and the governor stepped in every on-phase,
so the bloom's amplitude and arrival time are not the registered measurement; the registered clock fallback was not
implemented in code. The pipeline's orientation fit excludes E62-ORBIT (it takes two-arm blocks only); the orientation
comes from E62-WE42 + E62-NS42 and agrees with ORBIT's corners fitted by hand (−90.2 ± 1.1°). The renderer's own bloom
path was replaced by `posthoc/make_bloom_cycle.py`. Raw frames (1.26 GB + 3.59 GB) are on aifoundry2 in
`build/e62/runs/`, not the registered per-block path. Nothing was unsafe: peak die 69 °C against an 85 °C stop, peak
board 86.0 W against a 95 W cap.
**Report:** [Heat follows the work: ET-SoC-1 heat movies](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-heat-movies).

## E63 — hlc: the camera half of the Hamming ladder, E60's top rung against its zero filmed from the back of the board (Q122; card runs 2026-10-23 from 12:55 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q122):** "run the camera half of the Hamming ladder on aifoundry2. This is E-T10 of the 26 Sep camera plan,
with E60's rungs in place of patterns. […] Reuse E60's operand files (tools/claims-v3/hl) and E62's camera tools
(tools/claims-v3/hm). Switch the highest rung aifoundry2 can hold at 800 MHz against its zero at about 0.05 Hz, with
on-windows of 9.5 s or less and at least 5 minutes per rung. Record raw frames with the switch times logged, and add a
null run (equal watts, same place). In E62 the shutter fired every 8–10 s, so plan around it. Pre-register before the
first pass, claim the card in #community-lab […]."
**Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/hlc/`](../../tools/claims-v3/hlc/README.md), commit `5740cdb7` (12:54 PDT, pushed
before the first card access): E60's lq_h23 against lq_h0 on 8 minions in each of the 32 shires (256; the full card's
zero alone is +31 W, which continuous switching would carry past the governor's step), one 8.5 s launch per 10 s phase
idled out to its dwell (0.05 Hz; every on-window under 9.5 s), four blocks (TOP 9 + 21 laps, NULL ones/pi 3 + 18, MID
lq_h8 3 + 18, TOP2 3 + 15), the camera's settings untouched, and a lock-in that gives every stretch between two shutter
events a free offset, so no step is ever bridged; predictions, a hold gate and five decision rules. New code: E62's runner
with per-set operand files and a fixed period (`run_hlc.py`), `lockin_hlc.py` and its tests; E62's recorder and reducer
unchanged; E60's operand files checked byte for byte on the card (`tile_check.sh`). Amendment A0 (12:56, before any
launch) fixed a path in the tile check; **amendment A1** (`d6704c60`, 13:08, from telemetry only, no frame reduced): rule 2
failed in session 1, so the run repeated as session 2 at 6 minions per shire (192). 360 launches over the two sessions,
none failed; claimed and released in #community-lab.
**Result** (data in [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-23-e63-camera-hamming-ladder/`](../reports/data/2026-10-23-e63-camera-hamming-ladder/README.md);
the full tables in the README's Results): **the thermal camera weighs the flips.** Session 1's top rung put **+5.443 ±
0.008 W** on the board (154 fJ per designed flipped bit at 0.618 V) and the back of the board over the die followed with
**194.2 ± 9.1 mK at 0.05 Hz** (p at the 20,000-draw floor, SNR 21): **59.0 ± 3.0 mK per watt** of power at that frequency,
the heat **87 ± 5°** (about 5 s) behind the power. The null, ones against pi in the same 256 minions (−0.04 W apart), read
**1.9 ± 15.0 mK** along the top rung's phase; the middle rung moved the camera 0.24 ± 0.08 of the top rung's amplitude
for 0.27 of its power; the top rung run again read 178.8 ± 9.1 mK. Session 2 (192 minions) repeated it: 4.02 W, 118.7 ±
13.0 mK, null −13.6 ± 8.4, middle rung 0.33 ± 0.05 for 0.28. All five rules pass in session 2; in session 1 rules 1, 3, 4
and 5 pass and **rule 2 fails** (39 of 42 top-rung launches held 800 MHz, 92.9% against 95%; 98.8% of samples). The
in-phase map carries the capacitor field's emissivity pattern (high-passed correlation with E62's bloom map 0.90–0.92),
the focal plane's thermistor shows nothing at f, and the shutter (2.8–10.7 a minute by block) fell at no preferred phase,
so the signal is heat on the board, not the camera. Against the predictions: steps and lag as registered; the camera's
amplitude inside the registered band but 1.8× the central value (the transfer 59 mK/W against ~31 from E62's bloom); the
noise twice the predicted.
**Deviations and findings beside the rules:** (1) the transfer drifted through the hour, 59.0 → 52.9 → 47.8 → 42.4 mK/W
over the four top-rung blocks, the lag 87° → 109°, as the board's back and the camera warmed; the rules compare blocks
within a session and stand, but a camera wattmeter needs its own reference near each reading. (2) In 8 of 360 launches
the clock fell to 600 MHz for 0.6–1.5 s at 1.4–8.4 s into a launch, at every load and a 54–57 °C die, the governor's
idle-reset shape (`14-card-behaviour.md`, "A one-second idle reset inside a launch"); this, not heat, is what failed rule 2
in session 1. (3) The header's `fpa_c` is the scene's mean, not the focal plane (it tracks the frame mean, ~5 °C above
it); the registered camera-body check rests on VTEMP. (4) `reduce_hm.py` bridged one session-2 shutter step 5.0 K wrong;
the plain lock-in on the bridged frames reads that block 127 ± 119 mK, the registered fit 36.3 ± 3.3. Session 1's frames
were reduced on the Intel from a byte-identical copy (sha256 checked) while session 2 ran; raw frames (2.1 + 2.0 GB) stay
on aifoundry2 in `build/e63/runs/`. Nothing was unsafe: peak die 57.3 °C, peak board 42 W.
**Corrected Mon 26 Oct 2026 (Q126):** session 2's prices per flipped bit were three quarters of the truth, because
`summary_hlc.py` hard-coded 256 minions and session 2 ran amendment A1's 192. **113.8 → 151.7 fJ** (top rung),
**114.3 → 152.4** (the top rung again) and **90.2 → 120.3** (the middle rung), with the camera per 10¹³ flips a second
rising in step (33.59 → 44.79, 29.38 → 39.17, 29.52 → 39.36 mK). Session 1's 154 and 122 fJ stand, and no registered
number or verdict moves: the minion count enters no decision rule. The script now takes the minions from
`launches.jsonl`'s `harts` and the clock from the telemetry — **that record's own `mhz_minion` is sampled before the
launch opens and reads 600 MHz in all 360 records of both sessions**, while 99.6% of the in-launch samples are at 800
(the median over the steady windows is 800 in all eight blocks, the mean 797.4–800.0). Session 1's RED had to be rebuilt
for the re-run and finds 244 shutter events against the committed 243, which moves only its null's plain bridged lock-in,
13.4 → 14.2 mK against an SE of 47.
**Post hoc, no verdict (Q126,** [`tools/claims-v3/hlc/posthoc/`](../../tools/claims-v3/hlc/posthoc/README.md)**):**
(a) the square drive's third harmonic, which the registered fit carries as a nuisance column and throws away, read out
as **a second transfer point at 0.15 Hz**: pooled over the four top-rung blocks as a vector mean of the complex
transfer, **18.4 ± 5.4 mK/W at 151° against 52.7 ± 2.0 at 95° at 0.05 Hz**, a ratio of **0.34 ± 0.10** — the board is
rolling off between the two, **6.3 SE from flat**, as a single pole of ~12 s would or any pole longer (1 σ: τ ≥ 3.2 s,
no upper bound, because a one-pole ratio bottoms out at 1/3 and the measurement sits on that floor), and the 87–109°
lag at f is in any case more phase than one pole can give, so the pole is a scale and not a model.
No top-rung block's camera third harmonic clears 2 SE on its own (8.3 ± 10.2 to 19.5 ± 13.0 mK, 0.8–1.5 SE); the
middle rung's best is 21.3 ± 9.8 mK, 2.2 SE, and the board's own third harmonics are 4.3–14.3 SE.
*(Second pass, the same day: first published as 19.0 ± 5.2 against 54.1 ± 1.8, inverse-variance means of four
magnitudes — biased upward, since |Z| of a noisy complex quantity averages above the magnitude of its mean — and as
"3.3 SE from flat", which was the ratio's distance from zero. The ratio now carries one leave-one-lap-out jackknife
over both harmonics at once, 0.343 ± 0.104, against the first pass's quadrature of two SEs, 0.352 ± 0.100.)*
(b) The card's own rails, demodulated from the same 10 Hz telemetry: **84–86% of every rung block's board step is on
the minion rail**, under 1% on each of the SRAM and NoC rails, 85–88% on the three together, the remaining 12–15% being
the board's conversion loss and the rails the sampler does not break out.
**Report:** [Weighing flipped bits with heat: the camera half of the Hamming ladder](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-bitflip-camera).

## E64 — hpl: Horace, power limit — which of the governor's two limits sets the matmul's speed (Q123; card runs 2026-10-23 from 20:31 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q123):** "test the hypothesis that the underlying issue is power limit and not temperature limit. Look up the
existing numbers [...] Do a study of underlying specifications of the chip and check if it's indeed the power limit
that's limiting it, and give me the surface of this power limit. What kind of speeds would I expect for different types
of numbers? [...] Run necessary experiments on AI Foundry2."
**Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/hpl/`](../../tools/claims-v3/hpl/README.md), commit `2b951fca` (pushed before the
first card access): the Horace kernel (one 16×16 TensorFMA per minion on L1 tiles, `build/sparsity_t2`, kernel sha256
`990807f4…`) in four blocks — LAD (ten fp32 operand patterns on all 1,024 minions, 36–84 W at 800 MHz, from a die of 55 °C
or less, two shuffled passes), MIN (random normal on 768, 640, 512 minions), HOT (ones and zeros launched on a heated die's
falling edge at 67 or 63 °C) and SUS (fp32, fp16, int8 × zeros, ones, random, nine 8 s processes back to back) — with
nothing set on the card (TDP and threshold read back after every run), a 10 Hz sampler without `--reset-ms`, and after
every run the SP's trace ring, its throttle residencies and its configuration; rules R1–R4. New code: the runner
`run_hpl.py` (E63's etiquette: card lock, other-user checks before every launch process, `timeout 10`, the safety
watcher; a dry mode with a toy governor), `reduce_hpl.py`, and `sptrace_full.py`, a whole-ring reader. Amendments, each
before the data it touched: **A0** (`4b2b1401`: the use-count baseline is taken with our own sampler open; the first smoke stopped
before any launch), **A1** (`69380c49`: the dv2 trace reader stops at the first 16-byte binary power-status record and
lost most governor lines, so the whole ring is scanned; entries are placed by matching the SP's `New OP` lines to the
sampler's clock changes), **A2** (`1a899d8f`: the HOT heater could not take the die past a 65–67 °C reading, so R3(a)
counts only runs that start at 66 °C or more, and a HOT2 block heats with 8 s launches), **A3** (with the results: the
4 KB ring keeps only its last ~67 entries, a few seconds of a dither, so the placement is anchored at the run's end and
R4 is also reported on the sampler alone). Three sessions: the smoke (20:31:56), `s1` 20:37:01–21:08:43 (43 runs, 31.7
card-minutes, none failed) and `s2` (HOT2, 21:09–21:11); claimed and released in #community-lab.
**Result** (data in [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-23-e64-horace-power-limit/`](../reports/data/2026-10-23-e64-horace-power-limit/README.md)):
**both limits act, each on its own; which sets the speed depends on the die's temperature, and in sustained work on this
card's cooling heat wins.** R1 PASS: every one of the 10 cool-die runs whose work drew 67 W or more at 800 MHz was
stepped down first by the power test (98 power throttle-downs logged, at a 57–63 °C die, the governor's reading 65.0–67.9
W). R2 PASS: none of the 10 runs under 62 W got one; random normal data on 768 minions (69 W) was stepped down, on 640
(62.5 W) it was not. The power loop is a dither (800 → 700 → 600 → 700 MHz for random fp32, ~82 / 64 / 50 W at the three
points) around a ~1 s running average: a model with a 1.3 s first-order average compared with 65 W once per 0.24 s pass
reproduces the mean clock of all 26 cool-die runs to 3.3 MHz rms. On a cool die zeros run 13% faster than random normal
data (800 against 710 MHz): Horace's effect by Horace's mechanism. R3 PASS: work under the power level (ones, zeros)
launched at a 66–67 °C reading held 600 MHz until the die read 65 (4 of 4), and every such run that reached 66 °C at
800 MHz was stepped down by the thermal test and not the power test (4 of 4); 104 thermal steps logged in all, 102 at
66 °C and 2 at 67 °C. R4 PASS: in 80 s runs the thermal test sets the sustained clock (the line through the runs' last
40 s reads 65.5 °C at 50.3 W of board power, under the 65 W level): random fp32 and fp16 data end pinned at 600 MHz with
the die at 66–67 °C; ones and int8 random settle at 737–763 MHz; zeros hold 800 MHz at 59 °C; sustained, zeros run 33%
faster than random fp32 data (12.3 against 9.2 TFLOPS). Every number type's burst and sustained rate, and the governor
model's rate under caps of 35–85 W, are in the README's Results.
**Deviations and findings beside the rules:** (1) the governor's power input is a ~1 s running average, not the
instantaneous reading `ffca4cbb4`'s source names (14-card-behaviour.md, "Which limit acts"); (2) a power throttle-down is
not sticky (P5 refuted); (3) the trace ring carries `New OP` lines and binary power-status records, and the sampler's board
value trails the clock by ~2 samples; (4) a random-data heater cannot take this die past ~67 °C, because the two limits
hold it there (A2); (5) the governor model's surface (mean clock against the data's 800 MHz power and the power level) is a
model, never tested at another level: the lab rule sets no TDP, threshold or clock on a shared card.
**Report:** [Power, then heat: what limits the ET-SoC-1's matmul speed](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-power-limit).


## E65 — hb: every bit position, the flow-M case, and the hart ladder (Q126; card runs 2026-10-26 from 16:45 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q126):** the card block of the week-43 Horace recap's "Week 44: the moves" — the 26 bit positions E60 never
ran (gap 6), the one rung that flips `b` bit 11 against `a` = π, which is the direct test of the chip diagram's
gate-level price (the price list's flow-M row), the two-kernel hart ladder for E62's 5.80 W offset (gap 5), and a
second launch temperature (gap 4).
**Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/hb/README.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/hb/README.md) (blocks A–D, departures
D1–D4, predictions A1–A3, B1–B2, C1–C4, D1, decision rules), committed as `6da964d2` before the first measured pass,
with amendments `b27d323c` (A5), `e53c9945` (A6), `0adf4a3d` (A7), `5136a5ef` (A8) and `768b2cf5` (A9), each pushed
before the data it touches, and two post-data corrections, both disclosed as such: `658ae590` (A10),
which brought `reduce_ladder.py`'s printed C1 verdict back in line with the registered rule's signed second branch,
and `cd62ed56` (A11), which corrects three statements in the write-up that the committed data contradicts —
A9's launch temperature, D1's contrast (a departure, deviation 7) and A2's reference — changing no rule, no code
and no measured number. One card: **aifoundry3**, latched at 600 MHz by `et-board-clock-guard`, 521.9 mV on every sample.
**Departures:** D1, E62's and hp's heater are one program on this card (`lib.sh:35-43` builds `sparsity_t2` only on
aifoundry2), so the ladder's two arms are two operand sets through the one kernel — `randn`, which is E62's and hp's
stimulus argument for argument, and `zeros`. D3, no RTL predictions for the new items (no Verilator on this host),
so blocks A and B are registered against measured E60 anchors and the Q124 gate-level price. A7, block D's
registered 65 °C launch is unreachable (the card plateaus at 60–61 °C), so it runs with no approach at all.

**Results** (card runs 16:45–18:22 PDT; claimed in #community-lab at 16:45, released after the last pass). Data, the four reductions and every pass's telemetry: `docs/reports/data/2026-10-26-e65-bit-prices-hart-ladder/`.
- **Which bit, all 32 positions** (A = 1.0, above constant operands, 5 passes after the gates, 205 runs): the mantissa is **two regimes**. Bits 0–9 are flat at about 109 fJ per flipped bit; from bit 10 the price climbs monotonically to **1008 fJ** at bit 22. The exponent bits (23–30) average 174 fJ — dearer than the low mantissa but cheaper than every mantissa bit above 10 — and the sign bit costs 123 fJ. Span over all 32 positions **12.2×** (83 fJ at bit 6, 1008 fJ at bit 22). E60's six positions reproduce on the same card and clock within 1.122×, six days on — and within 1.106× of E60's aifoundry1 card 0 rescaled by f·V², which is the test A2 registered.
- **The flow-M case** (`b` bit 11 alternating, `a` = π, the stimulus the chip diagram priced at the gate level): **3.42 ± 0.26 W = 745 ± 56 fJ per flipped bit** at the board, all-in. Against `tfma.flip.energy`'s 0.11–0.13 pJ in the multiplier (0.15–0.18 pJ with that page's ×1.4 for glitches) the board is 6.2× and 4.4×, so **the Booth rows, the tree and the product adder are about 23% of what a flipped operand bit costs**; the rest is the bus, the read ports, the result path, the accumulator and the clocked bits.
- **The busy partner** costs more at every shared position: A = π over A = 1.0 is 1.59–4.73×, 5 of 5 at ≥ 2 SE — far more than E60's +11% on the ladder slope.
- **The hart ladder, and E62's 5.80 W offset: it is not there.** With an IDLE bracket on both sides of every rung, 64–1,024 harts: randn **-0.51 ± 0.03 W + 27.1 mW/hart** (residual rms 0.271 W), zeros **+0.013 ± 0.016 W + 2.08 mW/hart** (residual rms 0.014 W). E62's 5.80 W + 55.8 mW/hart at 800 MHz scales to this card by f·V² = 0.537 as 3.12 W + 30.0 mW/hart: **the slope lands, the offset does not**. The zeros intercept is zero to ±0.02 W, so the fixed cost of having a kernel running at all is under a watt here and independent of how hard the data switches; the randn line's small negative intercept is its own convexity (the per-hart price rises 24.4 → 27.0 mW/hart across the rungs, as the die goes 55 → 61 °C), not a negative fixed cost. E62's own baseline was one pre-session idle at a 49 °C die while its rungs ran at 52–61 °C.
- **The switching drift with temperature** (block D): the registered 65 °C launch was judged unreachable on this
card, so A7 ran eight no-approach passes and A9 six at a 61 °C setpoint. A7's runs launched across **55–62 °C**,
climbing monotonically pass by pass — 7 °C of spread — but **all eight passes were set aside by the registered
anchor-drift gate** (+0.57 to +0.74 W, every one the same sign): a pass that never idles back down warms
monotonically, so the climb that produced the spread is the climb that tripped the gate. A7 changed the protocol
and left the gate that assumes it. A9's passes reached 61.0 °C with zero
skipped runs, five of six surviving the gate, giving a gated 3.00 °C contrast against blocks A and B's 58 °C on
**`bit_22` − `lq_h0`** — a **departure** from the registered `lq_h8` − `lq_h0`, which blocks A and B never ran
(deviation 7 below): **+22.0 ± 36.1 mW/°C = +0.47 ± 0.78 %/°C**, 95% CI −1.05 to +1.99 %/°C. The point estimate is
the registered band's own value and the interval contains zero, so **D1 is CONSISTENT but UNDECIDED**. Block C saw
the same term with better leverage and a confound: the randn ladder's per-hart price rises 24.4 → 27.0 mW/hart
(+11%) as its die goes 55.0 → 60.7 °C, where the zeros ladder is flat at a die that barely moves.
- **Decision rules:** A1 PASS (Spearman ρ 0.927 over the mantissa, span 12.2× against the 5× mark); A2 PASS (E60's
six positions reproduce within **1.106×** under the registered rescaled test and **1.122×** same-card); **A3 FAIL on both
halves** (the sign bit is 10th of 32, not among the four cheapest; mantissa bits 11–15 cost more than every exponent
bit); **B1 FAIL on its band** (745 fJ against a registered 100–250 fJ) and PASS on board ≥ gate; B2 PASS (5 of 5 at
≥ 2 SE, against a 3-of-5 mark); **C1 UNDECIDED** (the intercepts differ by −0.52 W, 0.02 W outside the first branch
and the wrong sign for the second — A10); **C2 neither band** (−0.51 W against [2.5, 3.8] and [5.0, 6.6]), which is
the result; C3 PASS on randn (27.1 against 30.0 ± 6 mW/hart) and **FAIL on zeros** (8% of randn, under the
registered 10–40%); C4 PASS (worst residual rms 0.271 W against 0.4 W); **D1 CONSISTENT, UNDECIDED**.

**Deviations and findings beside the rules:** (1) **A3 fails both halves** — the sign bit is not among the four
cheapest positions (it is 10th of 32), and mantissa bits 11–15 cost more than every exponent bit, so "the exponent
is dearer than the low mantissa" holds only below bit 10. (2) **B1's registered band was mis-anchored**: 100–250 fJ
came from the *marginal* ladder slope, while the item is measured against the constant-operand zero and therefore
carries the turn-on, which E60's own amendment A1 had already warned about ("the bit scan is quoted as 'item minus
lq_h0', not as a per-flip price"). The number is reported both ways. (3) **E60 ran its six positions on *both*
cards**, so A2 is scorable two ways and passes both: its committed `hl.json` carries all six under
`aifoundry1-c0` *and* `aifoundry3`, because E60's amendment A1 **added** the family to aifoundry3 passes 5–8
while keeping aifoundry1 card 0's registered list. The registered rule as written — E60's aifoundry1-c0 values
rescaled by f·V² = 0.562 — gives a worst ratio of **1.106**, and the same-card reading **1.122**, both inside the
registered 1.5. E60's "Per card" table is an accurate record of its own registration and is left untouched.
(4) **aifoundry3 reaches 59–62 °C under this duty cycle**, not E60's probed 64 °C, and the block-D obstacle was a
gate, not a ceiling: blocks A and B's approaches peaked at **59–61 °C** (107 of 123 at 60.0) before idling down,
and the no-approach block-D passes launched across **55–62 °C** (median 60), climbing monotonically pass by pass —
**7 °C of spread**, more than the 3 °C D1 was finally scored on. All eight were then set aside by the registered
0.3 W `anchor_pi` drift gate, because the same within-pass warming that makes the spread trips the gate; that,
not a lack of temperature, is why A9's gated 61 °C setpoint regime was needed. (Amendment A9 states this as
"59 °C on every run" and "under 2 °C"; A11 corrects it against the committed log.) (5) **The randn hart ladder is convex and the
zeros ladder is not** (24.4 → 27.0 mW/hart against 2.3 → 2.1), and the randn rungs heat the die 55 → 61 °C while
the zeros rungs sit at 52–53 °C, so the convexity tracks the die temperature rather than the hart count — the same
switching-against-temperature term block D measures. (6) **E60's departures live in `hl/block.sh`, not in the
shared library**: running E62's `run_hm.py` and E60's `reduce_hl.py` outside their home block each needed one
aifoundry3-specific fix the block script already had (the idle-login test, A5; the launch-reading gate, A8).
(7) 🛑 **D1 was scored on a contrast the registration does not name.** Registered D1 is `lq_h8` − `lq_h0` across
58 °C and 65 °C, but `lq_h8` was registered in block D alone — blocks A and B, the only blocks at 58 °C, run
`bit_<j>` against `lq_h0` and `bpi_<j>` against `lb_h0`, and `runs.csv` has 0 `lq_h8` runs against block D's 14 —
so the registered contrast had data at one temperature and was unmeasurable by construction. D1 is scored on
**`bit_22` − `lq_h0`**, the only item pair the three blocks share. That is a **departure**, disclosed in
amendment A11 and in the data README, not the registered rule; the lesson is that an across-regime prediction
must have its items registered in every block it compares.
**Card-behaviour lessons** (the plateau and the drift gate) are filed in
[`docs/findings/14-card-behaviour.md`](14-card-behaviour.md) as AGENT.md §10 routes them.
**Report:** the data README, [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-26-e65-bit-prices-hart-ladder/`](../reports/data/2026-10-26-e65-bit-prices-hart-ladder/README.md); no public page was made.

## E67 — ha: the A side of the price list, the busy partner, and the interaction grid (Q128; card runs 2026-10-26 from 21:04 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q128):** "fill the missing pieces needed to address my original queries (tracking down difference in
multiplication speed down to cost of individual transistor switching) … run the experiments on aifoundry machines".
The card piece of that is the half of the operand price list nobody had measured. E65 priced all 32 positions of
**B** with the quiet partner A = 1.0; the **A side — the Booth-recoded operand, broadcast to all eight lanes and
driving the recoder that selects each partial product — had never been priced**, so every summed per-bit model was
assuming the two sides match, and nothing had ever tested whether per-bit prices may be summed at all.
**Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/ha/README.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/ha/README.md) (blocks 1–4, departures
D1–D4, predictions P1–P8, decision rules), committed as `2e9ad85a` at 21:01:37 PDT — **2 min 09 s before the card
was claimed** (21:03:46 PDT, from the Discord message's own snowflake) **and 3 min 11 s before the first measured
launch** (21:04:48 PDT, the earliest `t_start_ms` in the committed runs) — with amendments A11 and A12 in that
commit (the seven new operand families; the per-item zero the reduction needed), and A13 (`ee9faed6`), A14
(`655082c7`) and A15 (`4443402b`) each written before the data it touches: A13 and A14 after block 1's first pass
and before any block-3 data, A15 while block 3 ran and before its data was reduced. All three add reported
quantities; none moves a registered band, rule or number. One card: **aifoundry3**, latched at 600 MHz by
`et-board-clock-guard`, 521.9 mV on every sample — deliberately E65's card, clock and rail. **Every one of the 87
operand files E60 and E65 ran is byte-identical in the new set**, checked before the first launch.
**Departures:** D1, one shared A base word (E60 and E65 drew a fresh random base per position, so an item minus
its zero carried the flip *and* the difference between two random words); bit 30, the exponent MSB, cannot flip
from exponent 127 and uses the exponent-64 twin of the same mantissa with a zero of its own. D2, no RTL
predictions for the new items (no Verilator on this host). D3, the grid is 4 × 4 on mantissa bits. D4, E65's
launch pair (58 °C after 59 °C) unchanged.

**The hardware fact that sets the A side's units.** The issue order (PRM 9.4, `make_hl.py:issue_streams`) is: for
each B row k, for each A row i, two micro-ops. **A[i][k] is held across both** while B alternates every micro-op,
so an A word can change only every *second* cycle: **2,048 designed A-bit flips per multiply-add against B's
4,096**. A scan that assumed both operands flip every cycle would have reported every A-side price exactly 2× too
low and nothing would have errored.

**Results** (card runs 21:04–23:02 PDT, 1 h 58 min; claimed in #community-lab at 21:03, released after the last
pass). 14 passes, **10 kept** by the registered gates, 424 of 554 runs. Data, both reductions and every pass's
telemetry: `docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-e67-a-side-bit-prices/`.
- **The A side at all 32 positions** (B = 1.0, above the shared-base constant-A zero): the same shape as the B
  side, a flat low mantissa rising to a dear top — **153 fJ** per flipped A bit at bit 4 to **1,440 fJ** at bit 22,
  a **9.4× span**, Spearman ρ(bit, price) **0.851** over the mantissa. Exponent bits average 265 fJ, the sign bit
  247 fJ.
- **A costs more per flipped bit than B, but only modestly**: median A/B ratio **1.60** over the 23 mantissa
  positions (1.24 on the clean passes), 19 of 23 inside the registered factor-of-3 band, and the two lists are
  strongly rank-correlated (ρ **0.833**). The Booth-recoded operand is not a different kind of cost; it is the
  same cost at half the flip rate and a somewhat higher price per flip.
- **The tie to E65 holds to 0.40 W on the registered passes** (0.13 W on the clean ones) over the three items
  both ran (`anchor_pi` 36.39 → 36.32, `lq_h0` 36.49 → 36.50, `lb_h0` 36.47 → 36.07, four hours apart), which is
  what lets the A and B price lists be added without a conversion. That registered 0.40 W cross-experiment offset
  is itself the size of the cheapest A-side prices (`abit_4` 0.35 W, `abit_25` 0.36 W, `abit_8` 0.38 W), so a
  position below about 0.5 W carries that much cross-list uncertainty when the two lists are summed.
- **The busy partner costs more at all five shared positions** (B = π over B = 1.0), `abp_22` reaching 1,566 fJ;
  `abp_11`, the A-side mirror of the flow-M case, is **856 fJ** (971 on the clean passes) against E65's `bpi_11`
  at 745 ± 56 fJ, a ratio of 1.15 — the two operands' flow-M prices agree within 30%.
- 🛑 **Per-bit prices do not add, and the failure is saturation, not an offset.** All 16 grid cells are
  sub-additive: mean excess over the sum of their two margins **−1.351 ± 0.281 W** (4.8 SE), largest −4.05 W, and
  the excess tracks the size of the flips (Pearson r **−0.739** against the sum; `cell = 0.94 W + 0.64 × sum`,
  residual rms 0.73 W), so a cell costs **62–96%** of its margins' sum, median 83%.
- 🛑 **And the shortfall deepens with the number of simultaneous flips, which is what a real workload does.**
  Summing the measured per-position prices over the counted flip histograms of `nrd_a` (A random, 27,648 A bits
  per multiply-add) and `nrd_b` (B random, 56,784 B bits) over-predicts their measured steps by **2.3× and 2.0×**
  (measured/predicted 0.43 and 0.50; 0.50 and 0.49 on the clean passes). Scaling by the grid's own pair ratio
  (0.80) leaves 1.6–1.8×. **So the per-bit price list is an upper bound on a random workload, high by about 2×.**
- **The two operand paths, though, do add.** Both operands random is **16.18 W** above the constant-operand level
  where one random side plus the other sums to **18.37 W** — a ratio of **0.88** against a 0.42 W noise floor
  (16.48 W, 18.14 W, 0.91 and 0.33 W on the clean passes). What fails is decomposing one operand into independent
  bits, not separating A from B.
- **The anchors on this card at 600 MHz:** zeros **27.83 W**, ones 36.05, π 36.32, random fp32 **52.49 W** — a
  random-against-zeros step of **24.66 W** (27.86 → 52.88 W, a 25.02 W step, on the clean passes), which
  reproduces the 21 Sep step (38.29 → 63.40 W, 25.1 W) on a different card to within 2% (0.4% clean).
- **Decision rules:** P1 PASS (ρ 0.851, span 9.4× against a 5× mark); P2 PASS (ρ 0.833, 19/23 in band); **P3 FAIL**
  (the sign bit ranks 8th of 32, and 92 mantissa-below-16 bits cost at least as much as an exponent bit — the same
  way E65's A3 failed on the B side); **P4 FAIL on significance** (busy − quiet is positive at 5 of 5 positions but
  0 of 5 reach 2 SE on the registered passes; 5 of 5 do on the clean ones); P5 PASS (856 fJ, ratio 1.15);
  **P6 FAIL, sub-additive** (mean excess −1.351 ± 0.281 W, 8 of 16 cells within 20%); P7 PASS (0.687 against
  [0.3, 0.8]); **P8 FAIL** (0.43 and 0.50 against a factor-of-2 band).

**Deviations and findings beside the rules:** (1) 🛑 **The registered pass gate fires on the wrong passes.** It is
E60's `anchor_pi` first-versus-last drift over 0.3 W. E67 runs six items whose operands never change —
`aq_h0`, `aq_e64`, `ap_h0`, `lq_h0`, `lb_h0`, `ix_z` — plus `anchor_pi` twice, one physical state by several
routes, and their **spread within a pass** is the honest measure: 0.17–0.37 W in passes 750–753 and **0.62, 2.02,
1.12, 0.97 W** in 754–757. The registered gate sets aside 754 (the 0.62 W one) and **keeps 755 at 2.02 W**,
because `anchor_pi`'s own two runs agree there. The symptom is in the launch records: from pass 754 the host-side
launch overhead grew (calibration 18 → 23 ms, inter-launch gap 1.5 → 10–15 ms, rate 1.121 → 1.085 Gop/s) and a
minority of runs read 1–2 W **low** at an unchanged clock, rail, launch reading and cycles-per-op, so no per-run
gate sees them; the pass median never moves, so bad runs dip and never rise. 🛑 **The cause was the host, not the
card:** another lab user began CPU work on aifoundry3's **host** in four waves — 4 processes at **21:42:40**, 4 at
22:06:41 and 12 at 22:28:59–22:29:27, 20 in all on 16 cores at about 75% of a core each, load average about 22 —
and **never held the card**, so `et-who` read "no holder" throughout and the protocol's card-scoped other-user
test (departure D1) correctly did not block. Nothing in the protocol watches the host's CPU. The first wave lands
inside pass 754, the first degraded pass, and the rate fell then and never recovered; the correlation is not
monotone, though — the two highest-load grid passes, 774 and 775, have the session's smallest spreads — so host
contention is the well-supported cause of the sustained rate change and the likely but unproven cause of the
intermittent dips. Amendment A15 adds
`ha/constspread.py` and a **labelled post-hoc sensitivity** over the passes whose spread is ≤ 0.40 W (7 of 14,
277 of 277 runs). **The registered reduction is the result**; only P3 (FAIL → PARTIAL) and P4 (FAIL → PASS) move,
both toward the registered expectation, and every headline is unchanged. The lesson is filed for the next
experiment: a pass gate must watch every item that should not move, not one item's drift, and a pass should
record the host's load average beside its telemetry — the card being free is not the same as the host being free.
(This card-and-host trap belongs in [`docs/findings/14-card-behaviour.md`](14-card-behaviour.md) under AGENT.md
§10; it is not filed there yet.)
(2) **Amendment A13's turn-on correction is refuted by the block it was registered against.** A13 reasoned that a
price measured against a constant-operand zero is `T + e·n·rate`, so summing it charges the fixed turn-on once per
flipped position (13.5 times for `nrd_a`), and that the grid's excess would measure `−T`. The arithmetic is right
and the model is wrong: the excess is not constant but proportional to the flips, so subtracting a constant
1.35 W per position over-corrects and drives most positions negative (22 of 32 A positions and 21 of 32 B
positions on the registered passes), leaving a corrected closure of **18.37 and 2.44** against the raw 0.43 and
0.50 — and −3.17 and 2.31 against 0.50 and 0.49 on the clean passes, where the over-correction drives the
`nrd_a` prediction below zero. The over-shoot even changes sign with the pass set, which is itself a sign the
correction measures nothing. It is reported as a failed correction, not quietly dropped.
(3) **A14's bias is real and in the stated direction**: because the A side's designed count is half the B side's,
any fixed per-item cost inflates an A-side price twice as much per bit, so the raw A/B ratio of 1.60 is an upper
bound on the marginal one. With saturation rather than a constant turn-on, the marginal ratio cannot be read off
by subtraction, and the honest statement is that the two sides' **marginal** prices are within a factor of about 2
and the raw ones within a factor of 3 at 19 of 23 positions.
(4) **One shared base word per operand side is worth the extra zero.** E60 and E65's per-position random bases put
the difference between two random words into every price; E67's items differ in one bit alone, and the cost is one
extra item (`aq_e64`, for bit 30's exponent base).
**Report:** the data README,
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-e67-a-side-bit-prices/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-e67-a-side-bit-prices/README.md);
the Q128 page carries E67 and E68 together.

## E68 — sm: the stage map, which pipeline stage spends a flipped bit's energy (Q128; card runs 2026-10-26 from 21:11 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q128):** "fill the missing pieces needed to address my original queries (tracking down difference in
multiplication speed down to cost of individual transistor switching) [...] run the experiments on aifoundry
machines"; job (b): "which pipeline stage spends a flipped bit's energy [...] a card-level split of a flipped bit's
cost into multiplier against align / add / normalize / round, to test the gate-level stage splits."
**Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/sm/`](../../tools/claims-v3/sm/README.md), commit `b88c6bdc` (pushed before
the first measured launch at 21:11:45): eleven operand families, one shared base, each differing from its family's
zero in **its mask alone**, run through E60's `hl/block.sh` with two default-preserving knobs (`HL_EXPN`,
`HL_SHIRES`/`HL_PER_SHIRE`). The lever is exact arithmetic rather than a model: an fp32 multiplier never sees a sign
or an exponent, so two B words sharing their significand drive bit-identical switching through the Booth rows, the
Wallace tree, its register and the product adder — **f1 and f2 frozen by construction** — while f3–f6 see two
different results every cycle. The RTL design matrix (`predictions.json`, the `rtl-sim/fma_toggle` bench under
Verilator 5.052 on the Intel, not aifoundry2's 5.042) was frozen in the same commit and confirms the construction:
`sm_sgn`, `sm_e1`, `sm_e16`, `sm_e64` and `sm_pi_sgn` show **exactly 0** toggles in every tree-only module above
their zero, and `sm_sgnlo`'s stage vector equals `sm_lo12`'s plus `sm_sgn`'s to the last toggle in every column.
Every item ran on **512 minions at 800 MHz** so the governor's ~65 W limit never acted. Amendments, each dated:
**A1** (`351a73f5`, after passes 1–3: the leakage slope is fitted on gate-passing runs — this card idles at 600 MHz
and the governor raises it during the launch, so a slope fitted across the ramp reads 4.911 W/°C against the steady
runs' 0.456), **A2** and **A3** (more passes), **A5** (`a9fffd69`: A3's pass rate was wrong — passes 4–7, not 4–18,
ran in 7.5 minutes — and this session killed a running pass by misreading run_hl's end-of-pass marker as the
sweep's; the block's TERM trap released the lock and the pass is in the data as a non-ok pass the reduction drops),
**A4** and **A6** (`270985c4`: `dP_raw_W` and the per-flipped-bit price are reported beside the registered `dP_W`),
and **A7** (four corrections to the committed record after a review: A6's cross-card attribution, the leakage
slope actually applied, the post-hoc fit's quality and `s_mul`'s uncertainty, and a second aborted attempt —
no rule, band or verdict changes).
41 passes, 574 runs, 453 kept; claimed in #community-lab at 21:09 (`1564491254660595715`), released in the morning
after E66.
**Result** (data in [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-e68-stage-map/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-e68-stage-map/README.md)):
**the multiplier is about half of a mantissa flip's in-unit cost, a flipped bit that never enters the multiplier
costs as much as one that does, and energy is not proportional to toggle counts — but it is additive across
stimuli.** Every kept run held 800 MHz and the highest kept board reading was 51.1 W; 121 runs were dropped by the
registered clock gate, which is the 600→800 MHz ramp inside the window. 🛑 **Every price is a band, because the leakage slope is the one number not measured on this experiment's own data**
(A7). A1's `--leak auto` returned **1.508 W/°C**, 3.3× the 0.456 A1 itself argued for; **E66 measured this card's
idle leakage the same night over the band E68 ran in at 0.296 W/°C** (51–58 °C, rms 0.116 W, 2,248 samples),
5.1× smaller, and every independent estimate agrees with E66 (E60's own fit on this card 0.442, the published
law at 59 °C 0.36, E44's idle rails 0.5–0.6). The estimator fits power against temperature *inside each 2 s busy
run* (median rise 1.65 °C), so it reads a running kernel's transient, not idle leakage. Prices per flipped bit, at
A1's 1.508 and at E66's 0.296: `sm_lo12` (12 mantissa bits) **117.5 ± 2.8 / 135.9 ± 2.7 fJ**, `sm_hi12`
173.3 ± 4.3 / 194.4 ± 3.3, `sm_sp12` 187.4 ± 3.8 / 216.3 ± 3.9, `sm_sgnlo` 119.7 ± 2.9 / 135.4 ± 2.4, and the
frozen-tree items `sm_sgn` **179.3 ± 36.3 / 161.8 ± 27.7**, `sm_e1` 201.9 ± 46.1 / 223.3 ± 37.0, `sm_e16`
112.8 ± 31.7 / 123.2 ± 27.6, `sm_e64` 282.5 ± 32.0 / 276.4 ± 31.0, `sm_pi_sgn` 125.3 ± 39.3 / 147.6 ± 43.2.
**The cross-card check, corrected:** 116.7 and 129.9 fJ are both E60's *aifoundry3* at 600 MHz (its quiet and busy
ladders), not aifoundry1 at 800 MHz and not E65's; E60 ran **this same card** and read **121.3 ± 0.6 fJ**. Corrected
at E60's own same-card slope, E68 reads **133.7 ± 2.5 fJ against 121.3 ± 0.6 — about 10 % higher**. So the ladder
reproduces across experiments, cards and operand designs to about 10 %, and the earlier claim that it landed
exactly on E60 was a coincidence of quoting the wrong card against an over-corrected price. **P2 PASS** (the result the
power model needed): the RTL says a sign flip and twelve mantissa flips add exactly, and the card says their
*energies* add too — `sm_lo12` + `sm_sgn` = 4.860 W against `sm_sgnlo`'s 4.756 W, +0.105 W in 4.8, about 2 %, inside
a 0.476 W tolerance. **P3 PASS**: `sm_sgn` costs 0.548 ± 0.111 W, 4.9 SE above zero, with zero tree toggles — 0.127
of a 12-bit mantissa flip for one twelfth the bits. **P6 PASS**: `sm_sp12` − `sm_lo12` = +2.558 W at 15 SE, so
twelve bits are not twelve bits. **P7 PASS**. **P1 FAIL**: the registered two-column fit wants a *negative*
multiplier coefficient (b_mul −0.177 ± 0.160, b_back +2.480 ± 0.284 W per million toggles/op), and it does so at
every leakage slope tried (−0.086 at 0 W/°C through −0.177 at A1's 1.508), so it is the column definition, not the
correction: the bench counts **by file**, and `txfma_csa` and `txfma_adder` each sit in both the fraction path and
the exponent path, so for `sm_e16` the 201,216 `txfma_adder` toggles are the exponent adder's while the registered
map books them to f4. Post-hoc, with each item's ambiguous files assigned by whether its own arithmetic changes the
significand and the four wrapper files dropped, the fit is sensible: **b_mul +0.517 ± 0.161, b_back +0.957 ± 0.264
W per million toggles/op — a back-end toggle costs 1.85× a multiplier toggle — and s_mul = 0.50 ± 0.15**, so
between about a third and two thirds of a 12-bit mantissa flip's in-unit cost is the multiplier, against the gate
level's 15–17 % of a *board* flip, whose remainder is the operand bus, the register file's read ports, the result
path and the accumulator, all outside the unit. Two caveats on that fit (A7): it fits the nine items **worse**
than the registered columns it replaces — rms **0.429 W against 0.179 W** at the same slope, with structured
residuals (`sm_hi12` +0.923, `sm_sp12` +0.525, `sm_lo12` −0.479) — so it is a diagnosis of the column definition,
not a better model; and its two columns are **96 % anti-correlated**, which makes the split between them the
weakest part of the result. `s_mul` is nonetheless the one quantity stable against the leakage slope (0.50–0.56). **P5 FAIL**, and it falsifies something worth keeping: the RTL's back-end counts rise monotonically with the
exponent step (573 k, 708 k, 990 k toggles for ±1, ∓16, ∓64) and the energy does not — `sm_e16` costs 0.345 ± 0.097 W
where `sm_e1` costs 0.617 ± 0.141, a 2.2× spread in watts per toggle inside one family whose tree is frozen and
whose only difference is how far the aligner shifts. **P4 FAIL** on a difference that is not there: 0.166 W against
a combined SE of 0.163 W, 1.0 SE, which is the null P4 predicted; the registered rule had no SE clause, so the
tighter of its two bands (25 % of `sm_sgn`) scored a null as a failure.
**Deviations and findings beside the rules:** (1) this card's clock ramps 600→800 MHz inside the measurement
window, so 21 % of runs are dropped by the registered clock gate and the leakage slope must be fitted on the rest
(A1); (2) the leakage coefficient is the one number not measured on this experiment's own data, and it moves the
busy items most (`sm_sp12` reads 8.19 W uncorrected, 7.93 W at E66's measured 0.296 and 6.87 W at A1's 1.508);
**E66 measured it the same night at 0.296 W/°C over 51–58 °C**, 5.1× below the slope the registered reduction
applied, so every price above is published at both and the morning's re-run with `--leak` is a labelled
sensitivity, never a replacement for the registered scoring; (3) `txfma_csa` and `txfma_adder` cannot be split by file, so any by-file stage attribution of this
bench is wrong for stimuli that move the exponent path; (4) **two** passes were aborted and restarted, and both are now published as non-ok passes (A5, A7): `p1.attempt-1793074293`,
killed at 21:11:22 by a `USER: unbound variable` fault in `lib.sh` under a tmux session started without that
variable, and `p8.attempt-1793075288`, killed by this session's own error. `gather` reads only `p<digits>`
directories, so neither can reach a number.
**Report:** pending — the Q128 page carries E67 and E68 together.

## E69 — knob: the governor's cap and threshold, moved for the first time (Q129; card runs 2026-10-27, 14:20–14:42 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q129):** "Go through Horace's hub and fill in unresolved pieces … For instance, '5. What is still open'
section", with the owner's added authorization of about 13:40 PDT — *"Feel free to move the governor, by following
the instructions of Roman in Discord"*. Q128 §5's **item 1** is the one item that cannot be answered from a log:
every statement that the governor's cap causes the burst speed gap was read off the governor's own throttle lines,
with the cap never moved. aifoundry3's governor is latched at a boot-time TDP of 0 and aifoundry1's card 1 never
reaches 800 MHz, so the test exists on aifoundry2 or nowhere.

**The first authorized governor move in this project.** Two fields were written and nothing else:
`DM_CMD_SET_MODULE_STATIC_TDP_LEVEL` and `DM_CMD_SET_MODULE_TEMPERATURE_THRESHOLDS`, under the ET-SoC-1 firmware
field manual's G0–G4 gates, with a signed change record committed beside the data
([`tools/claims-v3/knob/CHANGE-RECORD.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/knob/CHANGE-RECORD.md): PASS / FAIL / UNKNOWN per
gate, UNKNOWN never read as PASS). **Pre-registered** in
[`tools/claims-v3/knob/README.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/knob/README.md) (rules K1–K5, the extrapolated 35–85 W cap
table, the abort bounds) and committed as `2d35d1b9` at 14:19:40 PDT — **before the first governor write and before
the first launch**; amendments A1–A5 are dated in their own commits (`4e0522f1`, `8fbc3c7f`, `4f3da00b`, `400cd56b`,
`bd217ece`). **No OTA, flash, PMIC command, card reset, PCIe action, driver reload or clock set at any point**, and
every setting stayed inside the code-enforced bounds (TDP 30–65 W, threshold 60–70 °C). 11.4 card-minutes across
five sessions; 39 DM set/restore commands, all `rc 0`, **zero read-back mismatches**, seven distinct (TDP, threshold)
settings; the originals (65 W / 65 °C) restored and read back in all five, and independently re-read on the card by
another agent at 14:58:09 PDT. Data: [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-e69-governor-knob/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-e69-governor-knob/README.md).

**Results.**
- **The cap moves the burst clock, in the predicted direction and by about the predicted amount.** `a_ones_b_randn`
  reads **764.3 → 698.8 → 604.1 MHz** at caps of 65 → 55 → 45 W (predicted 742.9 → 681.0 → 600.0); `randn`
  **709 → 638.7** at 65 → 55 W (predicted 704.8 → 633.3). **K3 passes as registered: 12 of 12 cells in band**
  (bar 10) with an rms of **11.50 MHz** over the twelve signed cell errors (bar 25), from a table extrapolated from
  65 W measurements alone. The per-run restatement — 22 of 24, rms 13.9 MHz — is secondary; the first write-up led
  with it and that is corrected (A5).
- **The pattern that had never thrown a power step throws them when the cap drops.** `ones` has shown **0 and 0**
  power-down steps at this card's own 65 W cap (as in E64's two runs); at a 45 W cap the same work reads **758 and
  739.6 MHz with 10 and 9 power steps** (733.3 predicted).
- **The sustained clamp is the thermal threshold, and it is now a measured sensitivity.** At the default 65 °C
  threshold sustained `ones` is pinned at a 65.2 °C die, **763.4 MHz, 51.89 W, 11 thermal steps, 0 power steps**; at
  a 70 °C threshold the same work **holds 800.0 MHz for 76 s with 0 thermal steps** (800-share 1.00) at a 64.2 °C
  die and 54.71 W. The 65 → 70 °C sensitivity is therefore **at least 36.6 MHz** of sustained clock — *a lower
  bound*, because the upper cell sits at the 800 MHz VMIN ceiling, with n = 1 per cell, no interval, inside one
  session. K4 passes on the thermal-step count and the 800-share; its 0.0 MHz prediction error is a ceiling
  artefact.
- **Which limiter acts is separately switchable.** At a 50 W cap sustained `ones` holds **769.6 MHz at 49.95 W with
  0 thermal and 11 power steps**, against the control's 11 and 0 — the same clock, the other cause.
- **`zeros` at a 35 W cap did not throttle at all** (800 and 800 MHz, 0 power steps, peaks 32.8 and 34.6 W, under
  the cap): the cell is in band but its mechanism never triggered. A cap near 30 W would, and is not in this
  authorization.

**Scored: K3 and K4 PASS; K1, K2 and K5 FAIL as registered; the registered drift check FAILS.** K1 and K2 fail on
one run of 24 (`BURST-01-tdp65-ones-r1` read 756 MHz with zero throttle events of either kind; the SP ring shows a
governor idle reset 1.5 s into the work, confirmed independently), and A5 records that K1's implementation carries
an undocumented ±5 MHz tolerance and K2 reads "800 MHz" as "≥ 775" — both fail under either reading, so no verdict
turns on them. K5 fails because half of it depended on an unmeasured cell and the other half's clock landed outside
its band. 🛑 **The registered drift check against E64 fails and is reported as a failure, not as a prediction
error:** the sustained control reads **763.4 MHz / 51.89 W against E64's 737.9 / 49.38** at identical nominal
settings and an identical starting die (58 °C), and the burst control shifts the same way (+20 to +26 MHz). Same
pinned die temperature, 2.5 W more carried — about 1.4 °C less rise than E64's own thermal line gives, so a
thermal-path change rather than a governor change; E69 measured no ambient. **Every cross-session comparison with
E64 carries at least that offset**, and no sustained cell in E69 was replicated.

**Not measured, and why.** Both sustained `randn` cells are **unmeasured**: one aborted at 85.2 W board power and
the other — the control, at the card's own defaults — at 85.8 W (amendment A4; A3's first reading is withdrawn). The
85 W bound was *not* raised (field manual rule 11) and `randn` was not retried, so **E69 concludes nothing about the
threshold's effect on `randn`**. The lowered-threshold cell (62 °C) is **inconclusive**: 790.3 MHz against 649.8
predicted, but its last-40 s bins show the die still climbing 59 → 62 °C with the clock at 800 for fourteen bins —
it never reached equilibrium inside 76 s. The same board-power property appears a third time in the return burst at
the restored defaults, which peaked at **86.61 W** with 12 samples at or above 85 W and a longest consecutive breach
of 2, so the registered three-sample abort correctly did not fire; that is the highest board sample of all 29 reduced
runs, and the earlier "peak 84.4 W" was that run's `p800_w`, not a peak (A5).

**Two firmware corrections this run forced**, both in [14-card-behaviour.md](14-card-behaviour.md): the card reports
**release 1.4.0 / BL1–BL2 0.22.0 / PMIC 1.5.0 / minions 0.25.1**, not 1.3.1 / BL2 0.20.0 / 0.23.0 (the `0.20.0` in
`et-lab-manifest` is the host driver's version); and at that build there is **no 1.05 × TDP power-down loop** — the
throttle check is a single pass per DM-task pass, so a TDP of 0 pins the clock at the VMIN floor and writing the TDP
back releases it. The ~1.3 s averaging the power trigger shows is 0.22.0 behaviour (`avg_soc_pwr_mW`), not 0.20.0's
instantaneous reading.

**Side observation, recorded not hidden:** the SP's error-event count went 6 (before the session) → 18 (before the
first set) → 222 (by the last restore) → 234, in 37 bursts following management-command clusters. All 234 are
Level Critical / Desc `SP Runtime Error` / Count 0 / Syndrome "Runtime Error Count Beyond Threshold", so amendment
A2's abort condition was never met.

**What it changes.** Q128 §5 item 1 **closes for the cap and for the threshold raised**: "the cap causes the burst
gap" stops being a reading of the governor's log and becomes a measured response, and "the sustained gap is a heat
clamp" gets its first testable number. **It moves no pJ** between accounted and unaccounted — this is the governor,
not the ledger. It does **not** close the Horace Hub's "temperature term in the governor's power curve": that is a
model-against-measurement gap (775 MHz predicted against 738 measured) and E69's 36.6 MHz is a
measurement-against-measurement sensitivity, numerically close by coincidence, with E69's own 65 °C baseline
separated from "measured 738" by the drift above. The sustained model still lacks a thermal time constant: its one
purely structural prediction (the raised threshold) was exact at the ceiling, and both duty-cycle predictions were
70 and 140 MHz low from the same hole.
**Report:** [From matmul speed to one transistor switch](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor)
(A24, rebuilt 28 October with Q129).

## E70 — hs: the saturation ladder and the A-side multi-bit ladder (Q129; card runs 2026-10-27, 14:08–14:43 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q129):** "Go through Horace's hub and fill in unresolved pieces … For instance, '5. What is still open'
section … reduce the gap between what's observed and what's accounted for, bottom up." Two of that section's items
are card work on one knob each: **item 2**, the saturation ladder (the per-bit price list over-predicts a whole
random word ~2×, and Q128 named two candidate causes without testing either), and **item 4's third point** (no
A-side multi-bit ladder, so the carry-over between operands was a choice between two defensible numbers that moves
the prediction by 6%). **Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/hs/README.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/hs/README.md)
(three candidate laws with a per-rung prediction from each, discriminators D1–D5, predictions P1–P8, decision rules,
departures D-a…D-d, amendments A18 and A19), committed as `bd39b335` at 14:07:07 PDT — **42 s before the card was
claimed** (14:07:49 PDT, from the Discord message's own snowflake) **and 1 min 33 s before the first measured
launch** (14:08:40 PDT). One card: **aifoundry3**, latched at 600 MHz by its boot-time TDP 0, 521.6 mV on every
sample — deliberately E65's and E67's card, clock and rail, so the three price lists add without a conversion.
**Every operand file E60, E65 and E67 ran is byte-identical in the new set** (154 of 154 against `build/hl-gen4`,
87 of 87 against `build/hl-gen3`), and `alq_h1` is byte-identical to E67's `abit_0` by construction.
**No card setting was changed: no clock was set and the governor was not touched.**

**The one knob that separates the two causes.** `lq_h<h>` flips B's **low h** mantissa bits together; `ls_h<h>`
flips **h bits spread** over the 23 mantissa positions. At h = 8 the sum of their constituent single-bit prices is
3.886 W against 12.628 W — a factor of 3.2 — while the Hamming weight is identical. A law in *how many* bits flip
must give the same step for both; a law in *which* bits flip must not. At h = 23 the two masks are the same mask,
so the ladder's top rung is a built-in tie. `alq_h<k>` is the same ladder on the **A** operand (A's low k mantissa
bits, B = 1.0), the first multi-bit measurement on that side.

**Results** (7 passes 790–796, 34.7 min of card time, 203 of 203 runs through every per-run gate; **6 passes kept**
— pass 792 set aside by departure D-a). Data, the registered six-pass report, the seven-pass `reduce_hl.py` output
and three leakage sensitivities: `docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-e70-saturation-ladder/`.
- **It is the wires, not the count.** Over the 15 B-side rungs — **8 `lq` (h = 1…16) + 7 `ls`**, the shared
  `lq_h23` excluded as registered — one saturating curve in the additive sum, `ΔW = W∞(1 − e^(−S/W∞))`, leaves a
  residual rms of **0.232 W**; the best *any* law in h alone can do (a free value per h) is **1.358 W**; summing the
  single-bit prices leaves **6.062 W**. With `W∞` frozen before the run at the value fitted on E60's own lq ladder
  (12.58 W), the same curve leaves 0.595 W. The fitted rungs span ΔW = 0.61 → 9.75 W. The excluded top rung tests
  both curves out of sample, and they part company there: the **refitted** curve misses `lq_h23` by **+1.851 W
  (11.7 SE, 8× its own rms)**, the **frozen** one predicts it to **+0.072 W (0.5 SE)**.
- **D1, the matched-h contrast:** the spread layout costs **3.860 ± 0.052 W** more than the low layout at h = 8 —
  **74 SE** from the zero a "how many" law requires. The contrast rises, peaks at h = 8–12 and falls back toward the
  zero h = 23 forces by construction; no law in h can produce that shape at all.
- **`W∞` is the curve's curvature, not a ceiling** (correction C1, below): the B-side fit gives 10.52 ± 0.19 W
  (per-pass; pooled 10.52) and the A-side fit 6.38 ± 0.07 W (pooled 6.37) — but each ladder's own top rung sits
  *above* its fit's asymptote (`lq_h23` 12.145 ± 0.158 W, +10.3 SE; `alq_h23` 7.560 ± 0.042 W, +28 SE), and so do
  both measured random-operand steps (`nrd_a` 7.209 ± 0.015, `nrd_b` 10.785 ± 0.024 W). The two are not added and
  bound nothing.
- **The A side's saturating form is rejected.** Fit rms 1.063 W (4.6× the B side's), systematic residuals
  (−0.16 … −1.84 … **+1.22 W**, to 84 SE) and increments that **accelerate** (+0.38, +1.53, +2.23 W at k = 8, 12, 16)
  where saturation requires decay. **P5 fails in both clauses**, and its ceiling clause fails against either B value
  (0.51 to the frozen 12.58 W, 0.61 to the refitted 10.52 W). Flip-rate scaling of the ceiling is *consistent with*
  the frozen value (6.29 W predicted against a 6.37 W fit) and **not established**: against the refitted B it
  predicts 5.26 W and misses by 21%. What the A side's real shape is, E70 does not answer.
- **The zeros→random step splits, and that part needs no law:** E67's `anchor_zeros` 27.83 W, E70's own
  constant-operand level 36.40 W (within 0.4 W of E67's) and E67's `anchor_randn` 52.49 W give **8.6 W of lane
  un-gating** plus **16.1 W of operand flips** = 24.66 W. Whether 16.1 W is near a saturation limit is not settled
  by E70.
- **A single turn-on is not enough:** the `T` an offset law would need runs **0.091 → 1.219 W** across the rungs, a
  factor of 13.4, with **10 of 14** rungs more than 3 SE from the 1.351 ± 0.281 W E67's interaction grid measured
  (`T_implied` is undefined at h = 1).
- **The carry-over between operands, measured:** on P6's registered window, h = 12 and 16, ρ (A price per flipped
  bit over B's, at matched h) = **1.316** — ρ(12) = 1.266 ± 0.011 and ρ(16) = 1.365 ± 0.033, whose mean carries a
  **between-h spread of 0.070** (a spread, not an SE: the propagated per-h SE is 0.017). **ρ(12) = 1.266 ± 0.011**
  is the cleanest single number at a random fp32 word's Hamming weight; against the 1.57 and 1.01 Q128 had to
  choose between. (Averaging h = 23 in as well gives 1.293 and is a departure from the registered window.) ρ is a
  mean of per-pass ratios, biased up at low h where the denominator is noisy — the ratio of means reads 2.097 at
  h = 4 against 2.602 — and the h ≥ 8 window is unaffected. **The price per flipped bit is not a function of h** in
  either layout: it falls 132 → 82 then climbs to 115 fJ in the low layout (each rung adds a dearer bit, which
  cancels the saturation) and falls monotonically 227 → 133 fJ in the spread one. The quantity that behaves is
  measured/additive, one function of the additive sum in all three ladders. (Those fJ figures divide **board**
  watts by a per-operand-tile bit count and a per-minion rate; they are a within-experiment currency and must not
  be carried into Q128's gate-level per-flip ledger until that scope is settled.)
- **The closure E67 failed** (0.43 and 0.50) reads **0.85** (`nrd_a`) and **1.17** (`nrd_b`) with the law D2 selects
  (refitted `W∞`), 0.77 and 1.04 with `W∞` frozen at 12.58 W, and 1.21 and 1.17 with each side's own fit — all six
  inside P7's [0.7, 1.4].
- **Departure D-a earned its place on the first run.** Pass 792's four constant-operand runs span 0.489 W against the
  registered 0.45, while its `anchor_pi` drift is only +0.110 W — E67's registered gate would have kept it. That is
  the failure mode A15 described, caught by the gate written for it.
- **Departure D-b saw nothing to report, which is itself the result:** the host's 1-minute load average stayed
  0.72–1.32 against the registered 4.0 and the measured op rate held at 1.1208–1.1210 Gop/s per minion across all
  seven passes, so E67's A16 disturbance did not recur.
- **The replicate holds.** E60's seven `lq` rungs on this card, a week earlier and from a different operand-set
  directory, reproduce within **0.207 W** (and `lq_h23` within 0.116 W).

**Scored: P1, P2, P3, P4, P6, P7, P8 PASS; P5 FAIL.** P3 is scored with the metric the registration actually names
— a **partial** relation, "the two Spearman ρ of the residuals of a single-variable fit" — which gives
ρ(resid-after-S, h) = −0.149 against ρ(resid-after-h, S) = −0.229, so its second clause holds and the rule passes;
residualising in rank space instead reverses that clause (−0.250 vs −0.075) and the registration does not name the
space, so both are published. **E70 as first committed scored P3 by comparing two univariate ρ (−0.804 vs −0.836)
and reported FAIL under a sentence claiming it had been scored as registered — that was wrong and is corrected
here (C5).** P5 fails in both clauses: its bend metric compared the A side's per-position price at k = 23 with
k = 1, and because the A-side single-bit prices rise steeply with position the low-k mask picks up dear bits as k
grows and that price *rises*, even though the ladder's measured/additive ratio falls 0.615 → 0.230; and the A fit
is outside 1.5× of the B value whichever B value is used. That failure is not reinterpreted, and it does not
establish the registered alternative either.

**Corrections, 2026-10-27, desk-only (C1–C11).** A second agent checked E70 line by line against `runs.csv` and
the registration commit and found eleven faults; all are fixed in the data directory's README, `report.md`,
`scored.json` and `report_e70.py`, with no card touched and nothing re-measured (the pre-correction script
reproduces the first published outputs byte-for-byte). The three that changed public claims: **C1**, the fitted
`W∞` were called ceilings, summed into a "16.90 ± 0.20 W cap" and read as "random data sits at 95% of the cap" —
**withdrawn**, since the data exceed both asymptotes and the percentage tracked whichever B value was chosen;
**C2**, a ratio computed against the frozen B ceiling was printed beside the refitted one, which is what the
flip-rate claim rested on; **C5**, P3 above. The rest correct the fit set's description (8 + 7 = 15, not 9 + 7),
the fitted span (0.61–9.75 W, not 0.6–12.1), a between-h SD presented as an interval, a mean-of-ratios bias, "10 of
15" → "10 of 14", P7's law, P2's rung count, and the fJ denominator's scope. **Unchanged by all of it:** the
verdict on Q128 §5 item 2, D1, the D2 residual table, every per-rung ΔW and SE, the gates and the replicate.

**What it changes.** Q128 §5 item 2 is closed **on the B side** with a cause and a one-parameter law; the A side's
shape is not described by that law and stays open. Item 4's third point is closed with a measured carry-over in
place of a 6% choice. **It moves no pJ in the bottom-up gate-level ledger** — it is a card-side measurement — but
it removes the 2× discrepancy that made the measured price list unusable as the bridge between the card and that
ledger.
**Report:** [From matmul speed to one transistor switch](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor)
(A24, rebuilt 28 October with Q129): section 3's ledger carries E70's carry-over and section 5 its verdict on
item 2.

## E71 — idle: aifoundry3's idle leakage slope, in the band E65 and E67 ran in (Q129; card runs 2026-10-27, 15:04–16:33 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q129):** Q128 §5's **item 3**. Every per-flipped-bit price is corrected for die temperature with a slope;
the registered estimator read **1.508 W/°C**, which E66 showed is a busy transient fitted inside each run's own 2 s
window. E66 measured aifoundry2's real idle slope (0.2936 W/°C over 51–58 °C), but E65 and E67 ran on **aifoundry3**,
whose idle slope had never been measured. **Pre-registered** as `8fab3404` (predictions S1–S6, E66's identification
rules reused unchanged, the predicted sensitivities, `idle_slope.py`'s four default-preserving options, departure D1)
— pushed before the first launch. **No governor setting was read or written, no clock set, no reset, no driver or
PCIe action**: the card stayed on its boot TDP-0 pin at 600 MHz / 523 mV. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-e71-idle-slope-aifoundry3/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-e71-idle-slope-aifoundry3/README.md).

**Results** (six heat-and-cool cycles p21–p26, all `ok`, the card lock released between passes).
- **aifoundry3's idle leakage slope over 58–62 °C is 0.3803 ± 0.0036 W/°C** — the median of six cycles (mean 0.3804,
  sd 0.0036, range 0.3749–0.3854; 95% t ±0.0038, 99% t ±0.0060; 791–856 admissible samples per cycle, rms
  0.167–0.184 W). The band is the one E65 and E67 launched in (every admissible run at 58.0 °C, windows to 62.4 °C).
- **It replicates September.** E44's three idle cycles on this card, re-fitted with the same tool, give **0.3799
  W/°C** (sd 0.0006) — 0.0004 W/°C apart after a month. Disclosed in the registration, so S1 was a replication test,
  not a blind one.
- 🛑 **It is the band, not the card.** Re-fitting aifoundry2's 18 E66 passes with the same tool: **0.2936** over
  51–58 °C (reproducing E66's published line exactly) and **0.3452 ± 0.0056** over 58–62 °C. aifoundry3: 0.3200
  (52–58), 0.3276 (54–58), **0.3803** (58–62), 0.3957 (58–65). Of the +0.0867 W/°C between E66's figure and E71's,
  **+0.0516 (60%) is the band and +0.0351 (40%) is the card**. E66's 0.2936 is 77% of the slope E65 and E67 needed,
  and **must not be reused as aifoundry3's sensitivity**.
- **The published idle law gets the band dependence right and the level wrong by under 10%**: mean slope 0.3186
  W/°C over 51–58 and 0.3708 over 58–62, so evaluating that law over the right band beats any single constant.
- **Prices move a few per cent, and systematically.** E65 blocks A+B: mean −0.059 W, max 0.172 W (`bpi_22`), bits
  0–9 −5.2%, bits 10–22 −2.9%, exponent −4.6%, sign −0.7%, span 12.15× → 12.81×. E67 registered: mean −0.070 W, max
  0.234 W (`nrd_b`); clean: max relative −8.97% (`abit_0`). Restated as energies: E65's flat mantissa floor
  **109 → ~103 fJ**, bit 22 1008 → 986, exponent 174 → 166, sign 123 → 122. 22 of 37 E65 prices move by more than
  their own published SE, 11 by more than 2 SE — the mechanism was registered in advance and confirmed (an item's
  window runs warmer than its own zero's, so a larger slope subtracts more from the item than from its zero).
- **The one number that moves materially is E65's D1**, itself a temperature coefficient: with one measured slope
  for both arms it reads **+32.4 ± 39.6 mW/°C (+0.71 ± 0.87 %/°C)** instead of +22.0 ± 36.1 (+0.47 ± 0.78). Verdict
  unchanged: consistent, undecided.
- **E67's 2× over-prediction is untouched** (A-side closure 0.433 → 0.443, B-side 0.499 → 0.509), because both
  sides of the ratio move together — so E70's verdict on it stands.

**Scored: S1–S6 all PASS**, S5 narrowly (max relative price shift −8.97% against a registered ≤ 10%).
`leak_sensitivity.py` reproduced every published price **exactly** (0.000e+00 W) at each reduction's own auto slope
before re-pricing, and no kept-pass set changes anywhere. **Four dated departures:** a 420 s cooling (registered via
E66's amendment A3) instead of aifoundry3's registered 2,700 s, which leaves 51–58 °C uncovered and floors the
cooling at 52–53 °C; `hl/block.sh`'s own departure D1 for the box's logged-in users; the heater reached 82–84 °C
rather than the registered 90 °C target, stopping on the 150-burst cap in all six cycles (the cap *is* the rule);
and six cycles rather than more, the driver's deadline stopping it after p26 against a registered minimum of five.
**Two text corrections** followed in `0b6e81da` (an arithmetic error in the idle-law derivative comparison, an
unsupported session count); no measured value, verdict or sensitivity changed.

**What it changes.** Q128 §5 item 3 **closes for the second card and for the band each experiment ran in**, and the
1.508 W/°C the registered estimator applied is **4–5× every measured idle slope**. **It moves 0.00 pJ** in the
waterfall, which rests on E38's registered means, but it moves the *measured* side of the comparison a few per cent
**toward** the open-netlist figure. New caution, larger than the card-to-card difference it is usually invoked for:
**a leakage slope is not a card constant** — it rises about 0.009 W/°C per °C of die temperature on aifoundry2 and
0.011 on aifoundry3 over 51–65 °C, so a correction quoted without its band is ambiguous at the 20–30% level.
**Report:** [From matmul speed to one transistor switch](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor)
(A24, rebuilt 28 October with Q129); the full re-reduction of every aifoundry3 experiment at this slope is
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-leak-sensitivity/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-leak-sensitivity/README.md)
(`9155fabc`): E60's rows 116.746 → 115.049 fJ (`lq`) and 129.918 → 127.687 (`lb`), E65's flow-M flip 745 ± 56 →
**722 ± 61 fJ**, E67's grid 8/16 → 9/16 cells within 20%, E70's `W∞` refit 10.52 → 10.16 W and its carry-over
ρ(12) 1.266 → **1.246 ± 0.014** — and **not one of E67's or E70's sixteen registered verdicts moves**, with Q128's
whole 1.946 pJ bottom-up ledger byte-identical (275 other numbers move).

## E72 — lq6: the same card at 600 MHz, pinned by a TDP of 0 (Q129; card runs 2026-10-27, 14:59–15:24 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q129):** the Horace Hub's Levels tab wanted "a second card at 600 MHz", because every comparison of a
flipped bit's price across clocks had the card and the clock changing together. aifoundry2 has never run below
800 MHz under load. **Pre-registered** as `001811ab`, committed and pushed at 14:58:51 PDT — **before the first card
access at 14:59:39** — and byte-identical since; the G0 gates were filled and committed as `20c4249f` at 15:01:22,
**ten seconds before the one write**. A governor move under the field manual's gates, change record in
[`tools/claims-v3/lq6/CHANGE-RECORD.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/lq6/CHANGE-RECORD.md): **one field, two commands**
