(`-l 0` at 15:01:32.038, `-l 65` at 15:24:07.531), both `rc 0`, zero read-back mismatches, the temperature threshold
**never written**, and no clock ever set directly. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-e72-600mhz/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-e72-600mhz/README.md).

**Results.**
- **The pin holds exactly.** 125 of 125 kept runs and 215 of 215 smoke samples at **exactly 600 MHz**, 517.9 mV
  (517–519 mV over all runs), zero clock-gate failures, zero passes set aside. After the restore the card stepped
  **600 → 700 → 800 MHz inside one 6 s launch** (114 / 2 / 98 samples, 619 mV, 59.7 W) — no reset.
- **A flipped mantissa bit costs 79.18 ± 3.09 fJ at 600 MHz** against this same card's **121.27 ± 0.61 fJ** at
  800 MHz (the `lq` h2→h8 secant at the board). **V1 passes as registered** (prediction 85.69, band 77.12–94.26).
- 🛑 **But the same test at matched leakage slopes FAILS at three of four slopes.** The registered reduction leaks a
  different slope into each side of the ratio. Re-run with the prediction moved onto the measurement's slope on a
  **fixed** pass set: slope 0.000 → −8.9% (PASS), 0.098 → **−10.2% FAIL**, 0.2936 → **−12.9% FAIL**, 0.4420 →
  **−15.0% FAIL**. The honest same-card reading is that the 600 MHz board price is **10–15% below a pure V²
  extrapolation**, which is also what V3 reports.
- 🛑 **The exponent is bracketed, not pinned at 2.** In `(V600/V800)^n`: board **2.43 ± 0.22** (registered, mixed
  slope) to **2.92 ± 0.21**; rail **1.60 ± 0.32** to **1.72 ± 0.31**. n = 2 is inside 95% only in the registered
  board row and the rail rows, and **outside in all four matched board rows** (2.1–4.4 SE). **What survives
  everywhere:** the clock-scaling alternative **n = 3.637 is excluded by every row, board and rail, at 3.4 SE or
  more** (minimum over ten rows 3.41 SE). Quote E72 as *"the price follows the voltage, not the clock"*, never as
  "the exponent is 2".
- 🛑 **"The rail is slope-free, quote the rail" is WITHDRAWN.** The rail is merely *uncorrected*: at the same assumed
  slopes it moves 16% where the board moves 10% (ratio 0.7547 → 0.6906), its window is 0.575 °C **hotter** than the
  board's on E60 and 0.028 °C cooler on E72 — asymmetric between the two sides of the ratio — and its own
  within-run power-against-temperature fit is 5.52 W/°C (E60) and 1.11 (E72), a launch transient rather than
  leakage, so it cannot be corrected from this data. Anyone quoting either number must name its slope and its
  window.
- **The second card at 600 MHz closes:** aifoundry2 **113.22 ± 0.47 fJ** against aifoundry3's 116.75 ± 0.61 =
  **0.970** (rail 0.972, busy 0.966), at 0.5179 V against 0.5218 V, every rung inside 0.90–1.09. The full h ≥ 1
  `lq` ladder and the whole busy `lb` ladder now exist on this card, and its bend (1.430 against aifoundry3's 1.360)
  says again that a single-bit price is a **local slope**.
- **The VMIN table has exactly three minion points** — 600, 700, 800 MHz — published with its evidence
  (`vmin-lut.json`: a 256-byte binary of 14 × 18-byte points, 11 all-zero, three independent parses).
- **The whole leakage sensitivity rests on 0.53 °C.** The per-pass `t_h8 − t_h2` is +0.85, 0.00, +0.80, +0.05,
  +0.95 °C from a sensor that reports whole degrees, and the five per-pass secants spread 71.9–90.0 fJ around 79.18.

**Scored: six of nine registered rules PASS in full (V1, V2, V4, V5, V6, G2); V3 FAILS as registered; G1 and G3 are
PARTIAL.** V3 fails on one of its two scored rungs (`lq_h2` 0.913 W against 1.040 predicted, 12.2% low on a ±10%
band; `lq_h8` 3.094 against 3.398, inside). G1 and G3 are partial for the same reason: their clock halves are
measured, their **governor-log halves are not**, because `hl/block.sh` does not dump the service processor's trace
ring and a per-run dump was not in the registered protocol. The first write-up reported "seven of eight scorable
rules pass" and scored G3 as PASS with its registered "at least one Power throttle up event" clause dropped; both are
corrected in `56cd6e50` (desk-only, no card touched; `rematch_e72.py --selftest` reproduces 12 of 12 published
reduction figures exactly). **The committed read-back is `gov.jsonl`'s `back_check` at 15:24:31.194**; a further
`ettelem config` at 15:24:48 and the module uptime were printed and **not retained**, so an independent reviewer's
read under the card lock at 15:46:51 PDT stands in their place and `handoff-evidence.json` states which is which.

**What it changes.** **No pJ moves.** What moves is the confidence the bit-price rung may claim: the cross-card
reading is replaced by a same-card measurement, the rival "it scaled with the clock too" is refuted everywhere, and
the exponent is bracketed rather than measured. It also adds a caution to the board-to-rail share: the
board/rail bracket around V² is **partly an estimator asymmetry**, so that share cannot be read off E72's two ratios
alone. The reusable firmware findings are in [14-card-behaviour.md](14-card-behaviour.md).
**Report:** [From matmul speed to one transistor switch](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor)
(A24, rebuilt 28 October with Q129).

## E73 — hlf: the camera's heat path at two more lock-in frequencies (Q129; card runs 2026-10-27, 16:0x–16:4x PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q129):** the Horace Hub's Levels tab listed "the camera's heat-path time constants (a further lock-in
frequency)" as missing — E63 ran 0.05 Hz and Q126 added a 0.15 Hz point, which is not enough to tell one thermal
pole from two. **Pre-registered** in `tools/claims-v3/hlf/` (rules R1–R6, two rival heat-path models frozen with
their predicted group delays, the registered phase discriminator at 0.01 Hz). Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-e73-camera-frequencies/`](../reports/data/2026-10-27-e73-camera-frequencies/README.md)
(`0953f14b`, corrected in `4baa3e6f`). **No governor, clock, reset, driver or PCIe action**; aifoundry2's governor
stayed where E72 had restored it.

**The run.** 248 launches in 5 blocks, **43.4 min of card time** inside a 52 card-minute cap, `stop_reason done`;
25,004 telemetry samples; camera 65,004 packets at 25.0006 fps with **0 gaps and 0 reconnects**, 287 shutter events
(6.6/min, nothing bridged). The lane's first session died on account quota at 16:45 while copying data off the host;
the data was recovered on 28 October with no further card access, and the registered reduction re-run on the box
with the **committed** `lockin_hlf.py` reproduced `lockin.json` byte-identically (sha256 `d29e801f263fa42a…`), with
`fit.txt` and all five `maps.npz` identical.

**Results** (the corrected numbers; use only these).
- **H(0.01 Hz) = 145.2 ± 16.5 mK/W at a lag of 59.4 ± 4.3°** (ROI 322.8 ± 20.6 mK = 15.7 SE; sign-flip p = 0.0029),
  and **H(0.025 Hz) = 88.7 ± 11.0 mK/W at 75.6 ± 7.8°**. The 0.05 Hz reference line reads **58.58 ± 3.55 mK/W** with
  a constant 98.60° phase and a worst residual of 0.45 SE (R2 PASS). Ratios to that line: 1.396 ± 0.255 at
  0.025 Hz and 2.610 ± 0.391 at 0.01 Hz (R3 PASS).
- **The heat path is one 16.0 s pole plus about a second of extra lag:** τ = **15.98 s [13.34, 19.12]**, delay
  **1.009 s [0.78, 1.24]**, zero-frequency gain **250.5 mK/W [217, 292]**, χ² 21.67/25; **group delay 16.99 s
  [14.47, 20.13]**. **One pole alone is excluded** (ΔAIC **+17.02**, Δχ² 19.02 for one extra parameter). Whether the
  extra second is a transport delay or a 1.0 s second pole is **undecided** (ΔAIC 1.48).
- 🛑 **Both pre-registered rivals are refuted by the phase**, which is what R4 was built to do: the measured lag
  59.364 ± 4.315° against M1's 42.190° (**3.98 SE**) and M2's 26.409° (**7.64 SE**); the magnitude separates nothing
  (2.610 ± 0.391 against 3.142 and 2.252, 1.36 and 0.91 SE). R4 is **reported with both models refuted**, not
  passed.
- **At 0.05 Hz the response is 0.1953 of the steady one** (63% of a step in 17.0 s, 95% in 49 s) — so a reader must
  say **which** steady gain is meant: the fit's **G0 = 250.5 mK/W**, whose own 0.05 Hz value (48.9) sits **2.71
  line-SE (16.5%) below** the measured line, **or** the **~300 mK/W** that the measured line implies through the
  fitted attenuation (~270 from E63's anchor). The 17% between them is itself a finding.
- **The camera's gain drift is not established by this session:** −44.1 ± 34.9 %/h is 1.3 σ, though it has the sign
  of E63's measured 59 → 42 mK/W. So a reference block minutes away stays mandatory for every absolute mK/W.
- **R6 (the hold) passes:** in-launch samples at ≥ 790 MHz are 100 / 99.6 / 99.4 / 99.7 / 97.7% per block, die means
  55.1–56.9 °C, max 58.3 against a 65 °C bar; 9 of 248 launches carried the one-second idle-reset dip, 8 of them on
  the zero arm — counted, no verdict, as registered.
- **How many frequencies:** 25 published points at **fourteen** distinct frequencies; 14 points at ten frequencies
  clear the board ≥ 5 SE inclusion rule and enter the fit. "Nine" is the design table's count up to 0.15 Hz, not the
  fit's.

**Scored: R1, R2, R3, R5, R6 PASS; R4 REPORTED with both models refuted.** 🛑 **A correction that re-issued the whole
table** (`4baa3e6f`): the frozen fit's χ² was **half** the proper one, because `se_H` is a total complex SE that the
frozen script weighted as if per-component, so every interval and both ΔAIC headlines are re-issued from
`posthoc/fit_sigma.py` — one pole alone loses by ΔAIC 17.02 where the frozen script printed 7.51. The point values
are identical; only the intervals and χ² change, and at the frozen σ the new exact gain profile returns the published
interval, so the move to [217, 292] is the σ fix alone. **The frozen outputs are left byte-reproducible on purpose**:
`analysis/fit.txt` and `fit.json` still print half the χ² and ~√2-wide intervals, the corrected table lives in
`posthoc/sigma-corrected.json`, and anyone reading `fit.json` directly must read the CORRECTION box first.

**What it changes.** **Zero pJ moves** — E73 measures the heat path from the die to the back of the board, not the
price of a flip. It **closes** the Levels tab's "heat-path time constants" item and **opens a sharper one**: the
fitted family is strained in two places, the 0.01 Hz lag (2.46 SE in the lag alone; complex residual 2.39 against a
null of √2) and the 0.05 Hz magnitude (2.71 line-SE low), both pointing at a third, longer pole or a diffusive path
— **suggested, not established**. Settling it needs a frequency **below 0.01 Hz** (a half period of 10 launches is
200 s; E73's 0.01 Hz block alone cost 16.7 min of card time for 11 laps). The chain's heat-path sentence reads "a
16 s pole and about a second of extra lag", not "two poles".
**Report:** the committed README is the deliverable; the reduced cube (`frames.npz`, 191.9 MB) and the raw stream
(2.85 GB) stay on the host, with all 535 files' sha256 in `left-on-aifoundry2.sha256`.

## E74 — rf: what the register file's own cells cost (Q129; card runs 2026-10-28, 11:14–11:57 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q129):** the Horace Hub's Levels tab, stop 4 — "what the register file's own cells cost" — and Q128 §2's
list of terms the one-lane bottom-up ledger does not reach. **Pre-registered** as `2be3f07e` and `547f4ae3` (rules
P1–P6, rule 5 publishing a **bound** if the board fit does not resolve the slope, rule 6 fixing the 64-bits-per-MAC
basis, the minion rail registered as a figure **reported beside the board and never instead of it**), both confirmed
on `origin/main` with `git merge-base --is-ancestor` **before the first launch**. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-28-e74-register-file-cells/`](../reports/data/2026-10-28-e74-register-file-cells/README.md)
(`3d9e33d4`, corrected in `e0f8ecf8` and `1b0391b7`). **The governor was never touched** (aifoundry3 stayed on its
boot TDP 0, 600 MHz / 522 mV; the clock-guard line reads `600 400 0` in the first and last pass manifest of the day,
`boot_id` unchanged), no reset, no driver reload, no PCIe retrain, no firmware or PMIC command.

**Results** (6 passes, 11.6 card-minutes; the first pass set aside on anchor drift).
- 🛑 **The board does not resolve it, so the result is a bound: e_cell < 2.73 fJ per flipped cell bit at 95%.** The
  point estimate is **0.855 fJ, 95% t [−1.02, +2.73]** over five kept passes, where the registration expected
  ±0.23 fJ and got ±1.87. A die-temperature covariate does not resolve it either (**1.359 fJ, SE 0.81, 95%
  [−0.31, +3.03]**; 1.383 under pass fixed effects).
- **Only the minion rail arm excludes zero: 1.643 fJ, 95% [0.704, 2.583]** — four times tighter on the same runs
  (SE 1.6e-4 against the board's 3.1e-4 W per bit per op).
- **Per multiply-add the cells are 0.055 pJ at the point and at most 0.175 pJ at 95%** (64 bits of register content,
  the registered basis) — **1.4% of Q128's 3.837 pJ remainder, at most 4.6%**. The useful part is the ceiling: the
  cells **cannot** be the explanation.
- **Both falsification tests pass.** The null arm's slope is 0.036× the real one with an interval spanning zero
  (P4), and `ops_per_cycle_per_hart` is 0.7574 on all 36 `rf` runs and 0.7356 on all 18 `rfz` runs with min = max
  (P5) — so **E61's 3% issue-rate confound is gone by construction**.
- **On E61:** its 1.267 ± 0.151 fJ sits inside every interval here, but the board arm **fails to contradict E61
  rather than confirming it**, since [−1.02, +2.73] contains 0 and 0.2 as readily as 1.27.
- **The leverage is disclosed, not hidden.** Per-pass `e_cell` is 0.576, 3.405, 0.822, −0.163, −0.365 fJ. Dropping
  p7411 (residual rms 243 mW, 7.6× E60) gives 0.218 fJ and a **1.13 fJ** bound instead of 2.73, and inside that pass
  the leverage is one reading (`rf_k96` 31.583 W against 32.188–32.327 W elsewhere). **Keeping it is the conservative
  direction**, because it raises the published bound. Putting the set-aside pass back: 0.850 fJ [−0.57, +2.27].

**Scored: P1 NOT RESOLVED (a bound), P2 FAIL (span +48.9 mW against a registered 60–134), P3 FAIL (residual rms
83.4 mW against ≤ 20; the curvature clause passes at −1.8 σ), P4 PASS, P5 PASS, P6 6.28 fF inside 5.9–13.2 but it
inherits P1.** Resolving `e_cell` on the board needs about **40 passes (~80 card-minutes)** from the measured
between-pass SE, or a re-registration with the **rail** as the estimator and the board as the check.

**What it changes.** Levels stop 4 moves from missing to **bounded on the board, resolved on the rail, consistent
with E61**, with the cost of resolving it stated. **It adds no row to the bottom-up ledger**, because the section-3
transport row already models those cells and counting them again would double-count. It **narrows** that row's
caveat without removing it: in the zeros stimulus the cells' stored bits do not change at all, yet the transport
model still charges 0.654 pJ per MAC where the whole board costs 0.27 — so the over-pricing lives in the **read
ports, the read mux tree and the clocking**, which E74 does not price. That is the single most valuable measurement
left in the waterfall.
**Report:** [From matmul speed to one transistor switch](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor)
(A24, rebuilt 28 October with Q129; its kernels block is read whole from `rf.json`).

## E75 — mf: a flip ladder on the memory path (Q129; card runs 2026-10-28, 11:14–11:57 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q129):** Q128 §5's **item 5**, first half — no flip ladder had ever been run on a memory path, although a
DRAM tensor load costs 94–134 pJ per byte against a multiply-add's 5.8 pJ (the resident loop's; dated note, 1 Nov 2026, E101). **Pre-registered** as `2be3f07e` /
`547f4ae3` (rules P1–P7, two designed counts per image — changed bits `k` and carried ones `w` — at four levels).
Data: [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-28-e75-memory-flip-ladder/`](../reports/data/2026-10-28-e75-memory-flip-ladder/README.md)
(`4fa0bf81`, corrected in `e0f8ecf8` and `1b0391b7`). **Governor untouched**, as E74.

**Results** (5 passes, 21.5 card-minutes; the first pass set aside on anchor drift; ± figures are SEs of the pass
mean, brackets 95% t at df 3).
- **Per changed bit, two levels resolved and two not.** **L1 0.0492 pJ [0.0482, 0.0501]** and **L2 0.274
  [0.183, 0.366]** are coefficients. 🛑 **L3 and DRAM are NOT RESOLVED** — their `k`-arms are **non-monotone in every
  kept pass** (DRAM reads 34.302, 34.239, 34.094, 34.063, 34.574 W over k = 0…512, so the whole positive slope rests
  on the top rung), with registered small-k slopes of −0.47 and −0.32 pJ/bit. **Quote them as bounds: < 0.37 pJ and
  < 0.42 pJ at 95%**, DRAM's also correction-dependent (0.323 / 0.181 / 0.135 across the three leakage settings).
  Bounds are rounded **away** from zero so rounding never understates a ceiling. L2's and L3's are the first
  data-dependence figures of any kind for those levels.
- **Per carried one, the first such prices: nothing at L1** (−0.0001 pJ, 95% [−0.0014, +0.0011]) **and 3.255 pJ at
  DRAM** [2.97, 3.55]. Of the **1,008 pJ per 64 B line** the two designed counts explain, **94% is the ones term**
  (946 pJ) and 62 pJ the unresolved changed-bit term. The point ratio is 10.1×, but its denominator is unresolved —
  **quote the 94% share, never a clean factor of ten**.
- **The chord reproduces the published figure on-chip and overshoots it off-chip:** L1 1.778 W = **0.1489 pJ/B**
  against the catalogue's 0.15; DRAM 3.250 W = **43.3 pJ per line byte** against 38 (**+14%**), per *line* byte
  because that is the basis the registration fixed.
- 🛑 **Bit-counting is nearly complete on-chip and two thirds incomplete off it:** the two arms explain **98.3%** of
  L1's zeros-to-random chord (error −1.7%, 95% [−6.4, +3.0]) and only **36.6%** of DRAM's (95% [30.0, 43.3]).

**Scored: P1 PASS; P2 FAIL (−0.32 against a registered 6–25 pJ/bit); P3 FAIL / not resolved (saturation −120%, and
both the DRAM and L3 k-arms non-monotone in every pass); P4 PASS at L1 on both bases and at DRAM PASS on the
registered basis (−3.06%, 95% [−3.14, −2.98], of the random anchor) while failing only on the stricter chord basis
the reducer substituted on the day — so P4-at-DRAM is NOT a pre-registered failure; P5 FAIL (1,008 against
2,432 ± 600 pJ/line); P6 PASS on the order ONLY as L1 < {L2, L3, DRAM} (the upper three overlap at 95%) and FAIL on
the span (6.6× not 100×); P7 PASS (L1 11.94 TB/s in 10.9–18.1; DRAM 75.07 GB/s of 64 B lines in 57–95; worst
`took_ms` 22 ≤ 60).** Departure D-M3 is dated: P4 is now scored on **both** bases, because the registration stated
the percentage without its denominator.

**What it changes.** **It moves nothing in the multiply-add waterfall** — that is arithmetic — and closes the first
half of §5 item 5 with coefficients where the data resolve them and bounds where it does not. What it changes is
**what the 94–134 pJ/B DRAM figure means**: the data-dependent part is **carried ones**, so reducing transitions is
the small lever off-chip and **packing zeros is the big one**. Newly open, with no experiment number yet: a third
image at the same `k` and `w` but a longer period, to test whether a two-value alternating image is compressible on
the link or in DRAM in a way random data is not. The scratchpad rung stays out of scope for the registered reason
(no host fill reaches the scratchpad address).
**Report:** [From matmul speed to one transistor switch](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor)
(A24, rebuilt 28 October with Q129; its kernels block is read whole from `mf.json`).

## E76 — tr: the thirteen instructions that trap, and what they cost (Q129; source read 2026-10-27, card probe 2026-10-28, 11:14–11:57 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q129):** Q128 §5's **item 5**, second half — thirteen instructions (fdiv, fsqrt, frsq, fsin, the integer
divides…) trap in U-mode and had no energy figure. **Pre-registered** as `2be3f07e` / `547f4ae3` (a probe stage that
gates a price stage: the price stage launches only for a mode the probe clears). Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-28-e76-trapped-instructions/`](../reports/data/2026-10-28-e76-trapped-instructions/README.md)
(`737841fe`, corrected in `e0f8ecf8` and `1b0391b7`). **Governor untouched**, as E74.

**The central question was answered from the firmware source before any card ran: there is no emulation path.** A
U-mode illegal instruction is delegated M → S, and the handler calls `return_from_kernel(KERNEL_RETURN_EXCEPTION)`
— **758 instructions on the installed build** — which **ends that hart's kernel**. So the thirteen have no
per-instruction energy at all: what they cost is what their substitutes cost. The library audit that follows from
it: libgcc's `__divsf3` (299 instructions) contains **none** of the thirteen, so **a C float divide is safe**, at
310–570 multiply-adds; libm's `sqrtf` and `fmodf` and libgcc's `__fixunssfdi` **do** contain them, so **`sqrtf` is
fatal** to the kernel that calls it.

**Confirmed on silicon** (2 probe passes, 84 probe runs, 7.7 card-minutes).
- **13 of 13 trapping modes end the kernel**: `ok:false` with **0 ops** and host exit 1, unanimous over both
  passes, and the `trx_` modes agree with their `tr.` counterparts (P1, P2 PASS).
- **7 of 7 controls return normally** with 65,536 ops (64 per hart × 1,024).
- **No lasting harm** (P4 PASS): 33 runs after a trapping launch, 0 not-ok; both passes `ok`; after thirteen
  kernel-killing launches the drift anchor still records its ten; `et-who` clean.
- **P3 FAILS, low rather than high:** the trap's wall-clock excess is a median **0.45 ms** (range 0.10–0.70, n = 26)
  against a registered 0.5–50 ms.
- **The price stage and the L strand did not run — by the registered rule, not against it:** they launch only for a
  mode the probe clears, and the probe cleared none.

**Not closed:** the static stage was not re-run on aifoundry3, so the instruction counts quoted are the
registration's, taken on aifoundry1 on 27 October (both boxes run the same driver and `srcversion`, and running
`objdump` on a card host during a measurement would have split passes). Cheap to close later, with no card.

**What it changes.** §5 item 5's second half closes **structurally**: there is no row to add to the waterfall,
because the hardware never executes these instructions. **It moves no pJ.**
**Report:** [From matmul speed to one transistor switch](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor)
(A24, rebuilt 28 October with Q129; its kernels block is read whole from `tr.json`).

**Lane lessons from E74–E76, recorded for the next registration.** In **both** E74 and E75 the pass set aside was
the **first** one, on anchor drift (−0.39 W and −0.55 W against +0.07 to +0.23 afterwards): a card coming off idle
has one transient pass in it, so budget n + 1 or queue a throwaway. A **fixed** idle leakage slope is not a safe
correction for a power-dependent ladder on this card — it doubled E74's `e_cell` and broke a null arm that has no
signal in it. The harness's `runitems.py --list` card-minute figures are a **4–6× ceiling** (41 card-minutes against
a priced 256), which is what had made E76 look unaffordable; `--list` charges 50 s of approach per run where the card
needs 8.5 s. And a test whose basis is only implied ("within ±15% of the ladder") gets re-specified on the day:
**registrations should state the denominator of every percentage test.** Harness faults F1–F4 still want an
amendment, chiefly that `BUILD-HASHES-2026-10-27.md`'s cross-box "code" rule is unsatisfiable for the host binary
(each box statically links its own device layer) and that `mf/block.sh` and `tr/block.sh` dropped `rf/block.sh`'s
`ET_DEVICES` preflight, which is latent on two-card hosts.

## E77 — the rail launched and read at one temperature, at two rail voltages (Q129's reservation, run under Q133's governor moves; card runs 2026-11-01, 17:26–17:59 PST, aifoundry2) — pre-registered; a governor move; registered FAIL, provisional

Reserved by Q129 for a run launched and read at **one** die temperature, which would give the rail-to-board step
without a leakage correction and split the regulators' 0.875 pJ row; it needs a governor move under the firmware field
manual's gates. **Q130 moved no governor and did not register or run it**, so the number stays reserved and E78
follows E76.

**Dated note, Sun 1 Nov 2026 (week 44), Q133:** E77 was registered (`33b3be77`, amendments `c1beebb8` and `b8db7e92`) and
run on aifoundry2, 17:26–17:59 PST, as one of Q133's governor moves under the owner's OK of that day (the GO `724a5473`;
TDP 65 → 0 → 65, read back each time, no reset; data and README `be8ec992` and `cb3f1475`,
[`2026-11-01-e77-rail-one-temperature/`](../reports/data/2026-11-01-e77-rail-one-temperature/README.md)). Its corrections
after verification were under way when Q133's records were written, so its result is not recorded here; an addendum records it.

**Recorded Sun 1 Nov 2026 (week 44) by Q134's records, after its verification and corrections.** **Asked:** the
rail-to-board step at one launch temperature, to split the rail ledger's 0.875 pJ row ("the regulators' delivery loss plus
logic in step with the rails") into the regulators' conduction loss and the rest (the Q129 rail ledger; the Horace hub's
Levels stop 2; the final critic's item 13, "not touched"). Its README's "section 11 item 13" is the final critic's item 13,
not the kinds page's (E120's README). **Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/e77/`](../../tools/claims-v3/e77/) and the data
README's registration (`33b3be77`, 15:02:58 PST; amendment A1 `c1beebb8`, residency reads of raw states 2–4 only; A2
`b8db7e92`, the GO interlock in code), before the claim; the field manual's change record, its live gates filled, was
committed as the GO (`724a5473`, 17:28:30 PST) before the one write. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-11-01-e77-rail-one-temperature/`](../reports/data/2026-11-01-e77-rail-one-temperature/README.md)
(`be8ec992` data and the filled
[`CHANGE-RECORD.md`](../reports/data/2026-11-01-e77-rail-one-temperature/CHANGE-RECORD.md), `cb3f1475` results; corrected
after verification in `4bb512f1` and `70f1e13a`, F1–F6, no registered number or verdict changed). Claim
`1566624449086628034`, posted 17:26:15 PST and released by an edit of the same post at 18:04; **32.4 card-minutes**. **The
move:** TDP 65 → 0 at 17:35:34.974 and 0 → 65 at 17:55:10.848 PST, each read back; the threshold never written; the closing
read 65 W / 65 °C at 17:59:25 and the verifier's own read at 18:30:53; module uptime 6 d 3 h 46 m, so no reset; a sampler
that sends no PMIC read, no revisions query and no statistics reset. Arm A ran at the 600 MHz pin, arm B at the card's own
800 MHz; 67 of 72 launches (5 gates not reached), 66 kept; the 8 s settling rung did not run (its preheat failed).

**Results** (as corrected after verification).
- **V FAIL, provisional.** On the central footing R_eff = **0.62 mΩ, 99% [−0.49, +1.73]** over three passes, an interval
  that contains 0 (`results.json` `footings.drift_split.fit`); the other two footings fail too. P1 FAIL (arm B over arm A at
  equal harts 2.33 and 2.68 against 1.913 ± 5%) and P2 four of four pairs mismatched (+22 to +79%), so by the registered
  rule the verdict is provisional.
- **The split stays unresolved (F2).** The registered figure, by the frozen literal conversion, bounds the conduction
  at 0.63 pJ of the 0.875 pJ row; in the registered model's own total-current form the 99% upper edge is 4.77 W,
  **1.06 pJ, more than the whole row** (`corrections-check.txt` C). So E77 does not bound the regulators' share below the
  row, and the frozen reading "the whole row is logic in step with the rails" is not supported.
- **Post hoc, labelled.** The minion rail's cost per hart follows f·V²: arm B over arm A 1.924 (99% over passes
  [1.81, 2.04]) against 1.899 from the measured 518 and 618 mV. The 800 MHz operating point costs 4.09 W (99% [3.44, 4.75]) on
  the minion rail and 1.73 W (99% [1.69, 1.78]) on the SRAM rail before any work; because the card idles at 600 MHz in both
  arms, arm B's deltas carry that step, which is why P1 and P2 failed. With the step fitted one fit bounds the conduction
  at 0.30 pJ, and a fit with the SRAM and NoC rails too gives no bound (`posthoc.json`; `corrections-check.txt` C, D).
- **At a known launch temperature** (secondary S-T): random fp32 launched at 54–55 °C puts **4.645 pJ** a multiply-add on
  the minion rail on every footing, and the block's drift lands 76% off the rails (`results.json`
  `secondary_rail_at_launch_temperature`, `drift`).
- **The run's own reads (F1, F3, F5).** The 22 `SP Runtime Error` events in its window are its 11 residency reads of raw
  state 4, two each, 6–8 s after each read: on BL2 0.22.0 the states are IDLE 0, UP 1, DOWN 2, SAFE 3, and raw 4 is no
  state (rc −5). Raw 3, POWER_SAFE, read 0 at all 11 reads; raw 2, POWER_DOWN, 4,702 s. The return burst's ring holds two
  fresh throttle-ups (17:55:14.92 and 17:55:15.10 PST), so the ring criterion is met; `run_e77.py`'s live check counts
  stale lines too and needs fixing before any re-run. Arm A launched at departure 3's 55 °C floor or 1 °C under it, arm B
  all under it (53–54 °C).

**Scored:** V **FAIL** (provisional); P1 **FAIL**; P2 four of four mismatched; the secondaries FAIL.
**What it changes.** E77 does not bound the regulators' share of the 0.875 pJ row; E120 (Q134), its redesign with the step
fitted and the ladder re-cut, does, and finds E77's post hoc 0.30 pJ bound does not replicate. Its POWER_SAFE zeros were
the lead for item 14 that Q134(a) took up. Its change record keeps eight placeholders and template gaps that E120's
record fills for itself.
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E78 — kinds: the kinds-of-numbers energy matrix, 51 kinds in three formats at one clock (Q130; card runs 2026-10-31, 12:56–14:03 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q130):** "fully explain the speed/heat/power issues of multiplying varying kinds of numbers". Before Q130,
fp16 and int8 had three measured kinds each (zeros, ones, random) against fp32's 36 (Q130(a)'s inventory, hole H1).
**Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/kinds/E78.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/kinds/E78.md) with
`predictions-e78.json` — both frozen models (Q130(c)'s empirical model, `2bbe03dd` with amendment A1 `320f3fdd`, and
Q130(b)'s RTL predictor r2, `fcfe82a7`) and the 51 tiles of the Q130(s) spec at revision 2 (`7d9c0d9e`) — committed
as `cfd94285` at 12:45:37 PDT, with amendment A1 (plumbing only) as `09c6e29b` at 12:48:23, before the claim
(12:55:49) and the first measured pass (12:57:33); claimed and released in #community-lab. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e78-kinds-energy-matrix/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e78-kinds-energy-matrix/README.md)
(`410c56f6`, `961aaf19`; corrected after verification in `861ef337`, which changed no number, verdict or frozen
input). **Nothing was set:** aifoundry3 sat at its boot-time TDP of 0 (600 MHz, 521.7 mV), every config read returned
TDP 0 / 65 °C, and the verifier found no setter call in the frozen harness.

**What ran.** One protocol, one card, one clock: all 51 kinds of
[`kinds.json`](../../tools/claims-v3/kinds/kinds.json) (18 fp32, 17 fp16, 16 int8), each from a raw tile file through
`sparsity_host --test fma --values file:`, measured against each type's own zeros (**D**, paired within the pass) and
against the run's own idle bracket (**S**); six passes 7810–7815, all kept; 388 of 388 runs passed every per-run gate
(two more were skipped before launch by the registered launch-reading rule). 64.8 card-minutes of the registered 90.
Leakage slope (the registered `auto`) 0.1994 W/°C over 250 runs; idle 25.911 ± 0.012 W.

**Results** (board power, read from the board meter; the minion rail, deconvolved, reads 0.800–0.865 of board D over
the 36 kinds above 3 W and is reported beside it, never scored; ± is the 95% interval over six passes of **one tile per
kind**, at the primary leakage slope; the registered arms move D by up to about ±0.6 W on the high-power kinds — fp32 random 25.344 / 24.725 / 24.163 W at
k = 0 / 0.1994 / 0.3803 W/°C — and re-drawing a kind's rule moves its gated fraction: fp32 `sparse50` gates
0.711–0.792 across 400 draws, this tile 0.774).
- **At a pinned clock the data never changes speed:** 546.001 / 546.001 / 318.000 cycles per op on every kind,
  duty 0.996–0.997.
- **Random data over each type's own zeros: 24.725 ± 0.082 / 22.162 ± 0.054 / 7.056 ± 0.059 W** (fp32 / fp16 /
  int8), i.e. **5.816 / 2.625 / 0.306 pJ per multiply-add over idle** (one op is one minion's 16 × 16 TensorFMA:
  4,096 / 8,192 / 16,384 multiply-adds; dated note, 1 Nov 2026: the resident loop's, and a matmul that streams costs more, E101
  below). Each type's zeros over idle: 1.986 / 1.987 / 2.605 W.
- **The zero gate shuts a lane whichever side is zero:** one operand all zero costs **0.62–0.81 W in fp32 and
  0.66–0.78 W in fp16** (2.5–3.5% of random) and 0.31–0.50 W in int8. Q130(c)'s registered decider C-R9 selects the
  gate arm (0.616 W against the bus arm's 5.19 W). If all of the residual is the moving operand's bus, a moving B-bus
  bit costs **at most 12.5–13.0 fJ** — derived, not registered; the A side's figure rests on an unvalidated count of
  the one broadcast word.
- **A lane on with nothing changing:** constants cost 8.1–8.5 W (fp32) and 8.4 W (fp16), 0.33× and 0.38× of random,
  and only **1.56–1.63 W in int8**; int8's static set bits cost nothing measurable (−1 against +1: +0.067 W, 95%
  [−0.050, +0.184], inside the registered ±0.30 W).
- **Mantissa bits cost, magnitude does not:** σ = 2⁻¹⁰ and 2¹⁰ against σ = 1 read 1.002× and 0.999×; zeroing the low
  13 or 16 mantissa bits saves 5.1 or 6.6 W (`fp16grid`; `bf16trunc`, 0.734×); powers of two 0.566×; small integers
  −8..7 save 10.6 W.
- **Order is a small lever:** the same words in the stream order of least change move 23–27% fewer operand bits but
  cost only 5.4 / 6.4 / 6.6% less.
- **A zero is worth most in fp32 and least in int8,** because the gate tests a whole 32-bit word: half the
  *elements* zero is 0.295× / 0.595× / 0.774× of random power; half the *words* zero is 0.318× (fp16) and 0.445×
  (int8); fp16 has no pair gate (one zero half in every two-term pair: 0.908×).
- 🛑 **Heat is resolved only above about 12 W of switching.** On those 19 kinds (10 fp32, 9 fp16) the die rises
  **0.113–0.129 °C per W of S** (random fp32 3.16 °C, fp16 2.80 °C over a 2 s window from a 58 °C launch). The die
  reading moves in whole degrees, so the other 32 kinds — every int8 kind among them — are step-limited (int8 ones
  reads 1.00 °C, the same as random int8): S × 0.118 °C/W is an estimate for them, not a measurement.
- **Replication:** E67's anchors on this card four days earlier reproduce within 0.07 W, and E79's
  constant-to-zeros steps from the same morning within 0.29 W.

**Scored: 28 pass, 2 fail of 30 as the frozen scorer counts them — 27 of 29 with E78-R3c counted once, as
registered — the same at both leakage arms.** Both failures are **E78-R2**, Q130(c)'s accuracy: rms **2.270 W**
against 2.0 in fp32 and **1.054 W** against 1.0 in int8 (fp16 passes at 1.757). Q130(c) ranks every format right
(ρ = 0.941 / 0.948 / 0.867), fails its own C-R1 in fp32 and C-R2 in int8, and over-states what survives mantissa
truncation by 4–5 W; Q130(b)'s r2 ranks best (ρ = 0.974 / 0.978 in fp32 / fp16) but over-predicts int8 by about 2× on
random-like tiles and up to 7× on constants. Departures, dated in the README: the directory's name (D2), `die_c` under
`timeout 20` (D4, `lib.sh`'s frozen design), and an interim reduction of passes 7810–7813 at 13:48 during pass 7814
(D6; nothing was changed or decided on it).

**What it changes.** The kinds table has a measured power and energy cell for every format × kind on one card, and
the pinned-clock half of the blog's story is measured: **the kind of number moves power and heat, never speed, unless
a governor turns power into clock.** The hub's Status item 9 ("fp16 and int8") closes for power and energy.
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E79 — hlp: the fp16 and int8 single-bit price lists (Q130; card runs 2026-10-31, 09:27–10:33 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q130):** the hub's Status item 9, "fp16 and int8". E65 and E67 priced all 32 bit positions of an fp32 operand
on this card; no per-bit price existed for fp16 or int8 in any form (Q130(a), hole H2). **Pre-registered** in
[`tools/claims-v3/hlp/README.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/hlp/README.md) (rules P1–P8, frozen `predictions.json`),
committed as `2a72d352` at 09:24:58 PDT, 1 min 56 s before the claim and 3 min 24 s before the first launch;
amendments A1–A3 (`e66f7c1a`, `270d2108`), A4 (`4216f1fb`: int8's micro-op structure measured, and an order probe,
before block B) and A5–A8 (`23197287`). Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e79-fp16-int8-bit-prices/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e79-fp16-int8-bit-prices/README.md)
(`8d2fe956`; corrected after verification in `c99f34e7` and `1fcf931a`, desk only, no price, interval or pass
changed). The same card, clock and voltage as E65 and E67 (600 MHz, 521.9 mV, TDP 0); no knob written; 63.0 of 75
card-minutes. Ten passes ran and the registered drift gate set two aside (7912, 7914), with no run failing any gate;
348 of 480 runs kept. 🛑 **Disclosed after verification:** a read-only probe opened the card's management device at
09:12:21, before the registration and the claim, twice outside the card lock (departure D2).

**Results.**
- **The port checks against a published number:** an fp32 tie at E65's dearest position reads **1,052 fJ [984,
  1,120]** against E65's 1,008 ± 21.
- **fp16's ten mantissa bits cost what fp32's top ten cost.** B: **205 fJ [204, 206] at bit 0 to 494 fJ [465, 523]
  at bit 9** (monotone, ρ = 1.000); A: 355 to **620 fJ [573, 667]**. In watts fp16 bit *j* costs 0.89–1.06 of fp32
  bit *j* + 13 at all ten positions, so the 0.47 per-bit ratio to fp32 is counting (two fp16 elements ride in each
  32-bit word), not a width law; the registered width checks measure that counting, and the significand-width
  question is **not tested**.
- **int8:** the A list resolves (magnitude bits 71–92 fJ, the top bit 152 fJ, robust to the leakage slope); 🛑 **the
  B list does not** — at E71's registered slope every position is a bound.
- 🛑 **A single-bit price in E65's construction is a composite.** At fp32 B bit 22 and fp16 B bit 9 the price with
  the flip on the column-half boundary against a constant-operand zero (`jg`) is **7.0× and 5.6×** the price in a
  layout that keeps the C accumulators balanced (`jgp`: **150 and 88 fJ**; the order-free watt ratios 0.136 ± 0.012
  and 0.175 ± 0.060). That E65's other 31 positions and E67's A list carry the same composite is **inferred, not
  measured**.
- **int8's 318 cycles per op are 256 of work and 62 of overhead** (two TIMA units per lane with four int8
  multipliers each: 64 multiply-adds a cycle against fp16's 16); A4 measured the structure — a column-half flip never
  alternates in int8.
- **Constant to zeros, paired per pass with no idle bracket (exploratory):** 8.736 ± 0.054 W (fp16) and
  1.834 ± 0.113 W (int8). 🛑 An all-zero tile is gated under any gate rule, so this does **not** test the gate's width.
- **aifoundry3's firmware, read for the first time since 25 September:** release 1.4.0, BL1 and BL2 0.22.0, PMIC
  1.5.0, minions 0.25.1, TDP 0, threshold 65 °C — the versions aifoundry2 reported under E69 (no BL2 git hash is
  exposed, so the same build is not established).

**Scored: 12 passed (two vacuously), 8 failed and 5 not scored of 25; no verdict moves at E71's slope.** Failures:
P1b (the fp16 span 2.41× against ≥ 3.0), P3a, P3a_A, P4_fp16, P6_fp16 (the layout control, which fails on exactly the
composite above), P3a_kg, P8, P8_kg; not scored: int8's P4, its three P5 rules and P6. The first version's 15 / 10 / 0,
"Q130(g) confirmed on silicon" and "zeros are worth most in the widest format" are withdrawn (corrections C1–C14).

**What it changes.** Both missing price lists exist on the same silicon as the fp32 pair, so the blog's bit-price
panel can carry fp32, fp16 and int8-A like for like and int8-B only as "not resolved". A per-bit price is quoted as a
pair (`jg` / `jgp`) where both halves were measured, and never summed over a random word without E70's law. The
firmware read answers the open half of the hub's Status item 7.
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E80 — kspeed: each kind's speed under aifoundry2's own governor (Q130; card runs 2026-10-31, 12:21–13:02 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q130):** the speed half. Horace's effect is a speed effect, and E64 had a burst and a sustained clock for
only nine (format, kind) pairs (Q130(a), holes H3 and H4). **Pre-registered** in
[`tools/claims-v3/kspeed/`](../../tools/claims-v3/kspeed/README.md) (`10f58581`, pushed 12:20:43 PDT, before the
claim at 12:21:13 and the first launch at 12:21:30); its frozen `predictions.json` is built from Q130(c)'s freeze, its
A1 and the kinds spec. Amendment A1 (`075b691e`, 12:34, during the sustained block): a labelled post-hoc view of the
idle reset, no verdict change. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e80-kinds-speed/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e80-kinds-speed/README.md)
(`c244f148`, `d8df2bbf`; corrected after verification in `79711b95`, desk only, no registered number or verdict
changed). **No governor move:** TDP and threshold read 65 W / 65 °C at all 57 per-run reads, at both ends of the
session and under the lock at the close. 18 kinds (fp16 7, int8 6, fp32 5), each a cool-die burst run twice and a
sustained run of about 76 s; 57 runs, 40.5 card-minutes of the 90 allowed.

**Results.**
- **Two limits, one each.** On a cool die only the 65 W power test steps the clock down; in sustained work only the
  thermal test does.
- **Burst:** random fp32 **712 MHz (10.7 TFLOPS)** against `bf16trunc` 774 and `sparse90` / `smallint` 800 MHz. E64's
  governor model fed Q130(c)'s predicted watts **passes E80-R1 at 21.7 MHz rms** — but 8 of the 18 cells sit at the
  800 MHz cap in both prediction and measurement, and over the other 10 the rms is 29.2 MHz. The two burst passes are
  not replicates: pass 2 never started cooler, and every power-limited kind read 5.1–32.6 MHz lower in it.
- **Sustained:** the thermal test holds nine fp32/fp16 kinds at **600–717 MHz** (die 65.5–66.0 °C); sustained, zeros
  against random is still a clamp (fp32 `sparse90` **800 MHz, 12.28 TFLOPS** against random **604 MHz, 9.27**); fp16
  all-ones sits at the trip (792.9 / 770.3 MHz). **int8 barely throttles:** only full-range random int8 reaches the
  trip, stepped down 6 and 7 times in two runs (3–7% of its last 40 s below 800 MHz; 796.7 / 790.0 MHz), and the
  registered ≥ 90%-at-800 rule labels it `none`.
- 🛑 **Why the sustained rules fail.** The heat path moved since 23 October: E80's die line (42.23 + 0.4349 °C/W, rms
  0.56 °C over 21 runs) reaches 65.5 °C at **53.5 W** against E64's 50.3, and the idle die read 45–47 °C against
  50 °C then; the cause is not measured (no ambient, inlet or fan reading in either session). And Q130(c)'s power
  over-charges three low-entropy fp32 kinds (`bf16trunc` 19.11 W against 24.27 [20.70, 27.85]).
- **Post hoc, labelled:** E64-T1 on the measured power and E80's own die line reproduces the 18 sustained cells to
  **9.2 MHz rms**, and fp16 ones to −2.0 MHz — with the thermal path in sample.
- **The governor's idle reset inside a launch** recurs: at least 5 of 225 work processes dropped 800 → 600 MHz while
  the kernel ran on and came back after 1.2–1.3 s (a lower bound: the detector sees 39% of the work time).

**Scored: 1 of 9 registered rules passes (E80-R1).** E80-R2 (13 of 18 limits), R3 (fp16 ones 781.6 against
745.2 MHz, +36.4), R4, Q130(c)'s R7 and R8, S1 (8 of 12 inside), S2 and S3 fail as registered; the departures under
R7 and R8 are named in the README.
**What it changes.** The speed column of the kinds table has 18 measured kinds. The open part is no longer the
governor — its two arms act as the source reads (Q130(d)) — but the **heat path's drift between sessions**: a
sustained prediction needs a die line measured the same day, as E83's was (its relation was fitted in E80's session
that afternoon).
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E81 — dp: the DRAM pattern question, period against arrangement (Q130; card runs 2026-10-31, 14:47–15:13 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked (Q130):** the hub's Status item 6, opened by E75: is a two-value alternating image cheap at DRAM because it
repeats, at the same changed-bit and carried-one counts as random data? **Pre-registered** in
[`tools/claims-v3/dp/`](../../tools/claims-v3/dp/README.md) (`14f219ef`, 14:45:16 PDT: E75's DRAM command and
binary unchanged, a byte-identical replica of the host's fill, rules P1–P7) before the claim (14:47:21) and the first
launch. Data: [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e81-dram-pattern/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e81-dram-pattern/README.md)
(`39cb1759`, `acc66254`; corrected after verification in `34fbb0dc`, no number or verdict changed). Ten passes
p8110–p8119 and a smoke, 130 runs, no run-gate failure, 22.8 of 45 card-minutes; 8110 is the registered warm-up and
8119 was set aside by the registered drift rule (−0.647 W, one board-meter dip), so the headline is eight passes.
Nothing set (TDP 0 / 65 °C, 600 MHz at both ends).

**Results** (95% t over the eight headline passes at the registered `auto` slope, 0.0132 W/°C; the registered
sensitivities 0.2936 and 0.3803 W/°C move items by up to −0.20 W, and every verdict is the same at all three).
- **At E75's k256 counts, E75's two-value image is 1.636 W [1.600, 1.671] cheaper than random bits**, per 75 GB/s of
  64 B DRAM lines (1.1729 × 10⁹ lines a second).
- **61% is the arrangement:** the same ones scattered inside each line instead of in runs cost **+0.996 W [0.936,
  1.055]** (+849 pJ per line).
- **39% is the period:** 8,192 distinct random lines instead of a fixed scattered two-line cycle cost **+0.640 W
  [0.574, 0.706]**, and **only a long period costs** — a scattered four-line cycle costs what a two-line one does
  (+0.030 W [−0.061, +0.122]). The step to random lines also randomizes which bit positions change and spreads the
  per-line counts by about ±11 bits, so repetition is the likely cause, not the isolated one. The split is 58 / 42 at
  E71's slope.
- At random's own counts a contiguous two-value image costs **64% of the zeros-to-random chord**, measured directly
  (E75's 36.6% came from a two-count model that left out the convex step from zeros); random floats cost 0.280 W
  [0.226, 0.334] less than random bits, though they carry more ones.
- **The step from zeros to data reaches the minion rail (+0.075 to +0.153 W, post hoc); the pattern effects do not**
  (no contrast moves it by more than 0.08 W), so the pattern-dependent part is on the memory side.
- A per-event account (pJ per one, per toggle between 16-bit pieces, per toggle between the line's two 32 B halves,
  per changed bit beyond four distinct lines) fits the ten items to 71 pJ per line rms, but it is post hoc, with one
  count built after the data, and LPDDR4x read DBI-DC fits about as well (`posthoc-counts.txt`).

**Scored: 8 of 10 pass; P4 (no period effect) fails, and P5 (closure within ±0.35 W) fails by 0.0007 W** (+0.351;
two board-meter dip samples pull it down 0.85 W, and the labelled dip view reads +0.237). Departures dated D1–D5,
among them an interim reduction of the warm-up pass at 14:55 (D5).
**What it changes.** Status item 6 closes with a two-part answer: **where the ones sit inside a line is the bigger
DRAM lever, and the period costs only past four distinct lines.** It moves nothing in the multiply-add waterfall.
Where between 4 and 8,192 lines the period starts to cost is unmeasured: the host's `file:` operand takes exactly
four lines, so a period ladder needs a registered host change.
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E82 — kinds on a second card: E78's matrix on aifoundry1's card 1 (Q130, second workflow; card runs 2026-10-31, 13:45–15:03 PDT, aifoundry1's card 1) — pre-registered

**Asked:** the owner's *"feel free to use more cards"* (about 13:20): does the kinds matrix travel to another card?
**Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/kinds/E82.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/kinds/E82.md) with
`predictions-e82.json` (`06bc042e`, pushed 13:44:00 PDT, before the claim at 13:44:56): E78's harness and reduction
unchanged, the cross-card rules X1–X3 frozen. Amendments: A1 (`249055ff`, before pass 8210) — **card 1 reports
release 1.4.0, BL1 and BL2 0.22.0, PMIC 1.3.0, minions 0.25.1, not the 1.2.0 / BL2 0.18.0 on record** in AGENT.md §4 and 14-card-behaviour.md (START.md had
carried release 1.4.0 since the lab lead's update of 7 October, `1c00d6ff`, and neither took it up), and the smoke
ran at 600 MHz in all 375 samples at up to 56.8 W; A2 (`0117ee1c`) — one pass added after pass 8210 was set aside by a
board-meter dip, and a labelled dip-filtered view. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e82-kinds-aifoundry1/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e82-kinds-aifoundry1/README.md)
(`0a041356`; corrected after verification in `86400045`, no number, verdict or frozen input changed). Seven passes
8210–8216, five kept by the registered gates; 78 of 90 card-minutes; 600 MHz in every busy sample at 498.7 mV; TDP
65 W / 65 °C read, nothing set.

**Results** (board power, as E78's: the minion rail, deconvolved, reads lower, fp32 random Drail 20.03 W against D
23.99 W, and is reported, never scored).
- **The kinds rank the same on both cards:** Spearman **0.995 / 0.997 / 1.000** over 17 / 16 / 15 non-zero kinds
  (X1 PASS).
- **The cards differ by about one scale:** D(aifoundry3) / D(card 1) has a median of **1.031** (IQR 1.025–1.034;
  1.030 / 1.033 / 1.027 by format; sd 1.2% across kinds, though χ² rejects one exact scale at p ≈ 0.002, led by fp16
  ones at 1.048). That is **outside** the 0.88–0.99 band September's cards predicted (X2a, X2b, X3 FAIL).
- **Per multiply-add over idle on card 1:** random 5.62 / 2.54 / 0.295 pJ and zeros 0.40 / 0.20 / 0.076 pJ (fp32 /
  fp16 / int8); a zero is again worth most in fp32 (half the elements zero keeps 28% / 61% / 77% of random switching).
- 🛑 **Card 1 reads 7–14% below E38's September anchors, depending on the leakage arm** (fp32 random 23.99 ± 0.11 W
  against 26.66), but those anchors came from launches near 80 °C by another protocol, where leakage over the window
  (0.81 W/°C × 3.41 °C = 2.76 W) is as large as the gap. Whether the card changed is **untested**; aifoundry3 reads
  3.4% above its own September value by the same comparison.
- Heat resolves only for the 19 kinds above about 12 W of switching, at 0.130 °C per W (aifoundry3 0.118).
- **A meter trap:** card 1's board reading sometimes lands in the 1–2 ms gap between launches (85 low samples in 61
  events over seven passes); the gates set two passes aside for it, and removing the dips moves no cross-card verdict.

**Scored: E78's rules on card 1, 22 pass and 4 fail of 26 as registered (23 / 4 as the scorer counts)** — the two
E78-R2 cells that fail on aifoundry3 too (Q130(c)'s accuracy in fp32 and int8) and R4's two September anchors.
E78-R2 fp16, R3f (int8 static bits) and R4 fp32 ones move between the registered leakage arms and are labelled
slope-sensitive; the verdicts both cards scored agree in 24 of 26.
**What it changes.** The kinds table has a second card: ranks and mechanisms travel, and watts travel at about one
scale with the card and the protocol named. The card table's firmware for card 1 was stale (A1).
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E83 — kspeed/e83: the 33 kinds E80 did not run, under aifoundry2's own governor (Q130, second workflow; card runs 2026-10-31, 13:46–15:08 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked:** *"feel free to use more cards"* — a speed for every kind E80 did not run. **Pre-registered** in
[`tools/claims-v3/kspeed/e83/`](../../tools/claims-v3/kspeed/e83/README.md) (`c253bd1c`, pushed 13:45:13 PDT, before
the claim at 13:45:55 and the smoke at 13:46:04): prediction (a) is E80's measured power-to-clock relation (a dither at
an effective cap, fitted on E80's 18 kinds only: burst W* 66.15 W, sustained W* 49.75 W) applied to Q130(c)'s
predicted power, and (b) Q130(c)'s own frozen clocks. No amendment. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e83-kinds-speed-rest/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e83-kinds-speed-rest/README.md)
(`6f02f1bc`; corrected after verification in `734e997f`, desk only, no number or verdict changed). 33 kinds (fp32 13, fp16 10, int8 10) and E80's five anchors; 118 runs, none failed, 80.8 of 100
card-minutes; TDP 65 W / 65 °C at all 118 per-run reads, nothing set.

**Results.**
- **All 51 kinds now have a measured burst and sustained speed** (E80's 18 and these 33).
- **Speed follows power, and the kind sets the power.** The relation fitted on E80's 18 kinds gives the 33 new kinds'
  clocks from their measured power to **7.9 MHz (burst) / 7.5 MHz (sustained) rms** (E83-S1 PASS); fed Q130(c)'s
  predicted power it reaches 11.8 / 14.3 MHz (R1, R3 PASS), beating Q130(c)'s own frozen sustained clocks (23.4 MHz,
  R4 PASS). E80's anchors repeat within 11.2 MHz (R5), and the heat path held since E80 (the session's die line sits
  0.15 °C below E80's).
- **Two limits for all 51 kinds:** 11 kinds stepped down in burst, all by the power test first (0 of 76 bursts
  thermal-first), and no sustained run of 42 carried a power label (the thermal cells sat at a 64.9–66.3 °C die and
  51.6–55.3 W of board power). **int8 holds 786–800 MHz sustained on all 16 kinds.**
- **The power model fails:** a lane gated by a zero operand costs 0.1–0.8 W above zeros where Q130(c) says exactly
  zeros (22 of 32 inside its 95% interval, S2 FAIL), and `fp16grid` draws 4.3 W less than predicted while `pow2` draws
  0.7 W more (S3 FAIL).
- The idle reset recurred in 7 of 454 work processes (1.5%), each with a POWER_IDLE record.

**Scored: 6 of 8 registered rules pass (R1–R5, S1); S2 and S3 fail.** The verifier held it with fixes (nine minor
faults), all corrected in `734e997f` with no number or verdict changed: the two interim looks at partial data (14:06
and 14:26, the second reading the registered verdicts on 90 of 118 runs) are disclosed as departure D2, and neither
copy slowed a cell; the switching term `c` is stated as board switching at 600 MHz and 0.517 V over E64's fitted
baseline, not a power over idle (at 800 MHz the same data switch 1.91× as much); one sustained run carries ±15.6 MHz
(1σ) on the slope and ±5.6 MHz at a clamp, not ±11 / ±4; the rms figures carry their values over the kinds whose
clock is free to move beside them (R1 19.8, R3 18.3, S1 10.6 / 8.4 MHz); and the burst passes ran in two rounds,
pass 2 from a die 1.3 °C warmer and 7.9 MHz slower on the power-limited kinds.
**What it changes.** The speed column of the kinds table is complete on one card in one afternoon, and the governor's
sustained clock closes out of sample once the heat path is measured the same day (for E83, in E80's session that
afternoon); the open part is the power model.
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E84 — kspeed/e84: E80's speed design on aifoundry1's card 0 (Q130, second workflow; card runs 2026-10-31, 15:42–16:07 PDT, aifoundry1's card 0) — pre-registered

**Asked:** *"feel free to use more cards"* — a speed replicate on another card, board and PMIC under the same governor
version. **Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/kspeed/e84/`](../../tools/claims-v3/kspeed/e84/README.md)
(`337b03b2`, pushed 15:41:12 PDT, before the claim at 15:41:50: 11 kinds, frozen predictions, E80's `run_kspeed.py` and
E64's `run_hpl` with five card-0 replacements), with amendment A1 (`1713a3f9`, 15:42:43, after the read-only identity
reads and before the smoke): card 0 reports **release 1.4.0, BL1 and BL2 0.22.0, PMIC 1.6.1, minions 0.25.1** — not the
1.4.1 / BL2 0.21.2 on record — and read TDP 65 W, threshold 65 °C and an idle at 600 MHz / 519 mV, so nothing frozen
changed. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e84-speed-aifoundry1-card0/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e84-speed-aifoundry1-card0/README.md)
(`d6d1a0c8`; corrected after verification in `70cf9200`, desk only: no number in `results.json` or `reduce.txt` and no
verdict changed). Smoke 15:42:50–15:43:12, session 15:43:32–16:06:38: 35 of 35 runs (each kind a cool-die burst twice,
in two rounds, and one sustained run; fp32 zeros and random sustained twice), exit 0, **23.1 card-minutes** of a
60-minute cap; released 16:08. **Nothing was set:** all 39 configuration reads gave TDP 65 W / 65 °C / 600 MHz / 519 mV.
The 139 launches ran under `timeout 10` with the stop file; the firmware read ran under `timeout 20` and the sampler is
stopped by SIGTERM (35 of 35 rc 0), as the data README now says.

**Results** (board power; "the same day" is aifoundry2's E80 and E83 cells).
- **Zeros are at least as fast as random data in every format, burst and sustained, on a second card** (P1), and int8 is
  the least throttled (P2): zeros minus random +160.6 / +246.2 MHz (fp32, burst / sustained), +24.0 / +162.2 (fp16),
  0 / +5.9 (int8).
- **fp16 and int8 are consistent with aifoundry2's sustained ratios, one run each:** zeros over random 1.275× (aifoundry2
  1.297×) and 1.008× (1.009×; aifoundry2's two random-data runs give 1.005–1.014×, as wide as the effect).
- 🛑 **fp32 random data trips card 0's PMIC over-power alarm** (post hoc): at 800 MHz it draws 72–75 W on the board (p800
  72.4 / 73.6 W), the PMIC's own readings reach 75.00–76.25 W against its 75,000 mW set point, and each alarm puts the card
  in POWER_SAFE at 300 MHz; the power arm climbs back 300 → 600 → 700 → 800 MHz and the alarm trips again, a median
  1.106 s from one safe state to the next. Only the four fp32 random runs logged it (all 436 readings at 300 MHz are in
  them); none of aifoundry2's 247 committed trace rings holds one, though its fp32 random data read 80–88 W at 800 MHz. So
  **zeros run 1.67× faster sustained** (replicate pairings 1.655–1.682×) against 1.29–1.31× on aifoundry2 the same day,
  and 1.41× in the burst (aifoundry2 1.15×).
- **All-ones data run near full clock on both cards** (card 0 793.5 / 795.6 MHz, one run each; aifoundry2 782.4 /
  781.6 MHz the same day, one of two runs thermal each). Card 0 holds them by drawing about 6 W less, not by cooling
  better: per board watt its die runs 1.1–1.5 °C hotter (die line 41.78 + 0.4753 °C/W, 65.5 °C at 49.9 W, against E83's
  53.4 W).
- Card 0 idled at 600 MHz between all 35 runs (19.0–21.2 W), where the card table recorded a 300 MHz idle on 25 Sep
  under 1.4.1. E60's 20 Oct card-0 telemetry idled at 600 MHz too, and 30 of its 33 300 MHz episodes inside the work
  match the alarm cycle; its trace rings were not committed, so the alarm is not confirmed for 20 Oct.

**Scored: all four registered rules pass (P1, P2, P3, S1).** P3 — the limiting test right for 9 of 11 kinds in the burst
and 10 of 11 sustained — rests on the eight kinds predicted `none` (8 of 8 in both blocks); of the three predicted to be
limited, 1 of 3 is right in the burst and 2 of 3 sustained, because the registered labels have no PMIC category.
Corrected after verification (C1–C12): the all-ones comparison uses the same day's aifoundry2 runs, E64's 23 Oct
737.9 / 737.1 MHz is withdrawn as the comparison and "better cooling" with it; the fp32 ratios use same-day cells (the
E64-based 1.133× / 1.325× are withdrawn); the idle range, the intervals and the device holds are stated as they were.
**What it changes.** "Zeros run faster" is a property of the numbers on two cards; its size in fp32 is a property of the
card — its power and its PMIC's alarm, not its cooling. A third speed limit joins the governor's two, which is why the
evening round left card 0 out. The card table's firmware and idle point for card 0 were stale (A1;
[14-card-behaviour.md](14-card-behaviour.md)).
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E85 — hlk: the thermal camera's view of different kinds of numbers (Q130, second workflow; card runs 2026-10-31, 15:54–16:36 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked:** *"feel free to use more cards"*, the second workflow's optional lane: does the camera behind the board, blind
to watts, weigh kinds of numbers as the meter does? **Pre-registered** in
[`tools/claims-v3/hlk/`](../../tools/claims-v3/hlk/README.md) (`8b441fd9`, pushed 15:52:31 PDT: the protocol, frozen
predictions, the lock-in reducer and its self-tests), before the claim at 15:53:52 and the operand check at 15:54:15.
E63 amendment A1's geometry unchanged (192 minions, six a shire, at 800 MHz); each kind against its own type's zeros at
0.05 Hz, with E63's reference rung first, in the middle and last; `run_hlk.py` imports E63's runner unchanged. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e85-camera-kinds/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e85-camera-kinds/README.md)
(`cf9eba15`; corrected after verification in `2c77e444`, desk only, no verdict moved). One session, 15:54:17–16:36:18,
240 launches, none failed, **42.0 card-minutes** (the runner's cap 44); no smoke run — a 2 s operand check stood in
(departure b); released 16:39. TDP 65 W / 65 °C read at both ends. 🛑 **One write:** the sampler, inherited from E63's
runner, ran `ettelem sample --every-ms 100 --reset-ms 1000`, which sends the SP a statistics reset that the SP forwards
to the PMIC (0x47): **2,205 resets** in the session. No governor, TDP, threshold, clock, card reset, PCIe, driver or
firmware command was sent. Whether the reset counts as PMIC work under Q130's and AGENT.md's rules was put to the
owner, and **the owner's ruling of 31 October 2026 allows it** (AGENT.md §5); E52's, E62's, E63's and E73's sessions
recorded the same flag, E64, E80 and E83 ran without it, and E86–E91 sampled without it.

**Results** (camera signal at 0.05 Hz; mK per W of the board's fundamental).
- **The camera weighs fp32 and fp16 random data as the meter does:** fp32 random 267.6 ± 19.9 mK (52.10 ± 3.82 mK/W),
  fp16 random 227.0 ± 13.6 mK (50.72 ± 3.21 mK/W); ratios to the registered reference line **0.869 ± 0.118 and
  0.923 ± 0.080, pooled 0.895 ± 0.086**, with the same lag (−6.2 ± 6.2° and −1.7 ± 4.7°). Against a constant reference
  (post hoc) they read 0.97 and 0.95, pooled 0.95 ± 0.07: E85 alone resolves no camera drift (−29 ± 29 %/h; a constant
  fits the three reference blocks, χ² 1.16 / 2), so either reading says "equal within about 25%".
- 🛑 **int8 was not detected:** random int8 (a 2.45 W step) read 64.5 ± 28.3 mK, p 0.054, in a block where the shutter
  fired 58 times in 18 laps; its ratio, lag and absolute passes carry no information.
- fp32 `sparse90`'s 0.24 W is invisible (29 ± 22 mK), as the meter says.
- Post hoc and suggestive: clock excursions cluster just after the sampler's resets (late ramps in 13 of the 47 launches
  checked right after a reset against 10 of the other 193, one-sided Fisher p 3.5 × 10⁻⁵; 4 of 6 mid-launch idle-reset
  dips); a run without `--reset-ms` would decide it.

**Scored: 8 of 9 rules pass as registered.** R1 (seen) fails for int8, and int8's R2 (tolerance ±1.014), R4 (±40.4°)
and R9 passes are uninformative. Corrections after verification (C1–C9): the safety paragraph's "no PMIC or reset
command" now names the statistics reset; "−29%/h" is −29 ± 29 %/h; "8 of 9 in full" is "as registered"; the ×1024/192
"full-card equivalents" are a linear scaling 6–14% under the measured differences and are dropped; the camera-read watts
(7.22 / 6.68 W against the meter's 8.32 / 7.47) are the ratio restated in watts, not an independent wattmeter; the
firmware is named from E69's read (release 1.4.0 / BL2 0.22.0, not read by E85).
**What it changes.** The camera, blind to watts, weighs whole kinds of numbers, not only flipped bits, at the reference
rung's mK per W within about 25% in fp32 and fp16. int8's heat stays unseen by the camera (E89 measured it on the die's
sensors). The statistics reset is a PMIC command older lab runs inherited, allowed by the owner's ruling of
31 October 2026; it restarts the PMIC's running average, so a run that tests the governor's power loop samples
without it ([14-card-behaviour.md](14-card-behaviour.md), the telemetry table).
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E86 — the real GPT-2 tiles' energy beside E78's anchors (Q130, evening round; card runs 2026-10-31, 20:07–20:41 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked:** *"feel free to keep using the lab"* (about 18:1x) and the Q130 critic's item 3: do real tensors cost what
E78's synthetic kinds cost? **Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/kinds/E86.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/kinds/E86.md)
with `predictions-e86.json` (`190c1ac4`, pushed 20:05:48 PDT, before the claim at 20:06:48), scored against Q130(n)'s
frozen points and its Amendment 1 intervals ([`tools/claims-v3/kinds/real/`](../../tools/claims-v3/kinds/real/README.md)).
E78's `run_e78.py` and `reduce_e78.py` ran unchanged, sha256-checked, with E86's items installed: Q130(n)'s nine tiles
(GPT-2 small's layer 0 MLP up, layer 6 MLP down and layer 6 attention QKᵀ, one 16 × 16 window each, in fp32, fp16 and
int8) beside E78's six anchors (three times a pass) and E78's `nn_like` and `gauss_q` (once). Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e86-real-tensors-energy/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e86-real-tensors-energy/README.md)
(`a3c0b60d`; corrected after verification in `65f0ab03`, no registered number or verdict changed). Five passes; the
registered random-range gate set 8614 aside (two board-meter dip samples in an fp32 random window), so four are kept;
card time 20:07:09–20:40:56, **33.8 card-minutes** of 40; released 20:41:29. TDP 0 / 65 °C and 600 MHz read at both
ends, nothing set, no `--reset-ms`.

**Results** (per pass, real ÷ random of the same format: D over the format's zeros, S over idle, and the whole board).
- **Real float tiles cost what random data costs:** D 0.983–0.994 (fp32; three-tile mean 0.988 ± 0.010) and 0.971–0.996
  (fp16; 0.987 ± 0.014); over idle 0.985–0.996 and 0.972–0.997; of the whole board 0.992–0.997 and 0.987–0.998. Dated note,
  1 Nov 2026: that is 97–100% of random data's switching on three GPT-2 tiles, the attention tile's 0.983 ± 0.002 (fp32) and
  0.971 ± 0.012 (fp16) exclude 1, and E95's thirty tiles fail it as a rule (below). No real
  tile holds a zero word, so nothing is gated. Layer 6's MLP down-projection in fp32 costs 99.4% ± 0.1% of random.
- **Real int8 saves through small values:** D 0.782–0.888 (0.827 ± 0.050), over idle 0.852–0.920, of the whole board
  0.958–0.977 — 11–22% over int8's zeros, 8–15% over idle and 2.3–4.2% of the board. 1–9% of the values are zero and no
  32-bit word is; E86 does not split zeros from small magnitudes.
- Switching over idle: fp32 5.72–5.79 pJ per multiply-add (random 5.81), fp16 2.54–2.61 (2.61), int8 0.259–0.280 (0.304).
- **Heat (the whole-degree reading, paired per pass):** the float tiles warm the die in 2 s as random data does (every
  difference's interval includes 0); the int8 tiles warm it less (−0.125 ± 0.046, −0.125 ± 0.046, −0.175 ± 0.157 °C).
- **Q130(n)'s predictor:** all nine tiles inside Amendment 1's intervals, rms 0.155 / 0.393 / 0.417 W; beside it, a
  no-model baseline ("each tile costs E78's random kind") scores 0.353 / 0.446 / 1.237 W, so the predictor adds 2.3× in
  fp32, 3.0× in int8 and only 1.13× in fp16.
- The launch-boundary board-meter dip E82 found on aifoundry1's card 1 occurs on aifoundry3 too (13 samples in E86's
  power windows; 1–7 a pass in E78's); with the dips removed (post hoc) all five passes are kept and R3 passes in every
  format.

**Scored: 4 PASS, 2 INCONCLUSIVE, no FAIL** (R1, R2, R5 and S1 pass; R3 is inconclusive on fp16's direction, upper bound
1.0014; R4 on int8 `l0_mlp_up` against `gauss_q`, −0.17 ± 0.77 W). Corrections after verification (C1–C7): the first
version's "the real tiles warm the die as much as random data" is reversed for int8; every ratio names its baseline; the
no-model baseline sits beside R2; E89's withdrawn per-rail form is no longer cited.
**What it changes.** The kinds table gains a real-tensor row: in these three GPT-2 tiles trained float tensors cost what
random bits cost, and quantised int8 saves a little through small values. E78's `nn_like` (half exact zeros) is not a
realistic stand-in for the float tiles; `gauss_q` is a fair one for int8. These are three windows of one model, not
GPT-2 in general.
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E87 — kspeed/e87: the real GPT-2 tiles' speed under aifoundry2's own governor (Q130, evening round; card runs 2026-10-31, 19:49–20:25 PDT, aifoundry2) — pre-registered

**Asked:** the Q130 critic's item 3, the speed half. **Pre-registered** in
[`tools/claims-v3/kspeed/e87/`](../../tools/claims-v3/kspeed/e87/README.md) (`7f84f454`, pushed 19:47:42 PDT, before the
claim at 19:48:48 and the smoke at 19:49:01): E80's and E83's design (`run_kspeed.py` imported unchanged) on Q130(n)'s
nine tiles plus E80's anchors (fp32 and fp16 random, int8 full, one zeros per type); a cool-die burst twice, in two
rounds, and one ~76 s sustained run per tile. `predictions.json` holds Q130(n)'s frozen points and Amendment 1's scoring
intervals. No amendment, no departure. Data:
[`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e87-real-tensors-speed/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e87-real-tensors-speed/README.md)
(`bc96f37d`; corrected after verification in `8a416ffd`, desk only, no number or verdict changed). 47 runs, every launch
rc 0, **35.8 card-minutes** (smoke 0.3 and session 35.5, 19:49:49–20:25:19) of 40; released 20:25. TDP 65 W / 65 °C at
all 47 per-run reads and at the final read (20:25:35, committed as `final-config.txt`), nothing set, no `--reset-ms`.

**Results.**
- **Real fp32 and fp16 run at about random data's speed:** burst real minus random −2.7 MHz (fp32, 703.3 against 706.0)
  and +7.8 MHz (fp16, 729.0 against 721.2: 1.1%, the predicted direction, but 1.3σ, so not established); sustained +3.7 /
  +1.7 MHz, both formats at the thermal clamp (600–620 MHz).
- **int8** bursts at 800 MHz (84.3 TOPS) apart from one idle-reset pass; sustained, the real tiles held 793.6–800 MHz
  and int8 full 774.7 MHz (thermal) in this session, but int8 full held 793.4 MHz in both E80 runs and 786.5 (thermal) in
  E83: single runs land either side of the 65 °C trip whatever the data, so **no int8 real-against-random sustained
  difference is shown.**
- **Like with like** (switching above zeros as a share of the same session's random reference): real fp32 0.985–0.995,
  fp16 0.962–0.990, int8 0.797–0.904 of uniform random int8 — E86's ratios, on another card in another session.
- E83's power-to-clock relation carries over to real data: on each tile's measured switching term it gives the clocks
  to 10.1 MHz (burst) and 5.2 MHz (sustained) rms (S1).

**Scored: all nine registered rules pass** (R1 burst rms 10.68 MHz; R2 9 of 9 sustained limits; R3 sustained rms
6.11 MHz; R4 8 of 9 bursts and 9 of 9 sustained cells inside Amendment 1's intervals; R5 12 of 12 anchor rows within
18.7 MHz of E80 and E83; S1–S4). 🛑 **The clock rules do not test the power predictor:** a no-model baseline (each real
tile = E80's random cell for its format) scores 9.75 MHz burst and 5.14 MHz sustained, as well as the frozen points. The
predictor's test is S4's int8 bar (0.835 < 0.9, which "real = random" fails) and the fp32 / fp16 sign (all six tiles
below 1; t 2.48 / 2.25 on 2 df, three tiles of one model). Corrected after verification (C1–C10): that framing; fp16's
+7.8 MHz is 1.3σ, not "inside the noise"; the first version's "real int8 stays below the thermal trip, random does not"
is withdrawn; the round differences, the round-2 populations and the p800 start-temperature confound are stated.
**What it changes.** The speed column gains a real-tensor row: real fp32 and fp16 data give the chip nothing to skip and
run at random data's speed, and real int8 runs as random int8 does. The power → clock link holds out of sample on real
data; on real data the kind → power link's evidence is like with like, not the clock rules.
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E88 — rpath: what the result path costs against the operand bits (Q130, evening round; card runs 2026-10-31, 18:54–19:32 PDT, aifoundry1's card 1) — pre-registered

**Asked:** the Q130 critic's item 2: no model had an accumulator term, and the blog material said only that "the lane is
clocked and its accumulator still walks". **Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/rpath/E88.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/rpath/E88.md)
with `predictions-e88.json` (`3dd99088`, 18:53:19 PDT, before the claim at 18:54:33): tile designs, built by
`make_e88.py` and checked on its desk emulation (14 of 14), that freeze or walk the accumulator, cancel or keep the dot
product and move the operand bits apart from the result, with a stream model's frozen bands; E78's runner and reduction.
Data: [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e88-result-path/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e88-result-path/README.md)
(`f1c7412e`, `01446baf`, `867b18bf`; corrected after verification in `052429d3`, desk only, no verdict moved). Six
passes; E78's zero-anchor gate set 8813 aside (fp16 zeros −0.297 W off its line), so five are kept; **37.6
card-minutes** of 45 (18:54:48–19:32:22); released 19:37. 600 MHz, 499 mV, TDP 65 W / 65 °C read at every pass's two
ends, nothing set, no `--reset-ms`; card 0 untouched.

**Results** (board power over the type's zeros; ± is the 95% interval over the five passes, statistical only — the
registered leakage arms move the parts by up to −0.38 / +0.52 W and the shares by at most 1.7 points).
- **A multiply-add on random positive fp32 operands** (|N(0, 1)|, 21.6 W) splits into **the clocked lane 7.92 ± 0.18 W
  (37%), the operand-side multiplier 8.61 ± 0.18 W (40%) and the result path 5.04 ± 0.19 W (23%)** — 1.73 / 1.88 /
  1.10 pJ per multiply-add. E78's random data (random signs) costs 2.24 ± 0.17 W more, which the split does not place: as
  shares of E78's random fp32, 33 / 36 / 21% plus 9%.
- The fp16 and int8 designs, whose values come in pairs so that a dot product can cancel, split **39 / 30 / 31%** and
  **26 / 47 / 27%** (fp16 7.96 / 6.19 / 6.29 W; int8 1.71 / 3.14 / 1.81 W); in them the multiplier side includes the
  in-lane adder up to where its sum cancels. They cost 0.959 and 0.977 of their random anchors.
- **A constant's accumulator walk costs almost nothing:** freezing it saves 0.10 ± 0.31 W (fp32), 0.01 ± 0.13 W (fp16)
  and −0.23 ± 0.25 W (int8).
- **About 70% of the fp32 result path is the bus swinging between the two accumulators a lane alternates between**
  (0.693 ± 0.018; 0.754 raw, before the design's own operand toggles, model-priced at 0.27–0.31 W, are taken off) — E79's
  hypothesis, now measured.
- 🛑 **Random signs stay unexplained:** with the result frozen by absorption, random signs (±1.0) still cost
  2.52 ± 0.19 W over a constant (registered at ≤ 1 W).
- On the minion rail the shares hold within 2 points; the watts are lower (fp32 6.98 / 7.14 / 3.92 of 18.03 W).

**Scored: 29 pass, 3 fail of 32** (13 mechanism rules and 19 model bands); all three failures are random signs
(H4-signs-mult, P-M32s, P-R32s). The stream model's split falls inside 17 of its 19 out-of-sample bands, which are
±0.75 to ±1.80 W wide, but misplaces the parts inside them (fp32 bus swing 4.75 W predicted against 3.80 W measured;
int8 result path 3.07 against 1.81 W, multiplier 2.12 against 3.14 W). The dips-kept arm keeps three passes and is not
scored. Corrections after verification: the split is named for what was measured (random positive fp32 operands, paired
fp16 and int8 designs) and as board power; "75%" is about 70%; the bands' width is stated; and the card's result was
**never read back** (the host skips its result check for `--values file:`), so frozen against walking rests on the desk
emulation and the power data agreeing with it.
**Dated note, Sun 1 Nov 2026 (week 44):** E104 read C back. fp32's freezes hold, and fp16's `r_cancel` and `c_cancel` hold
0, but fp16's `r_abs` walks one unit a micro-op: "two ways of freezing agree" (result 6) fails on the card, and A16 and H6
stand as numbers only. E92 and E104 tested the sign's share: see those entries, and this README's dated note.
**What it changes.** The critic's item 2 closes for the split of random positive operands: the lane being on, the
multiplier and the result path each take a share of the same order, the result path's mostly as a bus swing. The sign's
share is measured (here and in E90) but not explained. One card only (aifoundry1's card 1; E82's 1.031 suggests
readings about 3% higher on aifoundry3).
**Report:** no page of its own; the data README is the public record.

## E89 — e89: heat below 12 W of switching, the die's 34 sensors read one by one (Q130, evening round; card runs 2026-10-31, 18:59–19:42 PDT, aifoundry3) — pre-registered

**Asked:** the Q130 critic's item 7, the Horace hub's Status item 7: E78's heat column is step-limited below about 12 W
of switching, every int8 kind among them. **Pre-registered** in [`tools/claims-v3/e89/E89.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/e89/E89.md)
with `predictions-e89.json` (`850421ec`, pushed 18:58:18 PDT, before the claim at 18:58:57); amendments A1 and A2
(`ac95a217`, 19:03:20, before the passes): on this firmware build command 74, group 0, returns **raw 12-bit sensor
codes** (0.061 °C a code), and the sampler matches replies on the tag alone (10 Hz). Each kind ran against its own
type's zeros (int8 zeros against idle) as a 60 s square wave beside fp32 random data: two passes, four laps a kind.
Data: [`docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e89-heat-low-power/`](../reports/data/2026-10-31-e89-heat-low-power/README.md)
(`1bef3fc0`; corrected after verification in `ae24b0af`, no number, verdict or frozen input changed). The claim was held
43.6 min (18:58:57–19:42:31) and the two passes took **38.4 card-minutes**; TDP 0 / 65 °C and 600 MHz, nothing set, no
statistics reset. Departures dated in the README: no steady-state rise was attempted (D1, decided before the claim on
desk simulations: the heat path's 400 s and 2,500 s stages need about 30 min a kind), a 3 s sampler check (D3), and the
lap as the registered unit where the brief's unit is the pass (D7).

**Results.**
- **Seven of E78's 32 unresolved kinds measured:** six of its 16 int8 kinds (random, `gauss_q`, `smallint`, `sparse50`,
  −1 and zeros) and fp32 ones. The other ten int8 kinds and fifteen float kinds below 12 W stay S × 0.118 °C/W estimates.
- **Per board watt they heat the die as random fp32 does, within 4–11%:** random fp32 0.1979 ± 0.0041 °C per board W at
  1/60 Hz; the seven kinds 1.041–1.113 of it, every interval inside the registered [0.80, 1.25]. Five of the seven
  exclude 1 under the registered model (lap unit, quadratic trend), three with the pass as the unit, none with a linear or
  cubic trend. Post hoc, per minion-rail watt, 0.980–1.008: the difference is where the board's watts go (most likely
  regulator loss outside the metered rails, which was not measured).
- **E78's 2 s window, on the fine reading:** random int8 0.863 ± 0.073 °C over int8 zeros, int8 −1 0.222 ± 0.026 °C over
  int8 zeros, int8 zeros 0.340 ± 0.006 °C over idle, fp32 ones 1.059 ± 0.164 °C over fp32 zeros. Derived over idle (if
  2 s responses add), random int8 comes to about 1.20 °C, and the seven kinds 4.5–14% above E78's S × 0.118
