## E182 — where a held 1-bit costs power: one-number kinds whose A and B popcounts differ, read as A B B A trains launch by launch and in E178's kinds passes (Q153; registered, kitted, dated notes 1; aifoundry3 card 0 at 600 MHz pinned) — not run *(dated, Thu 12 Nov 2026, Q165's records: as first written; it ran on 8 November, FULL, blind: "Run on 8 November, blind", below)*

**Asked (Q146, a second time, and Q150):** which side's held 1-bits cost power in a one-number TensorFMA. E178 read his int8 pair, 5s
against 4s, at +0.053 W [+0.022, +0.085], about 0.29 of M10's frozen +0.186 W, and could not say whether the held operand bits or the
accumulator's flips set the order; its 13 int8 kinds had A's and B's bits nearly in step. **What it is:** in a one-number kind no operand
bit changes inside a launch; what switches is the accumulator walking 0, p, 2p, …, its buses, and buses that return to zero between
uses, so a held 1-bit can cost only where a bus returns to zero. **The 24 kinds, by a stated rule**
([`make_e182_kinds.py`](../../tools/claims-v3/e182/make_e182_kinds.py)): int8 (1, 1), (−1, −1), (1, −1) and (−1, 1); for b in 15, 85
and 127, (1, b), (b, 1), (−1, −b) and (−b, −1), where for odd factors −x = (~x) | 1, so popcount(−x) = 9 − popcount(x); int8 4s, 5s,
(4, 5) and (5, 4); fp32 4.0s, 5.0s, and (1.0, 0x3FFFFFFF) both ways. Swaps and sign flips at one product keep the accumulator's walk and
the A/B Hamming distance and move only where the held 1-bits sit. Six pairs run as **A B B A trains**, the two kinds alternated launch
by launch inside one host process (a train is 64 processes of 13 launches in int8, 76 in fp32), and all 24 kinds run again in E178's
kinds passes on E178's footing. **Registered** [`58b88850`](../../tools/claims-v3/e182/E182.md) (13:57:12 PST Sat 7 Nov, committed alone before any kit
file; 33 files, `SHA256SUMS-e182-registration` 32). **Kitted** [`41865c18`](../../tools/claims-v3/e182/KIT.md) (14:29:57; 48 files,
`SHA256SUMS-e182-kit` 79): a host-only change that alternates two kinds launch by launch (its desk test on a mock runtime 25 of 25, every
train launch's C checked against its own tiles), the scorer self-tested on E178's committed data (12 of 12), `dmcmd_audit` GO on 32 send
sites, the desk machine's dry pass 29 of 29, and the runbook's desk test with a stand-in for the box, 9 of 9. **Dated notes 1** for the
registration and the kit, [`6c158cd2`](../../tools/claims-v3/e182/KIT.md.dated-note-1.md) (16:22:21; 26 files,
`SHA256SUMS-e182-note1` 50), after a verifier that held both with fixes (2 major, 7 minor): the registered fallback, PASSES after a
failed train-host build, now runs end to end through steps 6, 8, 9 and 11 v2; the train host is built only from its 8 pinned build
inputs and `main.cpp`, which step 5 v2 sends to the box (the E174 class); the train runner's own stops are tested live at the desk (8 of
8); the runbook's v2 desk test reads 19 of 19. No rule, forecast, threshold, statistic, power figure, plan, cap or stop changed. **Not run;
0 card-minutes.**

**The frozen forecasts** (registered, not measured; aifoundry3, W, the first kind minus the second; P is M10 frozen, P + ACC is M10 with
M10-ACC's frozen term, E is E178's measured 2.7812 fJ a flip):

| Train | Pair | P | P + ACC | E |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| T1 | int8 5s − 4s (his pair) | +0.1862 | +0.1844 | +0.0701 |
| T2 | int8 (A 1, B −1) − (A −1, B 1): A and B swap 1 against 8 bits a byte | +0.1099 | +0.1099 | +0.0383 |
| T3 | int8 −1s − 1s: both sides go from 1 to 8 bits a byte | +0.1099 | +0.1099 | +0.0383 |
| T4 | int8 (A 4, B 5) − (A 5, B 4): one bit a byte swapped | +0.0157 | +0.0157 | +0.0055 |
| T5 | fp32 (A 1.0, B 0x3FFFFFFF) − (A 0x3FFFFFFF, B 1.0): 23 held bits swapped | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| T6 | fp32 5.0s − 4.0s | +0.0099 | +0.0104 | +0.0017 |

From T3 = 7 (β_A + β_B) and T2 = 7 (β_B − β_A), **every forecast says B-ONLY**: β_A 0 and β_B +0.01570 W a held bit a byte (P and
P + ACC) or +0.00547 W (E). **A train's half-width is 0.0051 W** at the registered t statistic (int8 0.00513 over 32 units, fp32 0.00510),
**0.0102 W** with E178's noise doubled (the registration's dated note 1; never ±0.0049, the z value). The split is read right as B-ONLY
with power 0.95 at base noise and 0.92 with it doubled, A+B 1.00 and 1.00, A-ONLY 0.95 and 0.93, NEITHER 0.90 and 0.86. **E182-W-int8's
verdict is its SIZE word against P (size_P)**; the scorer's `verdict` field carries size_E, arm E's reading, and every record quotes
size_P. E182-R-int8's line 0.4421 has size 0.026, not 0.025. Blind when run: T2 and T5 for every forecast; T1, T4 and T6 for P only; T3
for none; E182-W-int8 against E for 14 of its 20 kinds (dated note 1, section 2). **Planned:** FULL, the stop-path test, the smoke, the
trains T4, T5, T3, T6, T2 and T1, then kinds passes 18220–18222 (18223 if it fits): about 83.4–93.0 card-minutes, cap 100, the day's
post "for 2 h"; if the train host's first build fails, the registered fallback PASSES, about 31, cap 40, "for 1 h". E171's stops; every
device command a read (the excepted statistics-reset site in `lib.sh` is never passed); its october-forecast-model section is empty.

## E184 — the board meter read against steps of known size: a staircase of fadd.ps minions switched from zeros to random operands on aifoundry3, read A B B A inside one process, and card 1's SRAM-rail statistic in reset windows (Q153; registered, amended by A1 and A2, kitted with kit v2; aifoundry3 card 0 at 600 MHz pinned, and aifoundry1's card 1) — not run *(dated, Thu 12 Nov 2026, Q165's records: as first written; its arm B ran on 8 November, blind, and its arm S was not run, stopped by its registered smoke gate: "Arm B run on 8 November, blind; arm S not run", below)*

**Asked (Q150):** *"Are there more accurate ways of checking power draw? Maybe there are multiple sensors. I think there are per-shire
power draw sensors. Also, allow the possibility that maybe there's something wrong with the sensors."* Q150 answered it post hoc and
drafted a sensor-validation run; E184 is that run, registered. **Arm S**, aifoundry3 card 0 at 600 MHz pinned (TDP 0 W, nothing set):
the energy catalogue's `fadd.ps` on both harts of every minion of the active shires, on a zeros base; a step switches n minions to random
[0.5, 2) operands through a host-only change (`--schedule` and `--stop-file`; the patched `host/main.cpp` pinned by its sha256 and built
on the box from a copy; the kernel unchanged), so the rate, the clocks and the issue never change. One quad is one host process under
`timeout 10`: the settle, then A B B A or B A A B. Part L, the ladder at 32 shires: the null step n = 0 and n = 3–121, visited up, down
and up (324 quads); part Cal (2); part P, at E178's int8 operating point (30.132 W; 240 quads); part C, a scan across the board current
reading's code 1024 (32.78–34.46 W by the public PMIC conversion; 25 levels against n = 144; 300 quads): 870 quads in eleven passes. **Arm
B**, aifoundry1's card 1 at 600 MHz, never card 0: the SRAM-rail statistic in 1 s reset windows (B1, with the statistics reset in pass
18441 only), the SRAM rail's step response (B2), a cross-card set of 16 configurations read against September's aifoundry3 rows (B3),
and a glitch census (B4). **Registered** [`c6417611`](../../tools/claims-v3/e184/E184.md) (11:27:45 PST Sat 7 Nov, committed alone
before any kit file; 12 files, `SHA256SUMS-e184-registration` 11; the 467 operand images by their hashes). **Kitted** with dated
amendment [A1](../../tools/claims-v3/e184/E184.md.dated-note-A1.md), [`65f844be`](../../tools/claims-v3/e184/KIT.md) (12:23:35; 45
files, `SHA256SUMS-e184-kit` 44): E184-T read with E58's filter model, which reproduces E58 on its own data (the registered reader read
E58's card-1 SRAM bursts at 0.625 s against 0.525 s); the scorer's self-test 14 of 14, two readers among it checked on committed data;
the host change's desk test on a mock runtime; the desk machine's dry pass. **Amended** by
[A2](../../tools/claims-v3/e184/E184.md.dated-note-A2.md) `a887fc74` (14:04:38, committed alone; 7 files, `SHA256SUMS-e184-a2` 6) and
**kitted again** by [kit note 1](../../tools/claims-v3/e184/KIT.md.dated-note-1.md) with kit v2, `eab0c70e` (14:11:30; 46 files,
`SHA256SUMS-e184-kit-note1` 45), after a verifier that held the registration and the kit with fixes (4 major, 9 minor). A2: E184-C read
with an intercept, the scan's reference level set aside as its own null (A2-1, t* 3.004: one missing code at the reference reads SEEN in
0.04–0.07 of sessions, against 0.52 under the registered statistic); the registered R governs E184-L and E184-P unless the part's own null
reads OFFSET (A2-2); the readings are the board's response to the minion count, a LOW or HIGH names the meter only when the rail's own R
reads LINEAR, and METER EXCLUDED is bounded to a static compression of `fadd.ps` steps (A2-3); the scorer's gates registered (A2-4); a
refresh more than 0.5 W from its block's median set aside (A2-5); A1's reader off on one of E58's 12 card-1 bursts (A2-6); the smoke's
16-shire band a gross check, so it no longer censors E184-P (A2-7); three records corrected (A2-8). Kit v2: the pass and session gates
before the scorer, every hold process gated, a partial pass going on, and the runbook ported onto k6's drivers with every session file
named by its start time and an exact copy-back. Every forecast stands. Checks: the scorer's self-test v2 17 of 17, the runbook's desk
test 27 of 27, the dry pass v2 38 of 38, `dmcmd_audit` GO for each arm. **Not run; 0 card-minutes.**

**The frozen forecasts** (registered, not measured; E184.md, section 0, with A2):

| Rule | What is read | Forecast | Power, base noise / twice it |
|---|---|---|---|
| E184-L | the board's reading of small known steps (3–19 minions, +0.025 to +0.157 W) over large ones (30–121 minions, +0.25 to +1.0 W), at a 35.5 W base | LINEAR: the slope ratio inside 1 ± 0.10 | LINEAR 0.99 / 0.08 as registered, 0.953 at base noise under A2-2; a 20% shortfall reads LOW 1.00 / 0.98 |
| E184-P | the same at E178's int8 operating point (30.13 W) | LINEAR inside 1 ± 0.12, and METER EXCLUDED: the board cannot read a small step at under half its size | LINEAR 1.00 / 0.34; EXCLUDED 1.00 / 1.00 (0.00 if the meter read E178's 0.29) |
| E184-C | a step where the 12-bit current reading crosses code 1024 (32.8–34.5 W) | NONE SEEN | a one-code step SEEN 0.93–0.99 at base noise under A2-1 (0.933 at the 12.0 V carry) |
| E184-N and E184-J | the null step; a jump between ladder levels | NULL within ±0.020 W; NONE SEEN | NULL 0.95 / 0.02; a 0.05 W jump SEEN 0.79 / 0.20 |
| E184-M | card 1's SRAM-rail minimum and maximum over its average in 1 s reset windows, at idle and nine loads | the two-phase reading SUPPORTED: min/avg about 0.63 and max/avg about 1.59 at every load | SUPPORTED 1.00 if the ratios are constant |
| E184-T | the SRAM rail's step response | tau inside 0.44–0.64 s, rise and fall | |

**The phase-shedding question** has a plain answer before any card: **no stock READ returns the regulators' phase-shedding setting** (the
device-management table at et-platform `836a4ab60`; the service processor's PMIC register map, 0x01–0x33, has no phase register; the raw
PMIC read is reached by no device-management command; the public PMIC firmware sets its phase mode at start-up and never reads or sets
shedding), so the null steps, the zeros base inside every quad and the ladder's up-and-down visits guard against it; never that the
setting was read. **Planned:** arm S about 125 card-minutes (870 quads at about 8.3 s), cap 135, the day's post "for 2.5 h"; arm B about
18, cap 25, "for 1 h". Device commands: arm S `dmcmd_audit` GO on 32 sites, every one a read; arm B GO on 34, the statistics reset in pass
18441 its only write (the owner's ruling of 31 October 2026); the runbook GO 23. The stops: aifoundry3's board at or above 65 W, or its
minshire 0 at or above 70 °C, on a live sample; card 1's board at or above 66 W, or any minshire at or above 72 °C. **The readiness
critic: READY WITH GAPS**, on the owner's go and fresh lab approvals for aifoundry3 and aifoundry1. (a) A2 and kit note 1 (53 files) had
no adversarial verifier. (b) Both arms' patched hosts are first compiles on their boxes; a failed build skips that arm for the day. (c)
Arm B runs only if aifoundry1 has not rebooted since E82's firmware read (31 October 2026, 13:45:24 PDT): the look and the launch guard
stop it otherwise (IDENTITY STOP), with no firmware query. Stated limits: A2-1 costs E184-C power in the middle of the carry band; a LOW or
HIGH will most likely read "not separated"; METER EXCLUDED excludes a static compression only; B3 is read against September's rows. **A
verifier of A2 and kit note 1 is under way at these records (Q154).** **Corrections, quoted as corrected** (A2-8 and its fix pass): PREREG
`7d4fabad` is a sha256, and its commit is `5538de2a`; E184.md's inventory was read at 11:17 PST, not "about 11:30"; Q150's draft
registration moves from Used to Checked in its Builds on block; the line-3 comment of `lib.sh` on aifoundry3's tree is stale; "E178's int8
pair is the card's answer" holds only against a static compression (A2-3). *(dated, Sat 7 Nov 2026, after Q153's records' verifier: three more of kit note 1's records, its section 7, items (d), (f) and (g), which these records left out, quoted: (d) "`score_e184.py`'s docstring gives "1.8 and 0.06 W" for B2's minion and NoC steps; A1-2 and the catalogue's card-1 row give **1.86 and 0.12 W**. The code uses neither."; (f) "The self-test's E184-G check allowed three binomial sd (+-0.107 percentage points at 6,886 samples). The generator itself wrote **12 glitches in 6,886 samples** (0.174%, a high draw from its 0.087%): self-test v2's B1 counts them again from the raw files and finds the reader's count equal. The reader is right; the check was weak."; (g) "`logs/desktest-host-e184.txt` (H5) and `logs/desktest-runner-e184.txt` (U3) carry launch counts that depend on timing (launches in a fixed time on a mock), so they do not re-make byte for byte; both pass. The same holds for v2's runner log.")*

10:40–13:00 PST; desk only: no card, no device command, no lab box; the traces ran on a desk Mac; new files only). **What it is:**

lab's committed accumulator arithmetic (`rtl-sim/fma_toggle_v2/accum.c`, unchanged) through every op of a launch of the kit's own size,
and, for floats, a price per partial-product row of A beyond A = 1.0's (the open RTL recodes A) times each toggled significand bit of B.

11:02:22, and before any trace); amended by [A1](../../tools/claims-v3/m10-acc/REGISTRATION.md.dated-note-A1.md) `ceb643ca` (11:20:21: a

verifier held it with fixes (2 minor, 3 nits), answered by
[dated note 1](../reports/data/2026-11-07-q153-m10acc/README.md.dated-note-1.md) `c0c1f39a` (12:57:55), no number, rule or verdict
changed. **Held out, leave one run out over 1,149 committed rows in 39 runs** (M10's 959; pool 8's 40 kinds on E171 and E172; five
single-bit lists' 70 steps; E178's 40 kinds): floats 0.1873 → 0.1886 W rms (d +0.0013 W [−0.0004, +0.0036]), int8 0.1729 → 0.1748 W
(d +0.0018 W [+0.0008, +0.0029]); R1–R3 fail in both families *(dated, Sat 7 Nov 2026, after Q153's records' verifier: floats fail R1–R3, and int8 fails R1 and R2, its whole rule; R3, the S class no worse, is the floats' rule alone)*, so **the term does not enter M10**, nor does A1's (floats d +0.0008 W);

[−3.31, +3.55] and τ_bth +0.065 fJ [−0.14, +0.26]; int8 τ_acc −0.084 fJ [−1.00, +0.83]. **Post hoc** (pool 8 and E178 were read before

pair reads P + term +0.188 W against +0.053 W measured. Why, post hoc: the single-bit lists' measured steps follow the step of the
register's toggles at r −0.03, the lane's accumulator words at r +0.89, and M10's own bench step at r +0.99, so M10 already prices that
path. **Lines for the records, quoted from its dated note 1:** *"F-P9, read in P8-M1's form (no resolution floor): floats BETTER on card 1

price in with chance about 0.6 at +5.7 fJ a toggle, and 0.26 at the registered interval's upper end (+3.55 fJ); 'does not enter' bounds
the price, it does not set it to 0. R3 makes the floats' rule blind to a Booth price."* Never "P9-ACC is forecast NOT RESOLVED", and never

## E188 — what E182's fp32 swap is made of: A's 23 held mantissa bits against 1.0, one held B mantissa bit at five places, and two held B bits adjacent against spaced, read as A B B A trains with a same-session kinds pass (Q165; registered, amended by A1, kitted with kit note 1; aifoundry3 card 0 at 600 MHz pinned) — not run

**Registered** [`878b6f63`](../../tools/claims-v3/e188/E188.md) *(dated, Thu 12 Nov 2026, Q165's records)* (12:53:42 PST, committed alone, 21 files under `tools/claims-v3/e188/`, before any kit file and before any datum): seven fp32 A B B A trains on aifoundry3 card 0 at 600 MHz pinned, nothing set: **AALL**, A holding all 23 mantissa bits against 1.0 × 1.0 (one half of E182's swap T5); **B04, B05, B10, B19 and B22**, B holding one mantissa bit (bit 4, 5, 10, 19 or 22) against 1.0 × 1.0; and **PAIR**, B's two held bits adjacent against spaced; with two same-session kinds passes. M12 (as frozen) and M10 (frozen) are the blind arms: their lines, computed from the committed models with no refit, are in [`forecast-e188.json`](../../tools/claims-v3/e188/forecast-e188.json); the rules, the plan and the power are E188.md sections 5–7. A train is 76 fp32 processes by E182's sizing rule, 578.8 s or 9.6 card-minutes at E182's registered piece times. **Kitted** [`726a6b64`](../../tools/claims-v3/e188/KIT.md) (14:34:31 PST, 63 files) in E186's form, with E183's kit note 4's lessons: the train change is read from the built host's behaviour, and the rest rule reads every session-log form of ours. A1-2, a stop in the smoke's train processes is a failed smoke train gate, so no train runs that day and the fallback runs under the live stops; note 1 sets a passage of section 2 aside as description, restates a train's cost from public sources, and states two rules' FAIL word as "not PASS"), and [the kit's dated note 1](../../tools/claims-v3/e188/KIT.md.dated-note-1.md) (`16b41cf2`, 16:24:28 PST, 12 files: a rest rule v2 that counts a session cut mid-queue as ended at its run's latest write, carried by step 9 v2 and step 2 v2). **Its plan:** FULL about 82.4 card-minutes at E182's registered piece times (91.3–92.8 at E186's measured block times), cap 100; **Ledger v2, the joined load model revived, beside the register** *(dated, Thu 12 Nov 2026, Q167's records)* (Q166, on the owner's message of about 15:15 PST, 02; desk only: no card, no lab box, no device command; new files only). **What it is:** the joined load model of 26 October (Q126(c), [its README](../reports/data/2026-10-26-load-model/README.md)), retired on 5 November with a dated note that says what would revive it, rebuilt by that note's recipe as [`tools/ledger/`](../../tools/ledger/README.md): M10's kernel term (E78's and E82's zeros anchors and M10's held-out price of the data), the energy manual's instruction, memory and mesh prices with their zeros and random columns, the footing law for the card at rest, and three terms the first model lacked (sparse parity's second hart generating rows, each output tile's epilogue and set-up, and the time a running hart waits on another), every term with a band. Each load is scored twice, given its measured seconds (prices and counts) and with its seconds forecast (the whole ledger), each over idle and with the card at rest. **Frozen** [`06d60fa2`](../reports/data/2026-11-12-q166-ledger-v2/FREEZE.md) (`frozen-v2.json` sha256 `59ff9117…`, `predictions-v2.json` `1bce023f…`; made in a scratch clone at 15:52:51 PST, before any scorer existed; landed 20:09:27), **then scored** [`80eb3bdf`](../reports/data/2026-11-12-q166-ledger-v2/README.md) (the corrected scores, made at 19:22:27 PST after the ledger's verifier; landed 20:10:00). **Held out, not blind, throughout:** the three loads were measured in September, before the freeze. **The scores, v2 beside the first model's:** E39, the gemv sparsity ladder (three slopes and three intercepts on three cards), **0.69 W rms** (1.10); E37, mmbench fp32 from DRAM, **1.17 W rms** over idle (2.14), **post hoc for the awake rule** (a hart's time priced once, set during the build after arithmetic beside E101's DRAM arm, the same benchmark): +13–14% an op with it, +27–28% an op and 2.40 W rms without it; E59, sparse parity (four runs on aifoundry3), **0.75 W rms** over idle (3.02, which read 13–39% low), its joules a solve over idle **−1.7 / −2.5 / −3.3 / −2.9% given its seconds, as frozen**, −4.9 / −2.5 / −4.6 / −4.5% with them forecast, and with the card at rest −0.7 to −2.1% given and −1.8 to −14.4% forecast (L1's cycle model is 16.6% short). **E59's two errors that cancel** (Q166's critic): its op cycles are priced twice, about 447 cycles an op inside both the tensor price and the scratchpad-load price, 3.3–4.9% of each forecast (priced once, −5.9 / −7.2 / −7.8 / −6.1% given its seconds; post hoc), and hart 1's row generation is priced at an assumed 0.37 instructions a cycle where the counter counts about 0.2; with both removed sparse parity reads about 6–13% low given its seconds (post hoc desk arithmetic, rough), still far closer than the first model. The data README's title and the chart's title lead with the frozen, double-counted figures (1.7–3.3%; the chart's title as corrected, "Sparse parity's joules over idle land within 5%; the DRAM stream reads 13–14% high"); priced once, the op cycles leave it 6–8% low (post hoc). **Beside them, on one chart** ([`chart-v2.html`](../reports/data/2026-11-12-q166-ledger-v2/chart-v2.html)): E101's 7 loop cells **3.2% rms**, held out by price (its DRAM arm +18.6 / +5.7%, post hoc for the awake rule, +36.0 / +16.4% without it; its 7 L2-streamed cells 3.4% rms, in sample); E136's random minus zeros at 800 MHz, fp32 / fp16 / int8, −3.3 / +1.7 / −11.5% as frozen and **−2.6 / +2.4 / −9.6% corrected post hoc** (an L3 row that does not cancel removed); E86's 12 kinds 0.18 W rms and E95's 33 kinds 0.14 W rms, M10 held out by kind. **Corrected by a dated note** ([`FREEZE.md.dated-note-1.md`](../reports/data/2026-11-12-q166-ledger-v2/FREEZE.md.dated-note-1.md)): the freeze's sentence "no constant was fitted to any of the seven loads" is false; E101 is in sample for the set-bit weight and the L2 stream price, and M10 was fitted on E86's and E95's rows. **Report:** the kinds page (A25), section 11, "How well power and heat can be predicted now".

**The counter, beside the register** *(dated, Thu 12 Nov 2026, Q167's records)* (Q166; builds and simulator runs on aifoundry3's and aifoundry2's host CPUs only: no card, device node, card lock or device command; new files only). **What it is:** [`tools/counter/`](../../tools/counter/README.md) (`69e85905`, 20:12:05 PST): a profiling build of the cards' installed simulator (et-platform `353f20e`; reproducible, two builds byte-identical and matched on a second box; sys_emu sha256 `ce4469ea…`) and a parser, `count_kernel.py`, that turns a kernel file's simulator run into the ledger's counts: every retired instruction by the energy manual's classes, tensor ops by format, bytes by address region, branch directions and CSR names, with the firmware's work outside the kernel reported apart. **Desk checks of code** ([its data](../reports/data/2026-11-12-q166-counter-sparseparity/README.md)): on sparse parity (the 29 September card kernel, byte for byte) it reproduces every count the kernel reports about itself, exactly, for 12 minions in 9 runs; the profiling simulator emulates exactly what the installed one does, address by address; the parser's desk test passes 40 of 40, the simulator's tensor-store lines among them (their first parsing crashed; fixed before the land, after which E101's GEMM kernel counted 64 tensor stores, 65,536 B, its result exact). **Post hoc, an estimate:** on the card's L1 solve the counted work fills 0.193–0.199 of the median minion's measured cycles, at about 0.33, 0.50 and 0.2 instructions a fitted cycle (hart 0's row tile, the epilogue, hart 1's row generation); each int8 op loads 2,048 B from the shire's own scratchpad (a simulator count, where the joined model assumed 4,096). **What it cannot count yet:** bytes by cache level (the simulator has no cache model; reading the card's shire-cache and DRAM counters is a desk recipe never run on a card, and its 64 B a count is an assumption), mesh hops, and the card's own spin loops (they follow the simulator's timing); and counted work alone gives no seconds, which still come from the card's cycle counter or a model. **Report:** the kinds page (A25), section 11.

**The blind day's four programs, beside the register** *(dated, Thu 12 Nov 2026, Q167's records)* (Q166; built with the cards' toolchain and run only in the simulator, with `--sysemu`, `--dry` and `--selftest`, on aifoundry3's host CPU; not run on a card; nothing frozen or registered). [`workloads/ledger/`](../../workloads/ledger/README.md) (`46a16bd0`, 20:14:23 PST): one user-mode kernel and its host with three plain programs, a copy through DRAM (1 GiB, 14 passes, of zeros or random bytes), SHA-256 of 2,048 separate messages (one a hart) and a merge sort of 512 keys a hart on sorted and on shuffled input; and a sparse-parity instance that no earlier committed file names, on the 29 September card kernel (its parameters are in [its data folder](../reports/data/2026-11-12-q166-progs/README.md) only). **Desk checks of code:** every simulator check passed (13 counted cases on one shire; 4 functional runs at the card's 2,048 participants), the digests match Python's `hashlib` 2,048 of 2,048, and the counter resolved every run; the 1 GiB copy is unsimulated at 2,048 participants (its run was stopped at 5 min 18 s, past 10 GB of memory). **Estimates, not frozen, not blind** (desk arithmetic: the counts scaled to the card's configuration and priced by the ledger; one launch on aifoundry3 at 600 MHz): the copy 5.14 J over idle on zeros and 6.81 J on random bytes (0.37 and 0.49 J a GiB); SHA-256 2.30 J, about 44 nJ a 64 B block; the sort 2.26 J sorted and 2.74 J shuffled; the sparse-parity instance 7.35 J; the card at rest and its rise are 64–87% of every total. Their seconds bands are one-sided; the blind day's are to be two-sided. **Report:** the kinds page (A25), section 11.

**E189, the blind day: proposed, not registered yet** *(dated, Thu 12 Nov 2026, Q167's records; the number assigned)*: the checkpoint's step 4 as Q166's critic designed it: the four programs (six configurations: the copy on zeros and on random bytes, SHA-256, the sort sorted and shuffled, and the never-run sparse-parity instance) on aifoundry3 card 0 at 600 MHz as found, reads only, their forecasts frozen in this repository before the card is claimed (ledger v2 as frozen, with `workloads/ledger/ledger_estimate.py` pinned; two-sided seconds bands; two registered arms beside the primary: v2.1, sparse parity's op cycles priced once, and v2c, its hart work from the counter's counts), scored twice, given the measured seconds (a target within 10%) and with the seconds forecast (within 15%); about 95 card-minutes (cap 110). Its registration and kit are written beside these records (Q167) and committed after them; then a verifier, a fix pass, a readiness check and the owner's go. Nothing of E189 is registered or run at this commit, and no E189 datum is in any ref.

**Lane lessons from E78–E83, recorded for the next registration.** (1) A heat column read from a 2 s window over a
whole-degree die reading resolves a kind only above about 12 W of switching on these cards: register a longer window,
or a launch away from a step, before quoting heat for low-power kinds. (2) All six card lanes looked at
partial data while their card ran (E78 at 13:48, E81 at 14:55, E83 at 14:06 and 14:26; E79 at pass 7910, written up as
A4 during pass 7912; E80 at the burst block, A1 during the sustained block; E82 at pass 8210, A2); each is disclosed
and none changed a frozen prediction or rule, but three led to amendments that added items (E79), a labelled post-hoc
view (E80) and a pass with a dip-filtered view (E82), so a registration should say whether an interim look is
allowed. (3) Open no device — not even `ettelem --help` — before the claim or outside `flock -n` (E79's D2).
(4) Every registered leakage arm moved some cell by more than its interval: publish each cell at the registered arms.
(5) A sustained-clock prediction needs the die line measured the same day (for E83, E80's session that afternoon):
E80, scored on 23 October's line,
failed on a drift of about 3 W, while E83, scored an hour later on E80's same-day relation, passed. (6) Single low
board-meter samples, often at a launch boundary, set two passes aside on aifoundry1's card 1 (E82) and failed E81's P5
by 0.0007 W on aifoundry3: register a dip filter (E82 A2's definition) before the data.

**Lane lessons from E84–E91, recorded for the next registration.** (7) A card can carry a limit no rule names: card 0's
PMIC over-power alarm held fp32 random data at 300 MHz for a third of its sustained work (E84), and the registered labels
had no category for it — on a card whose PMIC differs, register a label and a stop for it. (8) Every ratio names its
baseline: int8's real-tile saving is 11–22% over int8's zeros, 8–15% over idle and 2–4% of the whole board (E86, E91).
(9) Score a predictor beside a no-model baseline: on real fp32 and fp16 data "real = random" scored as well as the frozen
points on the clock rules (E87) and nearly as well on power in fp16 (E86). (10) The unit of replication is the
independent pass, not a lap or a burst inside one: E89's lap-unit verdicts were restated with the pass as the unit (23
of 24 pass, 1 inconclusive). (11) Register a spare pass inside the budget and the budget in seconds, and implement it as
an exact check, never a whole-minute floor: E91 lost its registered minimum by 1 s. (12) A short session's automatic
leakage slope can decide its gates (E91: 0.422 W/°C against 0.20–0.24 elsewhere): register the slope, or a check on it.
(13) The sampler's `--reset-ms` sends the PMIC a statistics reset (E85 C1). The owner's ruling of 31 October 2026 allows
it: record it in the run's sampler arguments, and leave it out of a run that tests the governor's power loop, because it
restarts the PMIC's running average (E41).
(14) A version string does not say what a management command returns: on aifoundry3, command 74 group 0 returned whole
degrees on 7 Oct and raw 12-bit codes on 31 Oct (E89 A1), so probe a command read-only before the passes rely on it.

**Lane lessons from E92–E104, recorded for the next registration.** (15) A desk emulation is not a read-back: E88's and
E92's fp16 "frozen" designs were proved by an emulation that rounds to nearest, and the card truncates each aligned
product. Read C back on a designed tile (`--dump-c`, E104) before registering a state of C, and name the readings a read
must separate. (16) A selection that picks tiles where two models disagree forces the worse model's error: E103's R2 and
R3 follow from R1. Compute the floor it implies from the frozen numbers, and score a blind set nobody chose beside it
(E95). (17) Before registering a rule, say what it can detect: at n = 2 the reference alone set ±15.3% on E97's R2, and
a cap bounded E96's I1 at +10.6 MHz, so `DIFFERENT_BEYOND_BAR` was out of reach. (18) A test of steadiness needs an
inlet read beside it and no host compute on any box (E97's plateau moved three times, cause not established). (19) Name
a kernel for what it is: E101's "a real GEMM" was one port of a vendor's pattern at one miss rate, and its layout set
the rate; a desk cache model before the first pass would have said so. (20) A hot start does not hold the clock down
(E101's 97 W stop, 14 runs lost): start a cell above the limit from a registered rising edge, or budget its stop. (21) A
login ends a session (E98's P2): name what the rule leaves behind, register a spare, and let the queue resume with the
recorded runs skipped (E101's A5). (22) An amendment inherits the gates: E102's R blocks had no settling read after
their idle gap, so its own gate set three of four aside, and R2a is NOT SCORED. (23) A shared claim for a night's slots
does not pre-register what comes after it: E100's and E96's rules were pushed after the claim and before their first
launch, which meets the launch and not the claim. (24) A burst of host CPU beside another lane's card is the
coordinator's to prevent: E104's desk compute on aifoundry1's CPU, its own cards idle, fell inside E97's hold on
aifoundry3. (25) Mask a lab user's login before reduction, and read the committed tree back with compressed files
decompressed (E98).
(26) A rule that admits cells by estimate must be tested on the cell it exists to catch: E105's rule, on a lower kind's
rho, admitted random fp32 at 87.83 W, and it peaked at 96.25 W. Estimate from the same kind's S on a pinned card, and
lock by layout and format. (27) A registered branch that says a result "would make it a bracket" binds the headline when
it fires: E106's R5 read LITTLE, so fp16's held part is 0.19–0.45, not "about half". (28) A proxy for a state is a twin,
not the state: `z_ss` and `x_s` hold, and neither bounds `signs`; say what each lacks. (29) A claim post may not outlast
its run: tonight's went from the channel, so a lane's release has to be read back at once, and its times belong in the
lane's own log.

**Lane lessons from E107–E118, recorded for the next registration.** (30) A pass gate can void a registered minimum: E109
kept two of three passes and is NOT SCORED, E112(b) the same, and E115's primary kept one, so only its registered fallback
scored. Register 4–5 passes inside the budget, or how a set-aside pass is handled, and size a queue's deadline from the
slowest pass seen on that card (E109's 23 minutes admitted at most its minimum). (31) On aifoundry3 the random-range gate
sets passes aside on board-meter dips (two of three in E115; one each in E86, E92 and E95): register a dips-aware gate, or
the dips-removed arm as the primary, as on aifoundry1's card 1. (32) A choice made after a look is not a registered score:
Q133(b)'s model search was widened after its first fit's misses were printed, and Q133(a)'s primary netlist was chosen when
the arms were built, not registered. Freeze the search and the primary before the counts. (33) A price on a desk count is
post hoc: E107's prices per missing line rest on a desk L2 model's line counts, E109's prices per clock edge on the RTL
bench's register count. Give the count's source beside the price, and the mean minion's figure beside the launch's pace.
(34) A firmware-revisions query is a PMIC command (E114 C1): take a card's identity by uptime continuity, as E118 and E77 did.
(35) The shared ring decoder `tools/claims-v3/dv2/sptrace_events.py` stops at the first binary power-status record: E118's G3
read one throttle-up where the ring held two ([`posthoc_g3_ring.txt`](../reports/data/2026-11-01-e118-two-clock-card0/posthoc_g3_ring.txt)).
Read ring events after an operating-point change with `tools/claims-v3/hpl/sptrace_full.py` (E64 A1), by the delta between
dumps; counts published with the old reader were not re-read. (36) Simulations stay off the card hosts, even with the card idle (E107's C8): build
the simulator on the Intel. (37) A same-session control needs about five passes to stand in for a reference row (E118's C1
kept two of three). (38) Lesson (12) held again: E112 registered the automatic leakage slope by re-running E91 unchanged, and
card 1's 0.414 W/°C decided its R5. (39) Every RTL-side count names its merge: at gate level `nets_map` and `nets_hier`
agree on random data, and at RTL the floats' count reads 1.87× low by one and 2.37× by the other (Q133(a)).

**Lane lessons from E119–E125, Q134(a) and E77, recorded for the next registration.** (40) Dry-run every query against the
exact binary the session will call: E119 asked `ettelem-hpl` for raw residency states 1–3, which that build refuses (it takes
2–6) at its argument check before it opens the device, so 68 of 68 queries returned nothing; E120 dropped its residency read
by amendment for the same reason. (41) On BL2 0.22.0 ask for raw residency states 2 and 3 only: raw 4–6 are no state there,
and each such read logs two SP errors that the host counts as `SP Runtime Error` events (E77 F1; Q134(a) traces 4,228 of
aifoundry2's 4,234 and all 378 of card 0's to them); a POWER_UP read (raw 1) needs a new build. (42) An admission estimate of
a walk's burst median is not a ceiling for a sustained run near the trip (E119 C1): keep the hard lock and the stop as the
guards, or register a hot-die margin. (43) Register the dips-removed frame as the primary on aifoundry3 and aifoundry1's card
1: E124 kept four of four passes and E125 seven of eight where E109 and E113 had each lost a pass, and by the same filter
those two set-asides were board-meter dips. (44) A session can shift every level over idle and leave its differences alone
(E124's S 0.10–0.28 W below E82's and E93's on the same card, D unmoved): compare sessions on differences, and give the
offset beside any S. (45) A pooled SD can carry a verdict a row's own SD does not (E123's fp16 50% zeros, Bartlett p 0.011):
test the pooling before quoting the verdict, and print both readings. (46) Judge a redesign against what it measured as well
as against the figure it set out to bound: E120's bound sits below a row that is itself an uncorrected fit (CV1). (47) A
governor move's live ring check must count only entries newer than the previous dump: E77's counted every match in the dump,
stale ones included (E120 counted fresh entries only). (48) Several lanes rewriting the day's one card post need
`EXPECT_TEXT` on every edit: the post's read-back plan came back stale for E119, E121 and E122, and only the check's exit 7
kept a lane from erasing another's clause; rebuild the text from the edit box. (49) A launch that fails before it opens a
device still spends the budget: E124's apostrophe cost 2 min 37 s, which under the frozen claim anchor would have cost its
fourth pass (its A1); dry-run every script a session calls, not only the block's. (50) A residency counter can answer what
sampling cannot: Q134(a) read aifoundry2's POWER_SAFE time from its service processor's own counter, with card 0's alarm
episodes as an exact positive control, and found no over-power interrupt in six days; read the counters the firmware keeps
before planning a run to provoke an event.

*Dated note, Mon 2 Nov 2026 (Q136's records), on lesson (46): the row was not an uncorrected fit. Its coefficient was fitted on
board deltas already corrected with the idle law's leakage slope (the rails raw), and E120's "about 0.4 pJ" was its registered
0.81 W/°C drift slope at work; re-cut on each run's own drift the row is 0.75–1.05 pJ as a straight line and stands (Q136(b);
lesson 58).*

**Lane lessons from E126–E134 and Q136's desk lanes, recorded for the next registration.** (51) List and classify every device
command before the first launch, from the source of the build the card reports, and run the checker at your own commit: a
command named GET can write (the firmware-revisions query writes the PMIC's command register twice, `GET_FRU` its FRU
registers), the ruled statistics reset is one form of a command whose other forms (`dev_mngt_service -t SPST:…` and `MMST:…`)
are other writes, and a line another file patches reads as sent unless it is declared (E126's residency line, which E119's
`patch_residency` rewrites) (Q136(w)). (52) Name the build from a fresh read, not a brief: E128 and E129 first classified card
1's commands from BL2 0.18.0 while card 1 reports 1.4.0 / BL2 0.22.0 since 31 October (E82 A1); and name the binary that runs:
aifoundry3's `build/ettelem/ettelem` is built from `de262730`'s source, not the reviewed one (E132 C1, E134 C6). (53) A stop
field named PMIC may not read the PMIC: `temp_c.pmic` is the minion shires' mean (E127 C5), and the PMIC's 75 °C alarm reads its
own temperature, which no telemetry field reads (E128 C6); register a board-power stop with margin under 75 W (E128, E129 and
E132 registered none) and call the die stop a die reading. (54) On aifoundry1's card 1 a pass without a hot anchor runs 2–3×
its estimate: a launch on the cool die needed about three heats in E128 and 5–8 in E129, so E128 kept two passes and E129
three; size a session from the slowest pass seen with the same anchors, or keep E78's hot anchors. (55) A governed sustained
cell is a window of a warm-up: in a second session E126's random clocks ran 37–83 MHz faster from a 3 °C cooler idle and an
earlier place in the session (proxies; no inlet thermometer), while what the clock buys repeated to 0.002; give each sustained
gap its session, its idle and its start temperature, or gate each run on the same start. (56) In a blocked window every probe
goes through a 15 s hard-kill script, every 3 minutes, logged: E130's first probe sat two minutes behind `head -60`'s buffer
before its link was seen, and E133's first lane made 14 probes by hand, unlogged (its README's D4). (57) A dip under the
registered line can still carry a verdict: E134's R2 width and its 13410 set-aside trace to 3–4 W board-meter dips under E82
A2's 4 W line; register a dip rule that catches 2–4 W dips (6 of E134's 408 scored runs hold one). (58) Read a fit's inputs
before calling it uncorrected: the off-rail row's coefficient was fitted on leakage-corrected board deltas, and E120's "about
0.4 pJ" came from its registered 0.81 W/°C drift slope, 2.3× its own block's board drift, not from the window's heating
(Q136(b)). (59) Lesson (48) held again: `EXPECT_TEXT`'s exit 7 refused E126's, E128's (its second session's) and E133's rewrites
when another lane had edited the post meanwhile, and kept each from erasing another lane's clause; E130's plan read came back
behind its own saved edits, though each of its dry runs opened on the expected text; rebuild from the edit box, or wait a minute
after another lane's edit.

**Lane lessons from E135, recorded for the next registration.** (60) A cloud GPU's container may refuse to set the
board's power limit: on Modal `nvidia-smi -pl` answered "Insufficient Permissions" (exit 4), so E135's registered
lower-cap arm could not run. Try the set in the declared smoke, before freezing an arm on it, or rent a host with root
on the GPU. (61) A GPU's power reading is not instantaneous: NVML's updates every 0.1–0.2 s, and a boost can come within
half a second of the first call, so a reading taken before the boost is one to three samples (E135 C10); a relation
fitted on it is post hoc, and its own error at a measured point goes beside each figure it gives elsewhere (E135 C2).
(62) A chip-against-chip figure needs the same kernel on both: E135's first README set the ET-SoC-1's resident loop
against an A100's streaming GEMM and drew three contrasts its verifier withdrew (C1); name each figure's kernel.

**Lane lessons from E136–E140 and Q137(m), recorded for the next registration.** (63) A stop is armed only if its watcher reads
the file the sampler writes live: E139's 65 W watcher followed `telemetry.jsonl`, which exists only after a pass ends, so it read
no sample all session, and its test passed on a file of that name (E139 C1); test a watcher on a live pass's `.raw` file. (64)
Score the old model's frozen predictions beside the new one's: E140's registration carried M5's, its first README did not report
them, and on the pool they beat M6 (0.358 against 0.406 W rms over 25 tiles; E140 C1). (65) A nested leave-one-kind-out score is
not an out-of-sample bound: M6's nested 0.227 W in fp32 read 0.50 W on blind pool 4, one-sided, and its 95% intervals held 19 of 25
tiles; register coverage and bias as rules beside the rms. (66) Lessons (31) and (43) held again on aifoundry3: E78's pass gates set
two of E140's four passes aside on readings the size of board-meter dips, and two of its six power misses were dips on the kept
passes; register the dips-removed arm as the primary there. (67) A speed law priced from benchmark arms carries their C-store
frequency: E119's arms store a C tile every 32 ops, E136's GEMMs every 256 (`g4k`) or 64 (`g2k`), and its frozen time per op was
7–13% slow; give C's stores as a variable before predicting a new kernel (E136 post hoc). (68) Preparing one lane on the host of
another lane's running card disturbs it: E140's no-device setup ran on aifoundry3's host during E138's blocks 13811–13812 (E138
C7); prepare on the Intel, or between sessions.

**Lane lessons from E141–E150 and Q138's desk lanes, recorded for the next registration.** (69) Score a blind pool on the footing
the model was trained on: E141's B1 FAIL HIGH and P2 FAIL followed its session's own leakage fit (0.263 W/°C) against M7's
training rows (0.210–0.222 W/°C, 80 of 247 at a fixed 0.1994, 93 with the dips removed), and E142 and E140 read the same way;
register one leakage slope and one dips rule for training rows and tests alike, or the slope as a model term (E141 C1). (70)
Report a band's width beside its coverage: M7's 28 of 30 against M6's 22 on aifoundry3 came from float bands 1.6 times M6's with
the same errors (E141 C3), and on card 1 M6's narrower bands held 29 of 30 (E142 C1). (71) A rail reading can jump to its running
maximum: card 1's minion rail returned 34.50 W in 15 of 26,469 of E145's samples and none of 31,491 on aifoundry3, and four such
samples left E145's registered interval six times wider than planned; register a spike filter before the data on that card (E145).
(72) A field named for the hottest shire may be a latch: `temp_c.minshire`'s high and low entries are the sensors' latched sample
high and low, which the reading path never clears (65 and 46 °C in all of E145's samples on card 1, 87 °C through E138 on
aifoundry3), so a die stop on "any minion-shire reading" acts on the mean; give the mean beside any maximum, and test a die stop
on a moving reading (E145 C4; E146's die-stop test fired on the latch at its first sample). (73) A stop is armed only under the
keys its watcher reads: E136's protocol named its stops `die_c`, `board_w` and `board_samples`, which `run_hpl.py`'s watcher
ignores, so E136 ran on the watcher's 85 °C and 97 W; E146's runner refuses a protocol whose stops lack the watcher's keys (E146).
(74) A watcher that dates its lines must also refuse a stale one: E142's in-run watcher, copied into E145's and E148's runners,
computes a line's age and never acts on it; stop on a line older than about 1 s (E148 C7). (75) On a governed card score the
governed and the pinned apart, and keep the cap out of the scored bursts: 35 of the 56 bursts M7's aifoundry2 term was scored on
sat at 800 MHz, where the clock is not tested, and its cap was fitted on the same bursts (Q138(m) C1); E143 registered both
groups. (76) A file tile's C is not checked: `sparsity_host --values file:` reports every launch "unchecked" (E80, E143), so
"counted" means completed with no tensor error; say so in the registration, and emulate C on the desk with the card's own rule,
which for fp16's two-term update is E36's plain sum, not PRM 9.4's drop (Q138(b) C1). (77) Name a blind pool's anchors and twins:
pool 5's three zeros anchors are byte-identical to E78's fitted zeros tiles (E141 C2), and E149's held-out 75% sparse kind
re-seeds a fitting kind (E150 C7); count "new kinds" without them. (78) A cloud GPU's label is not its part: Modal's "A10G" served
a 150 W NVIDIA A10 in three of six containers and its "A100-40GB" served 80GB boards; check the GPU's name before the first window
and refuse another part (E150 A3, E149 C1). (79) Freeze before the next phase's data reaches the desk: E150's freeze was pushed
with two of phase H's board-passes already fetched, unreduced; it was a mechanical function of phase L, which a re-derivation
showed, but fetch only the frozen phase, and end the fit's series at that phase's last window (E150 C4). (80) A heat path fed by
watts over zeros counts the fast pole's leakage twice if it adds that leakage again: E149's registered blind path read 3.78 °C,
2.53–2.68 °C without the double count; register the corrected arm beside it (E149 N2, E150 Hc). (81) A whole-degree rest reading
anchors every heat verdict to ±0.5 °C: three of E144b's six PASSes do not hold across it; report the anchor's range, and get a
finer rest die before testing a per-kind coefficient (E144b C1, C2). (82) A frozen clock statement for a governed hold names both
limits: Q138(t)'s "800 MHz until the die reads 66, then the floor" missed that aifoundry2's power limit acts first, 2.3 s in
(E144b C3). (83) A subset's own expected readings go in the registration, before the first launch: E144's note N1 came after the
smoke's first launch and before any hold's data (E144 C6). (84) Quote the owner's words through the public-text scan: the private
archive's host name, which these records withhold, reached the data READMEs and the frozen registrations of E149 and E150 in their
quotes of his ask; the READMEs are reworded, the registrations stand as frozen (these records). (85) A desk batch writes its own
flags into each record: Q138(g)'s check count went wrong because a batch launched from a shell left no record of whether it ran
the settled-value check, and stopping a queue's parent shell orphaned a batch the Intel's load guard then paused (Q138(g) C2, D7).

**Lane lessons from E151–E162 and Q139's desk lanes, recorded for the next registration.** (86) Register the one footing as the
primary: E152 registered its session's own leakage fit (0.308 W/°C) 84 minutes after Q139(u)'s footing was committed, and reads R1
and R2 INCONCLUSIVE where the footing reads LINEAR and CARRIES (E152 C5); E153's own fit reads M8's floats −0.210 W (B1 FAIL HIGH)
where the footing reads −0.014 W. (87) A scorer's minimum must count distinct passes: E153's accepted one pass listed twice and
printed every verdict at 16:40:11, before amendment A1 was committed; such a look is disclosed with its printouts (E153 C1). (88)
Governed by the model is not governed by the card: three of the 17 tiles M8 put below 795 MHz ran at 793–797 MHz; split by the
measured clock too (E160 C6). (89) An interval over tiles inside one session carries no session term: E160's resolved M8 − M7
holds in that session, while the same model's mean governed clock error moved from −4.96 to +2.82 MHz between E143 and E160 (E160
C7). (90) Register what an anchor's idle pass does to the tiles it anchors: fp16's zeros anchor ran its first pass at 752 MHz
under the service processor's mid-burst idle status and put +0.73 W on every fp16 burst D (E160 C1). (91) Define a duty before the
data, and end a scoring window with the work: E161's post hoc work share counted the window's last 6 s and each process's start-up
as idle (0.94), where the hold's kernel duty from its launch records is 0.970 (E161 C1). (92) A pooled curve's pass can rest on
one rung: E158's registered `F6` pass needs `K4n8`, without which the curve misses by +8.0 / +7.9 / +5.9%; report the
leave-one-rung-out reading beside it (E158 C1). (93) A cold start is a term: E159's first hold began at 46 °C where every burst
behind P0 began at 47–56 °C, and about 3 W of its 7.2 W first-second deficit is the start die; gate each hold's start on the die,
or model it (E159 C2). (94) A first silicon read is the earliest check on any card: E160's morning launches read pool 6's new
ranges first, checked at 16:31:18, so E153's and E157's are the first checked reads on their cards (E157 C2). (95) Apply a
critic's rule as written, or say how it was applied: E156 put the 0.05 W line on the frozen term per image, not the literal +0.185
W (E156 C4); E154's per-hold rule asks the plateau and the checkpoints' rms both, which 1 of 4 holds met (E154 C1). (96) A point
estimate is not a bound: E152's "within 3% of full scale" were point departures, with 95% bounds reaching 10.4% (E152 C1). (97) A
price measured at one base word belongs to that word: every per-position int8 B price so far is at E130's B1 with A held at 0x01,
on the same 26 tiles (E151 C2). (98) A dip rule belongs to a card: on aifoundry2 E82 A2's dips are the governor's own low-voltage
phases (98.4% within ±2 samples of a clock and set-point change), and removing them would lift E143's governed tiles by 2.46 W
(Q139(u) C1). (99) Collect and release as soon as a queue ends: E160's detached queue ended at 07:12:39, 34 minutes before the
session stopped at about 07:46 on a usage limit, and its data waited 8 h 44 min on the box until a resumed lane collected it
read-only with the first attempt's collector, every file hashing as registered (E160 D1); its clause stayed in the day's first
post, and that post was later deleted by someone else (E160 D2). (100) Copy a registration to the box after its last edit, or
record the committed copy's hash: E154's box held a 17:49:03 draft of the registration README, its 12 code files byte-identical to
`8c23853c` (E154 C14). (101) A day post's plan text lags its save: `delete_msg.mjs --plan`'s text field still showed the previous
source after each save (E151, E152, E157, E159), so a rewrite built from it meets exit 7 under `EXPECT_TEXT`; rebuild it from the
edit box's text, as E141's lane did.

**Lane lessons from Q140's desk lanes, recorded for the next registration.** (102) Nest the selection, or label the held-out error
as carrying it: Q140(l)'s and Q140(q)'s terms were chosen on the folds that score them; nested inside each fold aifoundry2's bursts
read 5.12–6.90 MHz / 0.89–0.93 W / 0.88–0.92 W against the registered 5.00 / 0.89 / 0.75, and G4 about 1.47 W / 0.63 °C against
1.33 / 0.58 (Q140(l) C4; Q140(q) C1) *(dated, Wed 4 Nov 2026, after Q140(r)'s verifier: G4's 1.47 W / 0.63 °C are with the
selection nested and the span fraction applied, C1 and C2; with the selection nested alone 1.42 W / 0.60 °C)*. (103) Score the
model as frozen: Q140(q)'s held-out scores were G4 without its span fraction,
a term of the frozen form; with it 1.38 W / 0.61 °C (Q140(q) C2). (104) Register exactly one die law, with its reference
temperature: two laws over the same span compose to in-work levels 0.3–1.0 W apart (E161's G1Q −0.08 or +0.24 W), and the
reference die alone moves G1Q's frozen plateau 0.65 °C (Q140(l) C1; Q140(e) C3). (105) Check a blind pool against every committed
tile and every training kind, by bytes and by kind, at the copy: pool 7's first design held a held-B kind that E103 had measured and
M8 had trained on (replaced before the commit), and one class-S tile is byte-identical to a bench tile of M9's lane, a directory the
designer's scan was barred from entering (Q140(b)'s verifier; Q140(x) A1-2). (106) A table's header is not its content: pool 7's
aifoundry2 `.md` heads M9's columns as Q140(l)'s part and prints M9 through M8's relation; score the named JSON block, and test a
printed table against it (Q140(x) A1-1). (107) A bench count is an estimate with an error: on a high single bit the three-point
launch average over-counts by 0.78 ± 0.27 W at M9's prices against 27 points, and the nine-point mean itself carries ±0.51 W, so
count to a stated precision before naming a missing price (Q140(m) C1, C9). (108) Register a threshold's false-positive rate beside
it: G_A's registered +0.15 °C has a 13–35% false-positive rate on committed aifoundry3 holds (Q140(e) C10). (109) A post hoc probe that removes the
tiles where the error is large measures selection, not a bound: dropping each model's own six worst tiles does as well (0.15–0.17
W) as dropping the six named-miss tiles (0.17–0.19 W) (Q140(c), corrected after verification). (110) A frozen hash list covers what
the scorer imports: E168's scorer check hashed 8 of the 16 files on its score path (Q140(q) C11; an 18-file sidecar now pins them).
(111) Stamp a lane's times from git and the reflog, not its notes: Q140(c)'s window, Q140(e)'s registration header, its D6 and its
fix pass's notes, and Q140(x)'s order log each ran past or ahead of their commits (Q140(c)'s fix pass; Q140(e) C1 and `ac06c9be`;
Q140(x) A1-4). (112) Until a blind pool runs, the records describe it by counts and classes, never tile by tile.

**Lane lessons from E171–E177 and the desk lanes of Q142(h2)–Q143, recorded for the next registration.** (113) Check a fixed start-gate
line against the card's idle floor before a session: E176's G0 at 53.0 °C sat under the floor it met (53.20 °C) and is read after a smoke
that leaves about 0.4 °C, so a warm evening could never pass it. (114) A rest rule reads only real sessions: step 11 counted a dry
session's "session end" and lost 40 minutes. (115) Score after the gates, or under a staging name: an unscored session's files must never
sit in a data directory looking scored. (116) Read a proxy's resolution before writing a rule on it: a sensor that reports whole kelvins
cannot show a 0.5 °C cooling. (117) Quote a rule's own count, not its checker's: P8-KC counts 1,900 and 2,550 where the check of C counts
3,470 and 3,490. (118) Name a heat figure's footing: "given the measured switching watts" and "from the bits" are different claims (HC1-B
0.170 → 0.154 °C against 0.191 → 0.166 °C). (119) A gate's file list and the sync's list must agree, tested on a tree built from the sync list
alone (E174's D1). (120) A plan read lags a saved edit by up to about 90 s: take EXPECT_TEXT from the edit box or from a plan read at least
90 s after a save.

**Lane lessons from E178–E181 and the desk lanes of Q145–Q147, recorded for the next registration.** (121) Quote every rule a post hoc term moves, better and worse: the dip term turns three rules from fail to pass and makes all three of E175's worse. (122) Read an event's timing at its sampler's resolution: a climb that ends just past a window edge is seen one 0.1 s sample later. (123) Name the interpreter of a byte-for-byte rebuild: Python 3.12 changed how `sum()` adds floats. (124) Pair figures on one footing: 55.8 pJ at the board is net of the rail's own heating, so its minion-rail partner is 44.4 pJ, never the raw 46.4. (125) Build a box list from every file the runner and its imports read, and test it on a tree built from the sync list alone (E176's `reduce_e89.py`). (126) A switch that waits on a refit waits on a refit frozen for a pool, with its band, not on a post hoc one (FL3). (127) In public text a model kept elsewhere goes by its public name only, its numbers only after its freeze line (october-forecast-model).
(128) A leak correction read on whole-degree die readings can make a per-run order term (E178's int8 couple, +0.066 W): keep the alternating order, and read the footing from a sub-degree sensor when the runner samples one. (129) When a model's figure lies inside a measured interval that also holds 0, say both: the card cannot tell the model from no difference (E180-H). (130) Pin the readers' interpreter: `statistics.stdev`'s last bit and `sum()` differ between Python 3.9 and 3.12 or later (E180's arm, E178's rail reader). (131) Quote a run's lines for the records from its dated notes, never from the README sentence they correct.

**Lane lessons from Q153's registrations (M10-ACC, E182 and E184), recorded for the next registration.** (132) State a rule's power before any data, and let a class rule read only a class the feature exercises: M10-ACC's float rule let a price in with chance 0.26 at its interval's upper end, and its class rule was blind to a Booth price (its dated note 1, N2). (133) Build on a box as a detached job, or under a bound that covers the build, never inside a bounded read: E182's step 6 v2 builds inside a 60 s read, and an overrun rebuilds from scratch without reaching the fallback (E180's and E184's builds are detached jobs). (134) When two kits share a box on one day, write down their order and guard every box step against a live session of ours: E182's step 6 has no such guard. (135) Pin each hash list's count from the file that states it, never from the list's own line count (E182's step 2 v2; E184's step 2 pins 11, 44, 6 and 45). (136) Look before the post in every runbook: a post before the look claims a card that may be held. (137) A desk stand-in for the remote shell pipes the script into `bash -s`, never a seekable file: the desk machine's bash 3.2 re-reads part of a script after a background fork (E182's and E184's desk tests; E180's runbook test keeps the latent case). (138) A forecast's half-width uses the rule's own statistic: E182's trains read ±0.0051 W at t(n − 1), not the z value's ±0.0049.
